They had not reached safety. There were mountains to cross. The only reason he clung to this stubborn life was simple: even a broken life was still needed for now.

If only he could send them to the Northern lands.

If his disciples survived and the Southern Island Sect’s name lived on, he could laugh without regret even as his body was pierced by a thousand spears at the moment of death.

‘Now I understand.’

He looked at the Three Waves emblem engraved on his chest, then planted his feet and struck the ground with force.

“Summon your last strength! Just cross that river now!”

“Yes, Sect leader!”

Hearing his disciples’ tearful replies, Geum Yang-Baek smiled.

‘There are things more important than life.’

And that thing stood by his side now, steadfast and resolute.

Chapter 1408: It’s Time To Wake Up From The Dream. (3)

“Reporting! The Ten Thousand People Clan’s forces escorting Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso’s carriage have crossed Ma’anshan and are nearly at Nanjing!”

“Elder! The Black Ghost Fort that moved from the Hangzhou direction has reached the vicinity of Nanjing!”

“Pirates! A fleet of the Yangtze Pirates has reached near Nanjing, report!”

As urgent reports poured in, the beggars surrounding the map reached out. The markers placed on the map were hastily moved.

Cold sweat ran down the back of the King Crow Neung Sam, who was staring at the map. Black, red, and blue markers were closing in, as if encircling a single point on the map.

Nanjing. The place the Heavenly Friends Alliance, having crossed the river, had taken as their retreat… no, the only place they could use as a retreat.

The place the Heavenly Friends Alliance rushed to in order to rescue Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is a terrain blocked by enormous lakes: Shijiu Lake to the left and Changdang Lake to the right.

To escape from there, they had no option but to head north toward Nanjing.

And right now the Ten Thousand People Clan, Black Ghost Fort, and the Pirates were blocking that sole retreat.

‘Oh no!’

If they really arrived first and sealed off the retreat, the Heavenly Friends Alliance would have no hope. They would never be able to cross the Yangtze.

“Heavenly Friends Alliance! What happened to them? Still no contact?”

“We are doing our best right now, but…”

“Don’t just flap your mouths, do something! Now!”

“B-but the movements of those in the Southern lands are…”

KRAAAASH!

Neung Sam struck the table with the map down with all his force.

“You…!”

The murderous intent he exhaled made the faces of the beggars blanch in an instant.

“If you value your lives so much, why did you crawl into the Beggars’ Union! Are you the disciples of the Righteous Blood Beggars’ Union? If you live on handouts and then hesitate over your lives when needed, don’t you know you’re nothing but lowly beggar scum!”

At those words, the members of the Information Hall dropped their heads.

“Send orders again, at once!”

“Yes, Elder.”

At that moment the door burst open with a bang and a beggar rushed in.

“Elder! Shaolin! A message from the Shaolin Abbott!”

“Beop Jeong?”

A flash of displeasure crossed Neung Sam’s face for a moment. He quickly composed himself and spoke.

“Speak.”

“We must re-check the identities of those gathering at Nanjing! No matter what sacrifices it takes!”

“Goddamn it! Who doesn’t know that? Aren’t we already doing that?”

“N-no, it’s not that! They say we must check whether there are others besides the ones already revealed!”

“…What?”

“They say if the Hegemony Lord predicted this situation, he would surely have hidden one more move. We must re-verify whether the forces we’ve identified are truly their full strength.”

“…One more move?”

Neung Sam’s face went blank for a moment. Then his complexion turned deathly pale. Frozen as if he’d seen a ghost, he hurriedly looked at the map.

“Here… is there something more here?”

Impossible. Absolutely impossible. Hadn’t the Four Sects Alliance already mobilized every force they could?

Except for the Lower District Sect, which is too far to arrive in time, the sects that make up the Four Sects Alliance had all assembled.

But what if that were possible?

If the Four Sects Alliance’s strength was greater than they believed?

Sweat poured down Neung Sam’s face like rain.

Once a disparity in strength appears, it multiplies exponentially.

If equally matched fighters fought one-on-one, both sides would be battered, but if it becomes one-versus-many, the side lacking numbers won’t even be able to properly strike before getting pummeled.

This principle holds in military strategy as well. If the difference in forces exceeds twofold, the stronger side’s losses drop drastically while the weaker side’s losses grow even greater.

But if the forces gathering at Nanjing are larger than they thought? If they exceed the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s strength by several times?

‘It’s all over.’

The Heavenly Friends Alliance would be annihilated without even inflicting significant damage on the Four Sects Alliance. Then only the Nine Great Sects would be left to face the Four Sects Alliance, whose morale would be soaring.

The only reason the Nine Great Sects had managed to keep some margin against the Four Sects Alliance so far was one: the combined strength of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Nine Great Sects was superior to the Four Sects Alliance.

But if the Heavenly Friends Alliance were to utterly collapse, the martial world would be dragged into a terrible chaos no one could predict.

It would quite literally set the world ablaze.

“Impossible… It’s absolutely impossible to pull together that much force suddenly. At least another Nine Great Sects-level force would be needed… where on earth could such people be?”

“Elder!”

Neung Sam, who had been murmuring in a daze, flinched and nodded.

“Tell the beggar bastards waiting across the river to cross it! They must comb the area around Nanjing as if raking it! Check if the enemy has hidden forces!”

“But Elder! If we do that, the beggars will all die.”

“I know, so tell them to cross the river immediately! If they can’t, then we must help them ourselves!”

“B-but Elder, you don’t have the authority to…”

Neung Sam glared at the insubordinate man with murderous eyes.

“Since when have the Beggars’ Union waited for orders to practice righteousness? Since when?”

“…”

“If they’ve laid a trap, the Heavenly Friends Alliance is finished! Not only the Heavenly Friends Alliance, but the entire world will be set alight! Don’t you understand?”

Under those heavy words, the hesitant subordinate finally nodded.

“…Understood.”

“Hurry, now!”

“Yes!”

Watching the beggar rush out, Neung Sam clenched his jaw until the muscles stood out.

‘It is just worry. It’s practically impossible.’

It was impossible to muster that much force in a short time. And if someone had such strength, hiding it so thoroughly until now would be even less plausible. Where in the world would someone have that patience?

Yet there was one reason Neung Sam couldn’t shake the creeping unease.

‘If it’s the Hegemony Lord.’

That single word from Beop Jeong’s message wouldn’t leave his mind.

A thing no one else could do.

But if it was the Hegemony Lord, could he do it?

The man who toyed with the world might have predicted all this and laid a trap from which no one could escape.

“…Damn it.”

Neung Sam muttered a curse under his breath and stared intently at Nanjing on the map.

If they finished preparations quickly and blocked the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s path, Nanjing would be stained red with the blood of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

‘Alliance Leader, please… please hurry. Please!’

❀ ❀ ❀

“Hurry!”

Hyun Jong shouted until his throat was raw. At his command, the Heavenly Friends Alliance members gritted their teeth and ran.

They knew it, no matter how fast they ran, they couldn’t avoid engaging the enemy.

Southern lands, controlled by the Four Sects Alliance, wasn’t lenient enough to easily spare those who marched in brazenly. There would surely be forces holding the retreat.

But even if they couldn’t avoid battle, they could reduce the number of opponents. If they could reach the river before the Four Sects Alliance fully assembled.

“Leader! Southern Island! Southern Island Sect is falling behind!”

“No!”

A suppressed groan escaped Hyun Jong’s lips.

He understood. They’d already exceeded their limit. Unlike the Heavenly Friends Alliance, which had crossed the Yangtze not long ago, Southern Island had already fought through the Southern lands, making it remarkable they’d come this far.

But understanding didn’t ease his frustration.

“Those with strength left, carry Southern Island’s disciples on your backs, now!”

“Yes!”

Mount Hua’s disciples rushed backward and hoisted the Southern Island’s disciples, who were in front of them, almost as if bumping into them.

“I’m fine!”

Startled Southern Island Sect’s disciples pushed at shoulders and protested reflexively, but Mount Hua’s disciples held them tighter.

“Damn it! This is no time for pride!”

“Ugh…”

In the end, Southern Island Sect’s disciples allowed themselves to be carried. This was definitely not the time for pride. If Southern Island Sect slowed them and caused them to be surrounded, pride would mean nothing.

“This way!”

“We’ll help too!”

“Move aside, city gentlemen! Strength is the specialty of the Beast Palace!”

Not only Mount Hua but members from other sects who had energy left rushed in and hoisted the Southern Island Sect’s disciples.

Seeing that, Hyun Jong gritted his teeth and stamped the ground.

‘Soon! Any moment now!’

Fortunately, they weren’t caught from behind by the remaining Ten Thousand People Clan pursuers. At this rate, they could reach the Yangtze without a major engagement.

“Squeeze out your last strength!”

Baek Cheon, carried on Hyun Jong’s back as he urged the disciples, weakly raised a hand to cover his mouth as a cough escaped.

“Cough, cough.”

Black blood from the cough stained his hand. With blurred vision, Baek Cheon looked at his hand and silently drew it into his sleeve, clenching his fist to hide it.

“Are you okay?”

In response to Hyun Jong’s question, Baek Cheon forced a nod as if he were fine.

“…I’m fine.”

“Hold on a little longer. The Yangtze is just ahead!”

“…Yes.”

A bitter smile formed at Baek Cheon’s mouth. Hyun Jong kept pushing his innate qi into him, but Baek Cheon’s body, like a cracked vessel, simply leaked it away.

‘The others…’

Baek Cheon turned his head to check on his fellow sahyung.

Given their conditions, they could have let go of consciousness, but each of them was desperately trying to hold onto awareness.

They knew it too, their fates would soon be decided.

Wasn’t it said, do all you can, then await heaven’s decree?

They had done everything possible. No, even the impossible. So now all they could do was entrust themselves to heaven.

If heaven did not forsake them, if it understood even a bit of their efforts, it would have left at least a chance for survival.

Otherwise it would be too cruel.

“Alliance Leader, it’s close now! Once we pass that hill, we’ll see the Yangtze!”

Tang Gunak’s shout turned everyone’s gaze forward. A long hill stretched ahead; pass that and they’d reach the Yangtze.

Then at last this dreadful journey would end.

‘Please!’

Baek Cheon bit his lip hard.

He didn’t ask for much. Wishing there’d be no enemies at all ahead would be absurdly naive.

He wished for only one thing!

That the enemy not be too numerous. That there be only as many enemies as could be cleared by sacrificing this single life.

“Keep going!”

Hyun Jong urged his disciples and ran on.

The dense forest thinned and brushed past them. The crest of the high hill approached them in an instant.

‘Please!’

Had he ever felt such desperation? Such urgency?

‘Heavenly Lord!’

Hyun Jong prayed and prayed again.

If the Heavenly Lord truly watched over them, if the one who had so long neglected Mount Hua despite resenting it existed, then at least in this moment watch over them. Don’t ignore this plea.

If a price in the name of karma were demanded, let an old life suffice.

“Alliance Leader!”

Hyun Jong slammed his foot into the ground with all his strength.

At the crest of the not-so-high hill, where earth met sky, Hyun Jong rose and finally looked at the scene spread out below.

What first entered his sight was an immense river, so vast it would be more fitting to call it a sea.

His gaze slowly shifted downward.

The broad land stretching to the Yangtze was dyed black and red.

What, at that moment, could that mean?

Hyun Jong’s legs buckled with despair and he staggered.

“Ah… ahh…”

Every patch of land in sight was filled with people. The road to the Yangtze was packed with bodies.

It was despair by the name of the enemy.

Chapter 1409: It’s Time To Wake Up From The Dream. (4)

“Ugh…”

Beop Jeong’s eyes trembled.

The Pirates’ fleet filled the vast Yangtze River, looking like a menacing black wave.

Of course, it was a sight they’d seen sickeningly often until now, so there was nothing particularly surprising or shocking about it.

But the problem was different: the flotillas that had cleaved the Yangtze River suddenly slowed in unison and began to stop in place.

That meant the Pirates had reached their intended destination.

Ahead of that fleet lay the wide expanse of Nanjing.

“Th-This…”

The land that should have been covered in green grass or ochre soil was filled not with grass or dirt but with people — those the world called the Four Sects Alliance.

The Ten Thousand People Clan and Black Ghost Fort, and even the smaller evil sects gathered from all directions.

The moment Beop Jeong saw that tremendous force with his own eyes, even he felt his legs go weak.

“A-Abbot.”

The Kongtong sect leader Jong Ri-Hyung also spoke in a trembling voice.

“W-What should we do now?”

Of course, Jong Ri-Hyung had never seen such an assembly of martial men. He could feel, bone-deep, what it meant to be overwhelmed by numbers.

Even a pack of insignificant dogs, if there are dozens, can tear apart a tiger. No matter how their average martial skill compared unfavorably to the prestigious Justice faction, when numbers like that gather, such differences could be ignored.

‘But what if they board those flotillas and cross to the north bank?’

Could they stop them?

Even if the Nine Great Sects held the riverbanks, could they possibly stop all those who seemed enough to fill the entire Yangtze?

At that moment Peng Yeop spoke in an emotionless voice.

“…It would be better to withdraw.”

But Jong Ri-Hyung knew that even Peng Yeop’s voice trembled faintly.

“If they attack across the river, our forces here cannot withstand them. At best we might inflict minor damage and then be annihilated.”

It was a calm, objective statement.

“B-But Peng Family Lord. If we do that…”

Jong Ri-Hyung began with a flustered face, but Peng Yeop coldly shook his head as if he already knew what would be said.

“This is no time to talk about honor.”

“…”

“Or what? Sect leader Jong, do you think we can stop them with only us?”

Jong Ri-Hyung fell silent as if his mouth had been filled with honey.

Unless all the other Nine Great Sects were gathered here, there was no way three sects alone could hold them. Besides, Shaolin’s forces weren’t even intact.

“Abbott, make a decision.”

Peng Yeop gently urged Beop Jeong. But Beop Jeong said nothing and merely stared fixedly across the river.

“Abbott.”

Just as Peng Yeop tried to press again, Beop Jeong opened his mouth and a boiling voice escaped.

“I tried to prevent this… I warned so many times! Foolish ones!”

What could have been handled had become this worst-case scenario because of the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s foolish choice.

His jaw trembled with fury.

Now there was no way to stop the Four Sects Alliance’s crossing. They would surely advance past Hubei to Henan.

What Jang Ilso sought was clear.

The Central Plains are vast. The Nine Great Sects and Five Great Families are grouped under the Nine Great Sects label, but in reality they are scattered across the wide Central Plains. Only about half of those sects are gathered north of the Yangtze, centered on Henan.

If he could seize that area?

If he could fight a decisive battle against the heart of the Nine Great Sects — centered on Shaolin, Wudang, and the Southern Island Sect — and seize its center?

Then the other sects scattered in the remote regions of the Central Plains could be picked off one by one at any time.

If Wudang and the Southern Edge sects opened their gates and joined, it might be possible to stop them; the central sects of the Nine Great Sects were certainly strong. But there would be no avoiding the aftermath of that war.

Beop Jeong knew why Shaolin and Wudang could be the center of the martial world.

It mattered that their martial arts were excellent, but more importantly they were rooted in Henan, the heart of the Central Plains.

From cults to the Demonic Cult, enemies prepared to fight to the death to destroy sects had always advanced toward Henan from the periphery of the Central Plains.

Therefore, however brutal the wars, Henan never burned unless defeated. That was how, a hundred years ago when the Demonic Cult ran rampant, Shaolin at least was spared from burning.

But this time was different.

If the Four Sects Alliance marched straight north to Henan, then even if they won, Shaolin would inevitably suffer catastrophic losses.

At the very least, Mount Song would burn without leaving a single cornerstone. Even victory would leave only scars.

How could they accept the horrific result of their headquarters burning and losing all disciples who would carry on Shaolin, while others reaped the fruits of their victory?

Cold sweat ran down Beop Jeong’s back.

“Hegemony Lord… this cunning, treacherous man…”

They had expected the Four Sects Alliance would target the Heavenly Friends Alliance first. But Jang Ilso’s blade was aimed not at the Heavenly Friends Alliance but directly at Beop Jeong’s throat.

Knowing Henan would burn, would they withdraw for now?

Or would they stay to the last person and fight to the death here to at least lessen the damage to Henan?

Beop Jeong could not easily choose either option.

“A-Abbot!”

At that moment, Jong Ri-Hyung’s ripping shout snapped him out of his thoughts. Then Beop Jeong clearly saw the flotillas filling the Yangtze all turn their prows.

Their direction was south!

Beop Jeong opened his mouth in stunned daze.

“…Alliance is…”

“Yes?”

“The Heavenly Friends Alliance! Where is the Heavenly Friends Alliance now?”

“W-Well, surely…”

Beop Jeong’s gaze turned to the hill rising over the Nanjing plain across the river.

From that distant place a familiar figure could be seen.

A vein bulged in Beop Jeong’s throat, and then a booming shout burst forth.

“HYUN JOOOOOOOOONG!”

It was a cry of despair, of reproach, and of desperate pleading.

The world seemed soaked in silence.

No — perhaps it was filled with countless noises — but whichever it was, it mattered little to Hyun Jong.

What mattered was that as far as he could see, it was all enemy.

Countless flags bearing the symbols of the Ten Thousand People Clan and Black Ghost Fort fluttered, and between them huge banners stood, adding to the pressure. The red character ‘Hegemony’ written on them seemed to stab at their eyes and chests.

And more than anything else, catching the eye was the snow-white carriage standing in the midst of that enormous army.

The grandeur of the large carriage drawn by eight white horses was imposing even from afar.

Anyone who knew the current martial world even slightly, anyone with even a passing interest in the tides of the realm, would know who rode in that carriage.

“…Jang Ilso.”

He was here.

He led all who followed him and, as if mocking the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s desperate struggles, had already seized the retreat and stood aloof.

Hyun Jong’s mind went white with shock.

He felt neither anger nor hatred. What gripped him was a vast emptiness that words like ‘dreadful’ could not fully describe.

‘Oh Heavenly Lord…’

How could it be so cruel? How could one be so heartless?

He had not asked for much. He had not shouted for rightful recompense for the agreements and decency they had upheld.

He only hoped for a small measure of peace sufficient to not nullify the blood and suffering his disciples had shed.

But had even that been too much to ask?

“Oh…”

“Th-This…”

Those who had reached the hilltop a step later were, like Hyun Jong, speechless. Even Tang Gunak of the world, and Meng So who seemed fearless, clenched their fists before the spread of despair.

“Is it too late…?”

Im So-Byeong forced a bitter smile.

The reality they had looked away from in hope was now aimed at them like the sharpest blade.

Perhaps it had been decided from the start. Maybe it was the harsh judgment they had to accept for daring to set foot on land dominated by Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso.

“…Fighting is…”

Tang Gunak stopped mid-sentence and shook his head.

It was meaningless. No matter how much the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s strength was concentrated here, breaking through them all and crossing would be sheer recklessness.

“Green Forest King! Is there a way to bypass them?”

“…”

“Green Forest King?”

Tang Gunak grasped at Im So-Byeong as if grabbing at a straw, but Im So-Byeong only answered with a bitter expression.

“It’s too late. Our opponent is the Hegemony Lord. They will not let us go.”

Tang Gunak quietly closed his eyes.

He had known it, too, but hearing it from the Green Forest King’s own mouth felt different. It was like being sentenced to a predetermined end. All that remained was desolation.

‘Was our decision wrong?’

They could not say that.

But if it was not wrong, then why were they meeting this outcome?

Despair is contagious.

Those who couldn’t move forward to see the river’s situation could gauge enough from the reactions of those in front. With senses on edge, despair rushed in faster.

“Ah…”

At that moment the Pirates’ flotillas changed course and surged toward them. The instant the numerical might of the 18 River Families set foot on this land, everything would be over.

Just as everyone there was about to put down the two letters ‘hope,’ Baek Cheon, who had been carried, strained and pushed Hyun Jong’s back and stepped down.

“Ba-Baek Cheon?”

Once on the ground he steadied his swaying head. He opened his mouth as if to say something, then hurriedly bent over.

“Cough!”

A lump of dead blood gurgled out. Seeing the ominous color of the blood, Hyun Jong’s eyes widened. He realized, belatedly, that Baek Cheon’s condition was more serious than he’d thought.

“Y-You! Your body…”

Drrrkk.

But Baek Cheon, instead of speaking, slowly drew his sword.

“W-What are you going to do?”

“Now…”

Baek Cheon forced his drooping head up. His blood-soaked lips formed a smile.

“…If we just break through over there, it’s the Northern lands…”

“…”

“Right?”

At that moment those who had come down to the ground dragged their feet and approached Baek Cheon.

“We’re all here, sasuk.”

“Ha, it’s been long. Now it’s over.”

“Now we just have to break through there!”

“I’m tired. Let’s do it quickly.”

“Amitabha. I won’t come back to the Southern lands ever again.”

Shock and stupefaction filled Hyun Jong’s and Tang Gunak’s eyes as they looked at them.

The Five Swords, and Hae Yeon. They drew their swords with calm faces, their bodies battered yet still willing to fight.

It was meaningless. Resistance was pointless.

With bodies like those, they’d die without properly swinging a sword.

But who could dare tell them that?

Who could call hollow the will of those who dragged their unmoving legs and, with battered bodies, cried that they’d still fight?

“You all…”

“Do not worry, Former Sect Elder.”

Baek Cheon smiled faintly.

“Opening the path is our specialty. Right?”

All of the Five Swords nodded.

At that moment Hyun Jong realized — rather, he had been forced to realize.

That the disciples he had always believed he must hold tightly and protect were in fact leading him instead.

Mount Hua was not protecting them — they were protecting Mount Hua and the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Baek Cheon dragged his feet and stood at the edge of the hill. His blood-soaked robe fluttered.

He saw the enemies filling the land and the ships pressing in like waves.

“Magnificent.”

Any who had taken up a sword, anyone who had hoped to be a hero, had likely dreamed of such a sight at least once.

At last Baek Cheon’s wish was coming true.

A voice of Chung Myung that he had once heard echoed in his mind.

A hero? Well, I don’t know, but… perhaps a hero is someone who dreams an unbreakable dream, even until the moment of death.

“Alright.”

Baek Cheon lifted the corner of his mouth.

“Then I won’t let it break. Not ever.”

His sword soon glowed with a red murderous aura.

Chapter 1410: It’s Time To Wake Up From The Dream. (5)

Should one call it reckless? Or should one call it foolish?

No matter how you look at it, Baek Cheon’s actions now fall into neither category. Yet no one watching his back could bring themselves to say that to him.

Anyone who takes up a sword must contemplate their end.

As Chung Myung had habitually said, one who wields a sword must remember that one day they too may be cut down by that sword.

So when facing an unbeatable foe and an inevitable death, what should a warrior do?

Baek Cheon was now showing everyone the answer.

Hyun Jong turned his head and looked back. A small sigh escaped him.

“Ah…”

And it wasn’t only the Five Swords. The disciples of Mount Hua filling the rear, the Tang Family’s elders, and Namgung and other warriors soon looked at Hyun Jong with composed faces.

“Why that expression, Alliance Leader?”

Elder Han Yi-Myung of the Ice Palace smiled gently.

“Surely you don’t think we were dragged here merely by your order without the resolve to give our lives?”

“Elder Han…”

Han Yi-Myung shook his head.

“That expression is an insult to us. No one was dragged here. And no one came unaware that their life might be at risk. Knowing all that, we came of our own accord.”

Many nodded at Han Yi-Myung’s words.

The reality they faced was harsh. But no one blamed Hyun Jong. He had only done his best; it was they themselves who chose to cross the river.

“But…”

“Of course no one would be pleased to die here. However…”

Namgung Myung spoke with a resolute face.

“It’s a hundred times better than standing by and watching our allies die across that river.”

“As expected of our elder.”

“Of course. Namgung would die first rather than do such a thing!”

The Namgung Family, who had painfully learned what it’s like to be shunned on Plum Blossom Island, looked oddly relieved.

Hyun Jong bit his lip.

He knew. Most of these words were meant to ease his burden. Outward calm would mask an inner fear he couldn’t help but feel.

“Alliance Leader.”

At that moment Tang Gunak spoke in a cold voice.

“It won’t be easy, but we must try. Even if Jang Ilso is there, the Four Sects Alliance is not like us. Those who bicker among themselves cannot coordinate perfectly in battle.”

“…”

“So we just need to create an opening. The Tang Family will take the lead. And it seems the Pirates are almost here too; we can seize that boat.”

The Pirates landing meant more enemies to defeat on this land. It was by no means good news for the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

But Hyun Jong nodded instead of pointing that out.

“Yes, Family Lord. We’ll try.”

Hyun Jong gripped his sword tightly.

There was no room for surrender. All that remained was to fight with everything. Even if resistance was futile, they would fight until the last person.

Hyun Jong unconsciously looked over at the elders of Mount Hua.

Whether his intent had been conveyed or they already thought the same, their eyes shone with the same light as Hyun Jong’s.

At that moment Geum Yang-Baek pushed past protesting disciples and walked forward.

“If someone must take the lead… it should be me.”

“Sect leader!”

“Sect leader, you shouldn’t—!”

Though his knees seemed about to buckle, he stood firm.

“…You won’t say no, will you?”

A smile spread across Geum Yang-Baek’s pale face.

Hyun Jong, of course, could not refuse. Who would dare utter ‘no’ before Geum Yang-Baek now? No one human could.

“Sect leader Geum…”

Geum Yang-Baek’s smile deepened.

“It seems I’ve finally found a place to die.”

“…”

“I’ve lived long enough. Now I have only one wish left.”

No words were needed to know what that wish was. It was also Hyun Jong’s wish.

‘Ancestors…’

He began to understand why Mount Hua’s ancestors braved death on that distant peak. With what spirit they fought.

What they wished for was that those left in distant lands would survive. Nothing more.

“Baek Cheon, fall back.”

“…Yes?”

Baek Cheon turned back in disbelief, but Hyun Jong shook his head decisively.

For a moment Baek Cheon had indeed led them all. And there was no denying he had already surpassed him.

This was simply an old man’s final stubbornness at the end of a long life, an indulgent desire.

“Disciples, fall back. Elders, step forward.”

“Yes! Former Sect leader!”

Hyun Sang and Un disciples sent the young disciples back and stepped forward. Even Hyun Young advanced silently and resolutely.

“E-elders…”

And at the same time Geum Yang-Baek raised his voice.

“Elders of the Southern Island Sect, take the lead!”

“Namgung as well!”

“Youngsters, fall back! This is no place for you!”

Those who bore the title of elder, and those who considered themselves elders though they lacked the rank, all pushed the young disciples aside and stepped forward.

“Elders, this is…!”

“Shut up and fall back!”

At that sharp rebuke, those who would have objected retreated with pale faces. No dissent would be heard. Those left behind bit their lips.

Hyun Jong looked around at everyone and spoke.

“I am not saying do not fight.”

“…”

“Only that the manner of fighting differs. Opening the way is our fight. Your fight is to survive somehow and cross that river.”

“Alliance Leader…”

Who would dare utter that they would not?

If everyone here dies, it is over. But if someone survives, the will of the people continues. Then these deaths will finally hold meaning.

How, then, could this be called a trivial fight?

“As of the Tang Family Lord…”

Hyun Jong was about to ask someone to guard the rear, but Tang Gunak stood steadfastly at his side without a word.

“Now you’re going to slight me for my age?”

Hyun Jong ended up laughing. Who could dare say such a thing to Tang Gunak of the world?

“I am the lord of Tang Family.”

“Indeed… I was momentarily rude.”

“The pursuit force is approaching. There’s no time for long speeches.”

Hyun Jong nodded and exchanged glances with Geum Yang-Baek.

The end should be clean. If this is their last stand, at least show their disciples a dignified face.

Now they would simply fight with everything they had!

“Heavenly Friends Alliance, listen…!”

It was the moment Hyun Jong drew his sword and shouted with that resolve.

A loud voice cut in and interrupted.

“Al-Alliance Leader!”

“Hm?”

“There! There beyond the river! Ah, no! On the river! Look there, there!”

Hyun Jong whirled to look. His eyes widened at once.

“Uh… what?”

An unbelievable thing was happening. Something no one had ever imagined was unfolding.

“Shaolin! It’s Shaolin! Shaolin and the Nine Great Sects, Alliance Leader!”

Those who had been holding the riverbank beyond the river.

Those they knew existed but had tried not to acknowledge.

A group of monks in Shaolin’s yellow robes, the symbol of Shaolin, thrust off the water and sped across the river.

“S-Shaolin?”

At that moment a cry, almost like a sob, burst from Hae Yeon’s mouth.

“Abbottt!”

He could see them clearly. His sahyung-sajaes, whom he had known were beyond the river but couldn’t bring himself to call on, were risking their lives and barreling across.

Tears welled in his wide eyes.

It was truly Shaolin. His sahyung-sajaes were coming here now.

“…Ah, no, this…?

“It’s not only Shaolin! The Peng Family too! The Kongtong are leaping into the river! Reinforcements! The Nine Great Sects are coming to our aid! They’re crossing the river, Alliance Leader!”

Hyun Jong’s mouth fell open.

He couldn’t believe it. Shaolin, that Beop Jeong would risk his life to help the Heavenly Friends Alliance? Cross the river to fight on land occupied by the Four Sects Alliance?

Who could have imagined such a thing?

But no matter how many times he closed and opened his eyes, the scene did not change. They were unmistakably coming.

Right across this river!

SPLASH!

Beop Jeong struck the water repeatedly and let out a thunderous lion’s roar.

“First class disciples, check the Pirates! Elders, secure landing points for our allies on the riverbank!”

“Yes!”

SPLASH!

Each time his feet struck the water, his body shot forward.

Beneath him, warriors of Shaolin, the Kongtong Sect, and the Peng Family swam with all their might. Beop Jeong’s face, lifted to gaze across the river, twisted like a demon’s.

‘Did you underestimate me, Jang Ilso?’

Did he think Beop Jeong would retreat? That he would just watch with folded hands, as before?

If so, he was mistaken.

Beop Jeong was not afraid of battle. He feared defeat, precisely a defeat that meant losing everything. He would not calmly wait for such a loss.

He had avoided fighting only because better paths existed. Now that was gone; there was no reason to hesitate to fight and open the way.

‘Hyun Jong!’

Beop Jeong’s gaze passed the Four Sects Alliance and fixed on Hyun Jong.

Nothing had changed. If they could join forces with the Heavenly Friends Alliance, they could take on the Four Sects Alliance.

As long as the resolve to cross the river remained, the advantage was still theirs!

‘Shaolin does not burn!’

There are things that must be protected at all costs.

Beop Jeong would not hesitate even to burn himself alive to preserve the name Shaolin.

What was there to fear in hesitating to ford? What was crossing this river compared to that!

“Defeat the Evil Sect’s villains and save the Heavenly Friends Alliance!”

Beop Jeong’s booming voice echoed like thunder from the pavilion.

“Yes, Abbott!”

The Shaolin monks likewise answered with all their might.

The world’s foremost gate, the thousand-year Shaolin.

Those who had guarded the Central Plains for countless ages moved to where they belonged in this moment. Their majestic aura dyed the Yangtze river golden.

Meanwhile, a strange silence fell over the Heavenly Friends Alliance side.

Perhaps it was a moment for gratitude. Perhaps it was a sight to tremble with excitement.

Bitterly, Shaolin, the leader of the Nine Great Sects and long at odds with the Heavenly Friends Alliance, was now risking their lives to save them.

But the faces of those on the hill showed not so much emotion as bewilderment.

Jo Gul, staring dumbfounded at the unreal sight that was hard to believe even after seeing it, let his mouth slip open.

“Th-…”

After choosing his words several times, he could not help but mutter.

“…Has that bald head lost his mind?”

It was horribly rude, but no one pointed it out.

Chapter 1411: It’s Time To Wake Up From The Dream. (6)

The ranks of the Four Sects Alliance fill the riverbank. Fleets occupy the vast Yangtze River. A golden tide pours toward that imposing place as if in a deluge.

Overwhelming. Will I see a sight like this again in my lifetime?

But this was no time to be stunned by the sight.

“Al-alliance leader!”

“Y-Yes!”

Hyun Jong, who had been dazed, jolted and lifted his sword again.

“W-we, the Alliance too—!”

“Wait! Ah! Wait!”

In that moment, Im So-Byeong leapt almost bodily and grabbed Hyun Jong’s arm, clinging on.

“G-Green Forest King?”

“Not yet, not yet! Wait just a bit longer! We must strike all at once when those monk bastards reach the riverbank!”

“Ah, that’s right! Understood.”

Hyun Jong swallowed dryly. His heart pounded as if mad.

That the day would come when they’d join forces with the Nine Great Sects to fight—such an obvious thing—could it really happen in reality?

‘We can do it!’

Hope filled Hyun Jong’s eyes, which had been full only of resolve.

Of course, the situation remained grim. Even joining forces with Shaolin, facing the Four Sects Alliance would never be easy.

But it would be incomparable to fighting with only the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Moreover…

“Has heaven itself aided us? They’ve become a perfect pincer at a timely moment!”

The Green Forest King’s words were precise.

The best way to break a concentrated enemy is a simultaneous attack from both sides. They had unintentionally seized a perfect pincer opportunity.

If they made good use of this chance, they might pierce that thick center and open a path to the Yangtze River!

Even without each side risking total annihilation, there was now a chance to secure a route of survival.

“Alliance leader! They’re wavering!”

Tang Gunak shouted in an unusually agitated voice.

Indeed, it was true. The Four Sects Alliance, which had defended the riverbank like an iron wall, was thrown into confusion as if they had detected the Nine Great Sects crossing the river. Their once-imposing banners whipped and flailed like a storm-tossed sea.

‘It is working!’

A clear path cleaving through the solid iron wall appeared. It was a road connecting them to the Northern lands and also linking them to the Nine Great Sects.

“Everyone prepare! It’s almost time!”

The golden tide had now reached the center of the river. Given their speed, they would reach the riverbank soon. They had to surge forward at that moment without fail.

Everyone’s hearts thudded.

‘Hegemony Lord!’

Hyun Jong’s gaze turned to Jang Ilso’s carriage at the center of the Four Sects Alliance ranks.

He was certain. No matter how much Jang Ilso schemed to shake the world, or what world-reversing feats he performed, it would be hard to oppose the sincere unity achieved in the name of righteousness!

Perhaps Jang Ilso did not know: even if intents differ, if the destination is shared, forces can join without issue!

It was then.

“They’re moving!”

The Four Sects Alliance ranks began to writhe like waves. The shockwave radiating from the ornate carriage soon swept across the entire Alliance.

From the hilltop, Hyun Jong could see it clearly.

The Alliance forces lined along the bank were splitting precisely in two.

“…W-wait?”

Centered on a single line that had formed in the middle, the army was dividing in two. As if… opening a path.

“The Pirates’ ships are moving too! Left, left and right! They’re splitting to both sides and moving!”

A path opens. A road leading between the Gu faction and the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

The route they absolutely had to secure was opening on its own. From the Four Sects Alliance’s perspective, it was the very path they would have to block like an iron wall by any means.

But Hyun Jong could not cheer at the sight.

“Why…?”

Why… why would they open a path? Why on earth?

“What…?”

Beop Jeong was the same. Hovering in midair, he watched the scene in stunned bewilderment.

The black ships of the Pirates, which had nearly anchored at the bank, suddenly turned their bows left and right to open Shaolin’s way—almost as if welcoming them to the Southern lands.

“E-everyone stop! Slow your speed, immediately!”

Beop Jeong cried out urgently at the incomprehensible situation.

“Abbott! Staying in the water is dangerous!”

“I know that!”

If the Pirates attacked from the water, they could face the worst of crises. Beop Jeong knew that too. But without understanding the situation accurately, he had no choice.

Beop Jeong’s eyes trembled as if struck by an earthquake.

“What on earth… are you playing at, Jang Ilso!”

“G-Green Forest King!”

Hyun Jong whirled to Im So-Byeong. He thought the Green Forest King might interpret the situation. But Im So-Byeong was just as stunned.

Im So-Byeong repeatedly scrutinized the Four Sects Alliance’s movements with a flustered face.

He could understand opening a path. The Jang Ilso he knew would not hesitate to deliberately create an opening and set a trap behind it.

Yet Im So-Byeong was alarmed because he could not sense such intent in their movement. If that had been the plan, they wouldn’t have widened the path so quickly.

Then two possibilities remained: Jang Ilso was using a tactic beyond imagination, or he was genuinely opening the path with no ulterior motive.

But could even Jang Ilso possibly employ a tactic so subtle that Im So-Byeong wouldn’t detect a trace? Even if Zhuge Liang returned to life, it would be impossible.

His mind raced.

“Then why?”

Im So-Byeong’s face grew ashen as if he’d seen a ghost.

No—seeing a ghost might be better. The more he thought, the deeper the terror that came from facing the incomprehensible situation.

But the bewildering situation did not end there.

“A-alliance leader! T-they’re widening the distance further!”

Hyun Jong clenched his fist tightly and trembled.

The Four Sects Alliance forces that had opened the way did not stop there; they widened the gap even more.

Looking at events without fixation, the Four Sects Alliance was now dividing its army into two and advancing on both sides.

As if… yes, as if…

“R-retreating?”

From here?

Everyone of the Heavenly Friends Alliance on the hill was left speechless.

“B-because of the Nine Great Sects?”

“There’s no way that’s it!”

“No, but if not that, then what on earth…”

Even if the Nine Great Sects joined, the Four Sects Alliance had no reason to withdraw. They still held the advantage. Either side could still make a go of it.

No, no.

From the Alliance’s viewpoint, they must fight. If they let the Heavenly Friends Alliance go to the Northern lands, they’d gain nothing compared to their losses.

So why suddenly open the path? Why withdraw instead?

“…What the hell…”

Hyun Jong, almost spellbound, turned to Chung Myung.

The Ten Thousand People Clan had paid a tremendous price to kill Chung Myung and the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s young warriors.

And yet they’d retreat? Jang Ilso was no fool of the world.

An indescribable omen swept over them. Those showing their backs were certainly the enemy, yet it was they who trembled with unease.

“W-what should we do?”

The Heavenly Friends Alliance and even the leaders of the Nine Great Sects—the giants who lead the central plains—were floundering, unsure what to do.

Then someone’s groan broke the confusion and tension.

“Ugh…”

“Ch-Chung Myung!”

“Chung Myung! Are you awake?”

Chung Myung’s lashes trembled as he lay in Tang Gunak’s arms.

Soon Chung Myung opened his eyes and weakly scanned his surroundings with a gaze devoid of strength.

“Where am I…?”

Chung Myung asked, but no one could readily speak.

They did not know how to explain the current situation; even those who had watched with their own eyes did not understand.

But there was no need.

Chung Myung turned his blurry gaze and briefly observed the battlefield.

After a moment, his body convulsed with a twitch.

“T-them right now…”

He frantically pushed Tang Gunak away with trembling hands. They tried to stop him, but Chung Myung’s feeble touch carried such stubborn will that they could not.

Staggering down, Chung Myung’s eyes went bloodshot.

“Jang Ilso…”

“Uh, huh?”

“Jang Ilso is!”

The cracked voice burst like a scream. A startled Jo Gul pointed at the carriage in the midst of the Four Sects Alliance ranks.

“Th-there! It looks like there!”

At the sight of the massive carriage drawn by eight white horses—something only Jang Ilso would dare ride—an anguished sound escaped Chung Myung’s mouth.

“…There? You say he’s there?”

“Huh? Chung… Myung?”

Jo Gul flinched. In Chung Myung’s returning gaze there was a fierce emotion he had never seen before.

“Th-this damn—!”

Cursing, Chung Myung suddenly reached and drew Jo Gul’s sword from its sheath. He kicked off the ground and leapt up, spewing terrifying sword qi.

“Ch-Chung Myung! What are you doing!”

“Waaaaaah!”

Kraaaaah!

The red sword qi pouring from the tip of Chung Myung’s blade sliced across the riverbank like a torrent. In an instant it struck the carriage Jang Ilso rode in.

“Eek!”

“W-what are you doing, man!”

Bang!

Dust exploded. Chung Myung, back on the ground, clamped his hand over his mouth. Dark red blood welled up between his fingers.

“What the hell did you…”

“There! Look over there!”

Someone shouted that. Eyes that had focused on Chung Myung all turned at once to the front.

The thick dust settled, revealing a shattered carriage. In the space stood a single figure whose shape took form slowly.

A blood-red long robe, glittering ornaments on each finger and wrist, and the distinctive crown on his head.

“So, Jang Ilso…”

Baek Cheon bit his lip hard.

At the sight of his face pale as death and lips red as blood, everyone involuntarily held their breath.

But Chung Myung growled and asked.

“Who?”

“Huh?”

His face was contorted with rage.

“Who? Who is Jang Ilso, who? That bastard?”

“Chung Myung?”

Most thought Chung Myung’s mind had snapped a little. It was understandable—he’d been badly wounded and had just woken; he couldn’t be entirely sane.

But some understood the meaning of his words and hurriedly glanced back at Jang Ilso.

“Uh…”

“Ah… ah!”

Im So-Byeong sank to the ground as if his legs had given out.

“Green Forest King! What’s wrong?”

The Green Forest King muttered with a haunted face.

“No…”

“What do you mean…”

“No. He’s not Jang Ilso! He’s a fake!”

“…What?”

“Everyone… we’ve all been deceived. He wasn’t here from the start. He never intended to fight!”

“N-nonsense!”

Baek Cheon summoned all his remaining strength to examine Jang Ilso again. His pupils froze, then began to tremble.

He had seen that Jang Ilso up close many times. Thus he could tell.

This one was not Jang Ilso.

Same appearance, but clearly different. He looked similar, dressed similarly, but none of Jang Ilso’s characteristic intimidation was present.

Baek Cheon snapped his head aside.

“Th-then where is Jang Ilso right now? If he’s not here, where on earth is he?!”

“Ugh…”

In that moment, a spent voice escaped from Chung Myung.

“This damned… man…”

Thud.

“Chung Myung!”

“Damn it! Tang Family Lord!”

As his consciousness faded, urgent shouts echoed in Chung Myung’s ears, muffled as if heard from underwater.

‘No…’

I can’t faint here…

‘Jang Il…so…’

Someone’s mad laughter sounded like an auditory hallucination from far away.

❀ ❀ ❀

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso snorted and leisurely looked back.

Though the interior of the carriage was splendid, from the outside it looked shabby—quite literally like a giant cart piled with goods.

One couldn’t tell from the outside that inside the old, seemingly cheaply loaded wagon there was such a luxurious interior.

Aside from a thoroughly disguised door, the only connection between the inside and outside was a tiny window no larger than half a handspan.

After gazing at the ragged carriage and shabby horses for a moment, Jang Ilso turned to the other side.

Many people bowed before him. Their dress might be ordinary enough to be seen anywhere, but their eyes and the weapons on their backs made it clear they were no ordinary folk.

The elites of the Ten Thousand People Clan—disguised as lowly sect laborers who hauled grain in old carts and spread into the Southern lands beyond the Beggars’ Union’s sight—had gathered here now.

Like loyal ministers greeting their king, he silently scanned those kneeling on one knee. He then smiled and murmured.

“See? I told you.”

His mocking smile painted his lips blood-red.

“…It’s not worth it.”

After all, they’re mere pawns.

“You, and even I.”

He, his built reputation, the fear others felt toward him, even their perceptions—all were merely tools to be used by Jang Ilso. He could twist and crush them at will.

There was no value worth protecting.

No one who deserved to be hanged.

Everything that fills the world is just cheap trash.

After briefly looking at those prostrate before him, Jang Ilso slowly raised his gaze. A towering mountain stood ahead.

A blue mountain rising as if to touch the sky. So magnificent that the world called it Qingcheng Mountain.

Step.

Jang Ilso took slow steps through those kneeling, toward the path leading up the blue mountain.

“The more splendid the beginning, the better. Let’s burn it all down.”

His calm, gentle voice rang out. The Ten Thousand People Clan members following his steps rose one by one.

Behind the Ten Thousand People Clan who followed him, a procession formed of those disguised in varied attire and warriors wearing blood-red martial robes.

The Lower District Sect, and the Blood Sect.

The forces that had formed the Four Sects Alliance together with Black Ghost Fort and the Pirates had now revealed themselves in this Sichuan land.

All followed Jang Ilso, the madman, trampling up the Qingcheng Mountain.

“Hahahah.”

A suppressed laugh escaped Jang Ilso’s mouth. That small laugh quickly swelled into unbearable delight and overwhelming madness, echoing over the mountain.

“Hahahahaha! Ahahahahahah! Ahahahahahahahaha!”

He would scorch Qingcheng, sweep past Emei, sweep through the Tang Family and Dian-cang, and soon a vast blood-red conflagration would bloom to cover the world.

Right here, in the land of Sichuan.

Chapter 1412: If You Really Want It, You Should Take It. (1)

“…Sichuan?”

“Yes?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s face, watching the bowed person, twisted strangely.

“He is headed to Sichuan?”

“Yes! The Alliance lord ordered that we report the situation as soon as the troops arrive.”

Ho Ga-Myung was momentarily at a loss for words. When he raised his head, he saw the Four Sects Alliance forces withdrawing from Nanjing.

Even amid that chaos, what caught his eye was a person standing awkwardly in the center of a smashed carriage.

Jang Ilso. No, someone with an appearance similar to Jang Ilso.

Wearing the clothes only Jang Ilso wears in the world and the ornaments only he dons, trembling in fear.

Ho Ga-Myung knew precisely who he was. He had to know. It was none other than Ho Ga-Myung who had used Jang Ilso’s distinctive appearance to secure his safety.

Shadow warrior?

Yes, Alliance lord. It’s absolutely necessary.

Don’t we have the Thousand Faced Scholar?

He’s difficult to use so freely. Please allow it.

Hm, Ga-Myung. You always worry too much and do unnecessary things.

Alliance lord.

Ha, do as you wish. I won’t need to use him anyway.

“Ha… haha…”

A low laugh escaped Ho Ga-Myung. The entire situation spread across his mind as if he had seen it all at once.

“Hahahahahaha!”

The suppressed laughter gradually grew into a large, manic grin quite unbefitting Ho Ga-Myung.

Exactly like Jang Ilso. Terribly like Jang Ilso.

Twisting what the whole world believed without a second thought, treating Ho Ga-Myung’s earnest pleas as trivial yet using them when convenient.

All of it was quintessentially Jang Ilso.

He deceives everyone: the world, the Four Sects Alliance, the Heavenly Friends Alliance, the Nine Great Sects.

Even Ho Ga-Myung, who followed him most closely.

“Haha…”

Ho Ga-Myung stopped laughing and exhaled deeply.

‘Since when, exactly?’

No matter how much of an Alliance Lord he was, he could not have expected those Heavenly Friends Alliance bastards to appear in the Southern Island Sect.

That means he had envisioned all of this from the moment the Heavenly Friends Alliance came to the Southern Island sect and when he learned Ho Ga-Myung was following them.

In that short time, Jang Ilso had effectively placed the whole world in his palm and toyed with it.

His whole body chilled and goosebumps ran down his spine.

“Did the Alliance lord… say anything to you separately?”

Ho Ga-Myung asked, narrowing his eyes slightly. As if expecting the question, the bowed man raised his head and looked up at Ho Ga-Myung.

“The Alliance lord said… the troops already know what they must do, so there’s no need to tell them explicitly.”

“…”

“But he did say that once the troops arrive here, the command over everyone gathered here will be in the hands of the commander.”

Ho Ga-Myung laughed. Even that answer was so Jang Ilso.

Ho Ga-Myung shook his head and stared blankly at the Four Sects Alliance forces.

“Who is controlling them right now?”

“Temporarily, the lieutenants of the commander are acting according to the orders the Alliance lord previously issued.”

“Where are they?”

“On the left, in the center of the Ten Thousand People Clan.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes cooled ominously.

“Go that way. We must regain control.”

If Jang Ilso intends to trample Sichuan, there is one thing he must do: tie down those here so they cannot go to Sichuan.

After that, he will of course go to Sichuan himself.

“Move, now!”

“Yes, troops!”

A strange light briefly flashed in Ho Ga-Myung’s swiftly moving eyes. Even he found it hard to explain what he was feeling.

‘Alliance lord.’

Ho Ga-Myung felt a clear unease toward Mount Hua and the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost. He believed Jang Ilso was excessively lenient toward that Plum Blossom Sword Ghost. Perhaps that leniency and composure might one day twist to stab the Alliance lord in the throat.

That was why he obsessed over them and vowed to kill them with his own hands by any means necessary.

But even his desperate pursuit, ready to die, was for Jang Ilso merely another useful card to be played.

Someone’s piece. A pawn to be used casually.

Therefore…

‘How can one not marvel?’

How could anyone not pledge loyalty to the one who turns even Shaolin, Mount Hua, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance into mere game pieces?

Even if people were more numerous than grains of sand, only Jang Ilso could create such a spectacle in the world.

Ho Ga-Myung’s gaze suddenly turned. The leaders of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, whom he had relentlessly pursued, were gathered there.

They were too far away to read their faces, but he knew without looking what expressions they wore now.

‘Pathetic.’

Though he had hated them fiercely, in this moment Ho Ga-Myung felt something like pity for them.

If they had committed any wrongdoing, it was only one thing.

Turning Jang Ilso into an enemy.

❀ ❀ ❀

A heavy silence fell instantly.

The leaders of the Heavenly Friends Alliance stood speechless. People often say ‘lost for words,’ but never had that phrase felt so apt.

“Th… this is…”

Even when they managed to open their mouths, no words came out. Crushed words swirled chaotically in their heads, never forming sentences. Only bewilderment and despair felt vivid.

“W-What do you mean? Jang Ilso isn’t here? Then who is that guy? He’s right there, Jang Ilso!”

Jo Gul, bewildered and at a loss, finally shouted as if he couldn’t understand. His finger pointed at the man in a red robe standing in the settling dust cloud.

“…He’s not Jang Ilso.”

“W-Where else in the world would someone strut around in such flashy attire besides that bastard Jang Ilso…!”

Jo Gul, about to retort to Baek Cheon, abruptly lost his words. Mid-sentence, realization struck.

Only Jang Ilso walked around like that in the whole world.

That’s why the disguise was so easy.

If someone wore that red robe and ornaments and applied the distinctive makeup, no one would suspect he was not Jang Ilso.

Unless you could get close enough to inspect him closely, at a distance where his aura couldn’t be felt, anyone in the world would be certain he was Jang Ilso.

No need to look far; weren’t the people here doing the same?

“…Why didn’t we doubt it? Why…”

Baek Cheon bit his lip hard.

From the moment he saw that massive carriage, he had assumed Jang Ilso would be here. It was inevitable. This was a moment that would decide the fate of the world. In such a moment, it was unthinkable for Jang Ilso to withdraw. At least in Baek Cheon’s understanding.

This wasn’t Baek Cheon’s fault. No one in the world would have thought that way.

At that moment, a voice like a scream echoed.

“Then where is Jang Ilso? Where the hell is he right now!”

An indescribable dread washed over everyone.

The Ten Thousand People Clan had suffered countless losses chasing the Heavenly Friends Alliance. The problem was that Jang Ilso was never someone who suffered losses. If he intended to let those who chased him go after such sacrifices, he’d naturally inflict even greater losses.

What on earth was it?

A prey larger than the opportunity to trample the entire Heavenly Friends Alliance.

“Th-That’s impossible. There’s no place in the murim world with a concentration of power greater than here!”

At Namgung Do-Wei’s shout, everyone nodded in unison.

“Then what on earth…”

At that moment, a weak voice was heard.

“That’s… not it.”

“Yes?”

All the commoners turned to look. At the end of their gazes, Im So-Byeong sat down with a dazed face.

“Bigger… it’s not that. From the start, this place wasn’t a tasty prey for him.”

“What do you mean by that, Green Forest King?”

“…If it were him, he’d have considered the possibility that that Beop Jeong might cross the river. If that happened, even with the Four Sects Alliance’s forces concentrated, they’d face a battle whose outcome couldn’t be certain. Win and you gain much; lose and everything ends. No… even if he wins, he’d have to bear tremendous losses.”

Hyun Jong nodded.

Indeed, the Justice faction forces here were at least that strong. With the Heavenly Friends Alliance joined by Shaolin, the Peng Family, and others, even the Four Sects Alliance couldn’t take them lightly.

“But… he wasn’t interested in that kind of fight from the start.”

“…Yes?”

“Because he’s that kind of bastard! Damn it, exactly that kind of bastard! He mocks most of all those who take the hard road when easy prey is available. He saw things he could devour without shedding blood! Tender flesh he could pick apart with overwhelming force!”

A rancor surfaced on Im So-Byeong’s exhausted face.

This was a different order of defeat than being outread by Ho Ga-Myung. From the start, Jang Ilso hadn’t even stepped onto the board Im So-Byeong had been watching. No even Im So-Byeong himself had been one of the pieces on the board Jang Ilso observed.

A despair came over him as if some absolute being had lightly picked him up and placed him somewhere. A crushing helplessness overwhelmed Im So-Byeong.

“W-Where on earth is it then?”

“…Qing.”

“Yes?”

“Qingcheng! In Sichuan. He’s surely trampling the Qingcheng right now with hidden forces and the Lower District Sect in tow. Unlike here, that place has made no preparations for war one of the Nine Great sects!”

Everyone turned pale. Im So-Byeong continued.

“And of course he’ll burn Emei nearby as well. If he surprises and shatters Qingcheng, Emei will have to fight alone. With the combined strength of the Ten Thousand People Clan and the Lower District Sect, picking off Qingcheng sect and Emei Sect separately is nothing.”

A silence so complete you could hear a pin drop settled over the surroundings.

“Thoroughly…”

Countless images passed through Im So-Byeong’s mind like scenes.

The arduous journey from the Southern Island to here, the desperate fights where they risked everything to survive.

But to Jang Ilso, it was nothing more than rat-drive. While everyone’s gaze was fixed on that loud rat-drive, Jang Ilso strolled with muddy feet straight into the Justice faction’s inner sanctum.

“We were thoroughly toyed with. Thoroughly…”

Then someone who had been quietly listening spoke up.

“Next?”

“…Yes?”

Yu Yiseol met Im So-Byeong’s gaze with an impassive face.

“Qingcheng and Emei. What’s next after that?”

“Next is…”

Im So-Byeong turned his head slowly. In an instant, everyone’s gaze followed him to one place.

At the end stood a man whose face had gone pale.

“…The Tang Family.”

The head of the Sichuan Tang Family, the Poison King Tang Gunak.

Chapter 1413: If You Really Want It, You Should Take It. (2)

The mountain named ‘Qingcheng’ for its emblematic verdant greenery. That mountain was now dyed in a color that did not suit its name.

The green foliage and the antique pavilions were engulfed by red flames, and the ground where the fire dared not tread was covered in dark crimson blood more than flame.

It seemed as if the world had been entirely cloaked in scarlet malice.

Among them, Qingcheng Sect leader Byeok Hyun-Ja gasped for breath.

“Huff…”

Qingcheng was his lifelong home and everything to him. And now that Qingcheng was collapsing from an unthinkable assault.

“Cough!”

With the cough, dark red blood spurted from his mouth. From the holes in his abdomen and the deep wounds carved across his body, one could easily predict his fate.

But Byeok Hyun-Ja felt not an iota of pain. Compared to the agony of Qingcheng, more important than his life, the pain felt in this body was negligible.

“Cough! Cough!”

Spitting dark blood again, he forced his heavy drooping head up and looked ahead.

‘Ii Byeok…’

The sajae who had been with him all his life had already fallen as a cold corpse. Looking at the horribly mutilated body, Byeok Hyun-Ja’s heart was shaken by an indescribable grief and rage.

“Aaaaaaah!”

“Aaah! Sa—save me… Aaaaaaah!”

From afar came someone’s desperate scream.

The scream, striking like a blow against his ears, clawed at his chest. His disciples, precious beyond measure, were losing their lives beneath the cold blades of those wicked men.

But Byeok Hyun-Ja no longer had the strength to run and save his disciples.

No one escaped this catastrophe. The young disciples that he had somehow whisked away at the last moment might be fleeing desperately under Providence’s guidance, but there was no guarantee they could evade those fiends’ grasp.

It’s over.

Yes, it’s all over.

The illustrious Qingcheng, a prestigious clan of Sichuan with a long history, meets its end today, not at the hands of the Demonic Cult or the imperial palace, but at those lowly Evil Sect scoundrels.

‘How…’

How on earth did it come to this?

Why is Qingcheng burning?

Why have they shown up here?

He had thought he could merely watch the war on the Yangtze river from afar, but why did the Evil Sect’s blades fall upon this place?

“Huff… heh heh…”

Byeok Hyun-Ja groaned, somewhere between a laugh and a cry, and stared blankly.

The main gate of Blue Sky Palace, symbol of Qingcheng, burned in the flames. Not a gate to a pure Taoist hall, but like the gates of hell opened wide.

And through that burning gate, a man slowly emerged.

A man clad in a crimson robe that fluttered like the blood soaked across Qingcheng, fierce as the inferno engulfing it.

A man adorned from head to toe in gaudy ornaments that utterly clashed with the horrific scene.

Step.

A warped smile hung at the man’s lips.

The sight of this gaudily dressed figure entering through the burning gate was so overwhelming it seized even Byeok Hyun-Ja’s gaze, nearly dazed by grief and rage.

“Jang…”

Suddenly Byeok Hyun-Ja thought it all resembled a theatrical play.

All of this—the scene, the man wandering within it, even himself kneeling before him—felt like a scene from an opera, not reality; solemn and yet ludicrous.

“Jang… Ilso…”

Perhaps it truly was nothing more than a performance.

Wherever Jang Ilso set foot, only such things happened. He shattered the reality ordinary people conceived and only staged events as if from a play.

“Jang Ilsoooooooo!”

A beastly howl burst from Byeok Hyun-Ja’s mouth—filled with grief for the dead, despair at an unbearable reality, and hatred for the architect of it all.

But even hearing that gut-wrenching wail, Jang Ilso showed no notable reaction; he only deepened his smile.

“Now then, Jang…”

Byeok Hyun-Ja’s hand trembled.

The disaster standing before him rolled through his vision.

The flames consuming everything from behind, the disaster’s blood-red robe, the gaudy ornaments, even those blood-red lips.

Byeok Hyun-Ja looked into the eyes of the calamity, Jang Ilso.

Yet Jang Ilso’s pupils were calm and tranquil. Absent was the madness or thrill criminals often display when murdering; instead, they seemed almost sombre.

How was one to understand that the man who led this atrocious slaughter had the calmest gaze of all?

“Huuuu… huu…”

Moans like sobs escaped him continuously—anger, sorrow, fear, and pain intertwined.

Jang Ilso looked down at Byeok Hyun-Ja, who had collapsed wounded.

Surely sharp sneers or ridicule would pour out; otherwise, there was nothing left to share between them.

But, surprisingly, Jang Ilso’s response differed from what Byeok Hyun-Ja expected.

He… smiled brightly.

There was no malice. Perhaps one should call it casualness. With a cheerfully blank smile devoid of scorn, Jang Ilso spoke amiably.

“Now that I think about it… we’re acquainted, aren’t we, Sect leader? It’s been a while. So—how have you been?”

At the gentle, soft greeting, Byeok Hyun-Ja’s fingertips scraped the ground.

No mockery or reprimand in the world could wound so painfully. Even a poisoned blade piercing his lungs would not hurt this way—the blade at least could not ravage his soul.

“Why…”

Byeok Hyun-Ja released his clenched lower lip and spoke, blood trickling from the torn lip.

“Why… Why are you here? Why?”

“Hmm?”

“You should be on the Yangtze river! Why are you here? Why in Qingcheng of all places? Why! Why us! Why! Whyyyyyyyy!”

The beast-like cry rang through Qingcheng. Jang Ilso watched Byeok Hyun-Ja as if amused.

“Why…”

Muttering briefly, he chuckled.

“Who knows. Why might that be?”

“…”

“It’s a bit difficult to answer. There’s not really a reason. I just wanted to. Will that suffice as an answer?”

“Ugh…”

Blood-tinged tears flowed from Byeok Hyun-Ja’s eyes.

Is there any mockery more polite and affectionate than this? That demon showed no mercy even to the dying—only thorough derision.

“Now then, Jang Ilso… Jang Ilsoooooooo!”

“Oh dear, oh dear. You seem very angry. But truly, there’s not much of a reason.”

Jang Ilso stepped a little closer to Byeok Hyun-Ja.

“But if you absolutely must have a reason, there is at least one.”

“…What is that?”

Jang Ilso’s voice sank into Byeok Hyun-Ja’s ear like a knife.

“Because you’re weak.”

“…”

“Because you are the weakest. There’s no need to risk much to trample you. Like the tender nape of a lamb, a bite is enough to easily cut your breath off.”

“You, you bastard…”

“Do you need any other reason than that?”

Blood tears poured from Byeok Hyun-Ja’s eyes. With the flames wavering and even their shadows trembling, his face looked almost demonic.

The taoist teachings Byeok Hyun-Ja had guarded his whole life were meaningless at this moment.

All that remained was consuming hatred and rage.

“Waaaaaaaah!”

Byeok Hyun-Ja swung the sword he held with all his might—toward that demon’s pale throat.

Crack!

But before that, Jang Ilso’s hand pierced Byeok Hyun-Ja’s chest.

Eyes wide, Byeok Hyun-Ja slowly looked down at his chest. Red blood thickly ran down the white wrist embedded in his chest.

Jang Ilso spoke.

“You should have known.”

“…”

“That there is no longer any safe place in this Central Plains.”

Byeok Hyun-Ja’s body trembled like a trembling aspen.

“Of course I like it. Cowards like you. Those who leisurely enjoy the blood from behind while others bleed and fight. And those who show up late to take what was made by someone else’s blood while pretending to grieve—aren’t such people the truest humans?”

He is shattered.

Not only his body. Even his spirit is being utterly shattered at this moment.

“But they’re disgusting.”

“…”

“The complacency of believing the flames will spare only you even as the world burns disgusts me. When I see such people I want to burn them myself.”

Jang Ilso smiled brightly.

“That’s the only reason. How is it? Isn’t that sufficient after hearing it?”

Byeok Hyun-Ja, hands trembling violently, mustered strength to grab the hand of Jang Ilso lodged in his chest.

“W- a revenant…”

“Hmm?”

“Even if I become a revenant… I will curse… you… the curse… the grudge of Qingcheng…”

At those blood-choked words spat out with all his might, the corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth twitched.

“Ha… hahahahah! Hahahahahahahahahaha!”

And then he burst into a mad, roaring laugh.

“Why are the words of those who can’t even accomplish what they spit out always the same! Once you’re dead, it’s all over, isn’t it?”

Crunch!

Jang Ilso’s remaining hand grabbed Byeok Hyun-Ja by the hair.

“If you had the strength to become a revenant and curse, you should have bitten in life! If you wanted something, you should have fought for it, not waited! If you truly wanted it, you should get it!”

Watching Byeok Hyun-Ja’s light-dimming eyes, Jang Ilso twisted his face grotesquely and laughed.

“But now you have no such chance.”

Snap!

Jang Ilso’s hand was ripped out of his chest in one motion. The blood dragged out with it painted coarse strokes across the world like a canvas.

Byeok Hyun-Ja’s body slowly collapsed.

Thud.

Death came, yet he could not bring himself to close his eyes.

Qingcheng, one of the Nine Great Sects and a renowned house of Sichuan. It was an unworthy, utterly humble death unbefitting its Sect leader.

Crunch!

Jang Ilso stomped on Byeok Hyun-Ja’s head, crushing it half to death, then casually shook the blood from his hand.

“Hmm.”

He slowly looked around.

Everything burned.

The mountain, the pavilions, the people.

The mountain that had been verdant when he ascended it was gone. The pavilions had lost their former glory and collapsed, and the ashen mountain would never regain its form.

A most satisfied smile bloomed at the corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth. In the night sky hung a crescent moon that seemed like his smile.

“Isn’t this rather splendid? Huh?”

The hem of his red robe fluttered madly, like a surging flame.

“Hahaha! Hahahahahah! Ahahahahahahahaha!”

A blaze that could burn the world shimmered.

It was a blood-red flame called Jang Ilso.

Chapter 1414: If You Really Want It, You Should Take It. (3)

Startled by a sudden presence, Jang Ilso slowly turned his gaze.

He saw a person approaching in this direction.

It was a face he had never seen before. Of course, Jang Ilso could not possibly remember every grain of sand among the Four Sects Alliance, but at least among those who could approach him so closely, there were no such faces.

Then the intruder’s identity was painfully obvious.

“How are things?”

“The cleanup is mostly finished, Alliance lord.”

Thousand Faced Scholar.

There was only one person in the world bold enough to cross into Jang Ilso’s vicinity with a face he didn’t recognize: the Lower District Sect’s sect leader, the Thousand Faced Scholar.

The Thousand Faced Scholar, whose features were so ordinary they would blur from memory after a glance, silently stared at Jang Ilso’s back.

He was looking at the leader of the Four Sects Alliance, standing against the backdrop of a horribly burning Qingcheng.

‘Hegemony Lord…’

At first, he thought it was remarkable.

Across the long history of the murim world, there had never been a member of the Evil Sect who so easily toyed with the Justice faction.

This could not be described merely as excellence.

Jang Ilso seemed to see something others couldn’t. He admired it, and therefore acknowledged it.

But now, after trampling the Qingcheng — one of the Nine Great sects — so mercilessly, that feeling had completely changed.

“Have you taken care of everyone?”

“…A few rats did escape, but I’ll send a pursuit right now and they’ll be dealt with.”

“A pursuit?”

“Yes. From the Lower District Sect…”

“Stop the pointless gestures.”

When Jang Ilso cut him off indifferently, the Thousand Faced Scholar clamped his mouth shut.

“They’re just rats at best. No need to waste effort on them. Leave them be. They’ll spread the word — how utterly Qingcheng was destroyed.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar found himself nodding without thinking.

Not that he actively agreed; he simply believed Jang Ilso to be more correct than himself.

The Thousand Faced Scholar now realized he could never see what Jang Ilso saw.

“Will you leave the Blood Palace lord as he is?”

When the Thousand Faced Scholar asked, Jang Ilso glanced sideways.

The Blood Palace’s elite were slaughtering Qingcheng Sect’s disciples here, but the Blood Palace lord himself was nowhere to be seen.

Though the archers had been sent in time — so it was hard to say they hadn’t heard Jang Ilso’s orders — the lord’s absence felt strange.

Jang Ilso shrugged.

“At least he did the minimum.”

“You mean that’s enough?”

The Thousand Faced Scholar narrowed his eyes and asked.

“I don’t expect loyalty. He will merely take the reward for what he did — whether that reward is good or bad.”

The corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth lifted. It looked like a gentle smile at first, but the Thousand Faced Scholar caught a faint, chilling undercurrent behind it.

Jang Ilso was not one to waste things. In this situation he wouldn’t needlessly create conflict with the Blood Palace lord. Yes — for the Jang Ilso up to now, that would have been enough.

But the Thousand Faced Scholar knew.

Now Jang Ilso simply paid a bit more attention to the situation.

Jang Ilso never forgot.

Even if he temporarily yielded to the wall of reality, he never forgot that he had yielded. That was why Desolate Gold Elder met a miserable death, and the Black Dragon King, with one arm severed, became Jang Ilso’s dog.

Perhaps the Blood Palace lord’s fate would be little different.

If anyone continued to judge Jang Ilso from this awkward distance, one day Jang Ilso’s claws would sink into their throat.

And what of the Thousand Faced Scholar?

Was he really standing in a safe place now?

Now the Qingcheng was all flames everywhere. Before dawn it would be nothing but ashes.

The Qingcheng sect’s strength was great, and the Thousand Faced Scholar had prepared for massive losses when he climbed the mountain. Attacking the Nine Great Sects warranted that. He had to be ready to pay that price.

But now the Qingcheng sect was trampled beneath Jang Ilso’s feet with such ease.

‘If it had been a normal situation, this would never have happened.’

Attacked at an entirely unforeseen time by an entirely unexpected enemy, there would have been no chance to fight back. Crushing those who hadn’t steeled themselves was easier than twisting the wrist of a scrawny child.

But it’s always easy to judge in hindsight. The truly difficult thing is making that easy-to-judge outcome a reality.

There stood someone who calmly accomplished the impossible. The Thousand Faced Scholar stared at him.

Then Jang Ilso raised an eyebrow and asked.

“Why that look?”

The Thousand Faced Scholar flinched.

Jang Ilso’s gaze looked strange. Whether from the heat of the surrounding flames, or the boiling desire within him, his light-colored eyes shimmered with an indescribable gleam.

It was the eye of an ambitious villain. That gaze pressed down on the Thousand Faced Scholar.

From the moment they first assembled to form the Four Sects Alliance, to now burning Qingcheng, Jang Ilso had seen the world with that same look.

It wasn’t Jang Ilso who had changed. It was the Thousand Faced Scholar looking at him.

“Are you suddenly afraid?”

Jang Ilso sneered as he asked.

“Even if you’re scared, there’s no turning back. Now that the Qingcheng sect has been burned, the only road left is for either them or us to be annihilated.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar snorted a laugh.

He was used to hiding his true face and masking expression and feeling, but this rare laugh sprang clearly from genuine sentiment.

He could only laugh.

“Of course I’m afraid of the Nine Great Sects.”

“How honest.”

“I’m afraid of ruin too. It’s not exactly pleasant that the way to live like a makeshift king in the Southern lands has collapsed.”

Jang Ilso nodded slowly.

He despised the ambitionless, but he didn’t particularly dislike those honest about themselves. Acknowledging oneself was no small thing.

“But what I’m afraid of isn’t them or my own ruin — it’s you, Alliance lord.”

“…Me?”

Jang Ilso looked at him as if amused. His eyes were calm though they gleamed in the flames, but the Thousand Faced Scholar’s gaze trembled unmistakably.

“They say if a person fears someone, the method is obvious.”

“Yes.”

A grotesque smile touched Jang Ilso’s face as he answered.

“You kill them. So they can no longer frighten you.”

Jang Ilso glanced around and said.

“How about it? Conveniently, there’s two of you. You don’t seem without a chance.”

A hollow laugh escaped the Thousand Faced Scholar’s lips.

There might indeed be a chance. He too was the sect leader of the Lower District Sect. He wouldn’t necessarily lose to Jang Ilso. Jang Ilso’s unknown ability was a variable, but ‘unknown’ didn’t automatically mean formidable.

“Not a bad plan. But… I know an even better one.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar slowly lowered one knee. The knee that would never bow touched the ground.

Jang Ilso watched that profoundly symbolic sight with nothing more than an indifferent gaze.

“Sect leader of the Lower District Sect, the Thousand Faced Scholar Dam Yeo-Hae.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar bowed his head deeply, very deeply.

“I pledge my loyalty to Alliance lord.”

A man kneeling amid the roaring flames — it might be a grander sight than a burning the Qingcheng.

Jang Ilso twisted a smile and let out a low hum.

“Hmph. How sudden.”

“…”

“Wasn’t it always this way?”

The Thousand Faced Scholar raised his head. The moment their eyes met, he sensed it.

Those eyes hadn’t changed in the slightest.

What did it mean that even after securing the Thousand Faced Scholar’s loyalty, that unchanging gaze showed no sign of alteration?

And the Thousand Faced Scholar naturally understood what he must do.

His hand slowly moved toward his face. When the trembling hand covered his face and then fell away…

At last a different smile bloomed on Jang Ilso’s lips, unlike any before.

“So that’s the Thousand Faced Scholar’s true face.”

He gave a small nod. Then, as if teasing, he added.

“You didn’t have to show that.”

“It’s only natural to swear loyalty to the Alliance lord.”

Jang Ilso slowly approached the kneeling Thousand Faced Scholar. His red cloak billowed and then settled down.

Chin.

Jang Ilso lightly rested his hand on the Thousand Faced Scholar’s shoulder and inclined his head slightly.

“Listen to me.”

A whisper sounded in the Thousand Faced Scholar’s ear.

“I’m someone who doesn’t much care about intentions.”

“…”

“No one can know true sincerity. So it’s enough to know what one wants. What is it you want?”

The Thousand Faced Scholar’s shoulder trembled slightly.

“…Life.”

“How petty.”

Jang Ilso lightly tapped the Thousand Faced Scholar’s shoulder.

There were no other words.

There was no promise to guarantee the life he desired, nor any praise for kneeling and joining his ranks. He merely touched his shoulder once.

But the Thousand Faced Scholar did not ask for anything else.

Before this man, everything was meaningless. Promises, feelings, even displayed sincerity — all were just cards to be used when needed.

What the Thousand Faced Scholar desired was only mercy and value.

That cold, metal-forged flame should burn him and the Lower District Sect as late as possible.

“Finish it.”

“Yes, Alliance lord.”

Both his manner and tone had changed considerably.

This did not only mean the relationship between the Thousand Faced Scholar and Jang Ilso had changed.

The Four Sects Alliance, formed from five sects from the start — this signaled that the alliance would take a different form. In the blaze ignited using the Qingcheng as an offering.

“Shall we head to Emei?”

“We should.”
