Jang Ilso’s gaze flicked to Byeok Hyyn-Ja’s corpse.

A worthless death. Its only value at most was buying a few units of time.

“News probably hasn’t reached Emei yet. Emei will be easy to take.”

“Right. But we still must hurry.”

A red line of a smile crossed Jang Ilso’s pale face.

“It would be unfair if only the Nine Great Sects suffered. We should make the Heavenly Friends Alliance feel the same. To burn Emei and trample the Tang Family, we’ll need to hurry.”

“Yes!”

The Thousand Faced Scholar bowed deeply and then turned his body.

Watching his receding back intently, Jang Ilso’s face twisted peculiarly.

‘A rat…’

It’s good to be perceptive. But those who lower themselves out of fear of death eventually show their true colors when they no longer think they’re in danger.

The Thousand Faced Scholar’s fate would be decided not by Jang Ilso, but by himself. In any case, he was useful manpower.

“Tsk.”

Clicking his tongue briefly, Jang Ilso cast his gaze toward the distance — not north where Ami was, but east where the Yangtze lay.

“Ga-Myung is going to scold me again.”

He chuckled and then turned his body.

The flames that had scorched the Qingcheng were spreading northward. Their ferocity was such that no one seemed able to stop them.

Chapter 1415: If You Really Want It, You Should Take It. (4)

“…No.”

For a moment, a voice escaped as if in pain.

Everyone’s faces were pale, and Tang Gunak was hardened like iron armor.

Jang Ilso is heading to the Sichuan Tang Family. Is there anyone here who wouldn’t understand what that means?

Something terrible would surely happen.

“W-what should we do?”

A bewildered voice escaped from Jo Gul’s mouth. But there was no one to answer that question.

“No! This isn’t the time for that…!”

“Gul, calm down for now.”

“Calm down? Is this a time for calming down? Don’t you know what it means that Jang Ilso is going to the Tang Family?”

Bloodshot veins stood out in Jo Gul’s eyes.

Even if he had heard that Jang Ilso was heading to Mount Hua, Jo Gul would not have been this distraught. It would have felt like his guts were being cut out, but he could endure that.

The halls left on Mount Hua contained ancestral history, and seeing those halls burn would be horrific… but halls are just halls.

But the Tang Family is different.

The Tang Family is a settlement where those of one bloodline live together. Beyond those here, hundreds of dependents remain there intact. Haven’t they already seen how those people live?

Those who make poisons, those who create anesthetics, and those who will one day become proud warriors of the Tang Family to fight alongside them. All of them remain exactly at the ground Jang Ilso is heading to—the Sichuan Tang Family.

They’re in a situation where all of them might lose their lives at once.

“Let’s go!”

Jo Gul shouted as if clawing at the air.

“What are you all doing? We have to go at once! Let’s go to Sichuan. If we hurry, we might be able to stop it!”

“Gul, I said calm down first!”

“Damn it! This is not the time to be talking about calming down, sahyung!”

Uncharacteristically, Jo Gul glared at Yoon Jong. He even roughly shook off Yoon Jong’s hand that lay on his shoulder.

Instead of getting angry, Yoon Jong let out a heavy sigh.

“It’s already too late. We…”

“What kind of weak talk is that! So because we’re late, should we just watch?”

As Jo Gul glared fiercely, Yoon Jong clamped his mouth shut.

In truth, what Jo Gul was insisting on was nothing more than a desperate outcry. But Yoon Jong couldn’t blame him. How could he not understand that feeling?

“That’s also Tang Soso’s home!”

At those words, everyone’s gaze turned to Tang Soso.

Tang Soso bit her lip and fell silent; her face was pale.

Her usually bright face was now so pitiful that it was painful to look at.

Jo Gul clenched his fist tightly and shouted.

“If no one goes, I’ll go alone!”

“Hey, you bastard! Right now…!”

“I’ll go too.”

At that moment, someone added weight to Jo Gul’s words.

“We can go.”

“Taoist Baek Cheon.”

Namgung Do-Wei looked at Baek Cheon, who spoke with a hoarse voice. Even looking half-dead, Baek Cheon spoke decisively.

“It might not be too late. We must go right now.”

“…That’s absurd. Do you know how far it is from here to Sichuan? And if we try to cross the river hastily now, we’ll be annihilated. The pirate fleet hasn’t fully withdrawn yet.”

Im So-Byeong murmured weakly in opposition. But Baek Cheon’s gaze did not change at all.

“If we can’t cross the river, then we simply won’t cross it.”

“…What?”

Baek Cheon’s gaze turned toward the retreating forces of the Four Sects Alliance.

“Then we’ll break through them, won’t we?”

“…”

“If our goal is to reach Sichuan anyway, there’s no need to cross the river. If we follow the riverbank upstream, we can reach Sichuan.”

“Bae-Baek Cheon.”

In the end, even Hyun Jong’s voice came out, sounding as if trying to dissuade them.

Even now, they want to fight? Even after seeing that tremendous army with their own eyes?

“We must go.”

“First, calm down and…”

“Do I look excited right now, former Sect Leader?”

At that, Hyun Jong fell silent.

Baek Cheon’s words were not wrong. There was no sign of excitement in him at all.

“Those in Sichuan are also our friends. I do not abandon friends just because the situation is difficult.”

At that, everyone fell silent and stared at Baek Cheon.

No matter how many times you think about it, it’s impossible. Even if they left at once and went all out, by the time they reached Sichuan, the Tang Family would be reduced to not even a cornerstone.

And even if they had time, trying to break through the Four Sects Alliance forces to get to Sichuan would incur enormous losses.

Baek Cheon could not be unaware of that. Yet he was shouting that they would do it.

What could anyone say to that?

Even someone as battered as that was still willing to fight.

“…There is no time for discussion. I will break the path for now, so Alliance Lord, please gather the collective will and support our rear.”

“Bae-Baek Cheon, you damn!”

Baek Cheon gripped his sword as if he had nothing more to say.

“Calm down, Acting Sect Leader.”

A voice seized the back of Baek Cheon’s neck. Even Baek Cheon could not ignore those words.

Because the owner of that voice was Tang Gunak.

“Calm your ardor. If you cannot see reality clearly, you will commit an irreparable mistake.”

It was indeed a cold statement, but how could one blame someone for speaking like that about the disaster facing them?

“If the opponent is the Hegemony Lord, then even if we run now, it is already too late.”

Tang Gunak’s face was utterly calm. Baek Cheon cried out urgently.

“But… but shouldn’t we at least try something?!”

“What if that’s what the Hegemony Lord wants?”

For a moment Baek Cheon’s hand trembled slightly. Tang Gunak reproached him in a calm voice.

“Even if your rage is exactly what the Hegemony Lord intends, can you still speak like this now?”

“That is…”

Baek Cheon couldn’t finish his sentence and bit his lip hard.

It’s absurd. How could anyone manipulate even a person’s emotions according to their intent?

But the problem was that the opponent was Jang Ilso. It was ridiculous, but with him, it seemed he could even hold people’s emotions in his hand.

Tang Gunak glanced calmly to the rear and spoke.

“Ho Ga-Myung isn’t coming.”

“…”

“Alliance Leader. First we must join with Shaolin, then cross the river. We don’t know what might happen here.”

“Family Lord…”

A fleeting look of pity passed over Hyun Jong’s face.

“Alliance leader.”

But Tang Gunak didn’t even blink. He spoke as if he could guess what Hyun Jong wanted to say.

“Do not underestimate the Tang Family.”

“…”

“Even if they are not a sect devoted to martial arts, the Tang Family is the Tang Family. The Tang Family is not fearsome because it is strong, but strong because fearsome people gather there.”

Those who heard that shivered slightly.

“Even if they are not warriors, they bear the Tang surname. They will know how to extract themselves; you needn’t worry about that.”

A variety of emotions passed over Hyun Jong’s face.

He understood what Tang Gunak was saying. He also knew that was what had to be done.

But he could not bring himself to nod in agreement.

How could a person be like that? He knew that those words were not entirely sincere.

As Hyun Jong still only moved his lips, Tang Gunak looked at Meng So and spoke.

“Palace Lord.”

“Uh, yes. Speak.”

“Please lead the alliance. Right now, the only person here who can make a proper judgment is you.”

At that, the beastly lord clicked his tongue in a ‘tsk’.

“Are you really okay with that?”

Tang Gunak did not answer, but stared intently at Meng So. Understanding the meaning of that look, Meng So sighed deeply.

Adding words to a warrior who has already steeled himself would be nothing but an insult.

“It’s fine.”

And the answer to Meng So’s question came not from Tang Gunak but from Tang Soso.

“…They’re all capable people; they’ll escape somehow. They will, without fail.”

Tang Pae also nodded.

“The Tang Family’s mechanical defenses and traps cannot be so easily breached. There will be a way.”

Everyone knew it. Even they didn’t truly believe those words.

No matter how much the Sichuan Tang Family relies on poisons and secret devices, without the elite present here, it’s hard to face the force of Four Sects Alliance.

But no one dared point that out.

After a long silence, Meng So spoke.

“First…”

He looked down for a moment. The barren land revealed as the Four Sects Alliance withdrew was being trod by the Northern Faction that had crossed the Yangtze river.

“First… let’s join with them. It would be right to discuss together. Sichuan isn’t the only one in crisis; they will be anxious too.”

Several people nodded.

It’s not only the Heavenly Friends Alliance that has fire at their heels. If anything, the Qingcheng sect and Emei Sect—the Northern Sects that would be the first targets of invasion—would be even more urgent.

“Alliance Leader!”

“…Understood.”

Hyun Jong also nodded. He knew there was no other option now.

“Let’s go.”

Hyun Jong led the way and struck the ground of Nanjing. To that bleak place the Four Sects Alliance had just been treading.

The others of the Heavenly Friends Alliance followed him in unison.

As Jo Gul gritted his teeth and pushed off the ground, Yoon Jong’s small voice came to his ears.

“Gul, are you okay?”

“…What do you mean?”

“…Aren’t your family near the Tang Family?”

Jo Gul closed his mouth.

Right. His family is right in front of the Tang Family gate. The Four Seas Trading Company where his family is.

“You…”

“That’s not what’s important right now.”

Jo Gul cut him off and increased his pace, going ahead. Watching his retreating figure, Yoon Jong bit his lip hard.

‘Jang Ilso!’

Yoon Jong never imagined in his life he’d come to hate someone this much.

‘How far do you intend to go?’

When he turned his gaze, he saw Chung Myung had again lost consciousness.

‘Please…’

He knew.

That asking anything more from Chung Myung in this moment would be cruel to the point of brutality.

But still, Yoon Jong couldn’t help but hope.

Because in a situation like this, the only one who could remain unshaken would be Chung Myung.

Even if he couldn’t swing a sword once, just being conscious would have given many people comfort.

Before Yoon Jong could finish that regretful thought, Hyun Jong met Beop Jeong who had crossed the river.

“…Abbott.”

Beop Jeong, who had just come out of the Yangtze river was soaking wet, looked at Hyun Jong after confirming that the Four Sects Alliance forces had withdrawn far away. Then he asked.

“What do you think?”

Hyun Jong could not bring himself to speak, but forced his mouth open.

“The Hegemony Lord… Sichuan…”

“I’m asking what you think.”

Beop Jeong’s eyes were chillingly cold.

“Are you not asking how it feels to have sacrificed others as offerings to the Four Sects Alliance as the price of that arrogance?!”

Beop Jeong’s furious shout thundered and struck Hyun Jong’s ears.

Chapter 1416: Even If It’s Meaningless. (1)

Hyun Jong bit his lip gently.

Beop Jeong, the Abbott of Shaolin, should be a symbol of mercy, yet the Beop Jeong whom Hyun Jong remembered was mostly wrapped in wrath. Though the figure he showed Hyun Jong wasn’t merely angry, those images were far more vivid in his memory.

Thus, he sometimes rebelled and considered him someone unworthy of association. How many times had his excesses been unleashed against Mount Hua and the Heavenly Friends Alliance?

But this time even Hyun Jong could offer no rebuttal.

“So…”

Beop Jeong ground his teeth and spoke.

“How many times did I not tell you! It’s nothing but self-satisfaction! Your self-satisfaction may drive this murim world into greater chaos! How many times! How many times did I tell you!”

“…”

“What on earth do you intend to do about this! About the situation where those bastards are attacking Sichuan, now that it has become unclaimed land, what are you going to do about it!”

Beop Jeong, unable to contain his fury, bit his lip.

In truth, it wasn’t only Hyun Jong to blame. Beop Jeong was equally responsible for causing all this. If he hadn’t been played like a child by Jang Ilso’s scheme in the first place, there might have been a way to protect Sichuan.

But he failed to see. Blinded by the huge current of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, he didn’t notice at all what Jang Ilso was aiming for. There had been signs several times already.

Had he not missed Jang Ilso’s movements, Sichuan might have been saved on its own, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance might have been preserved as well.

Yet even knowing that, his anger toward the Heavenly Friends Alliance did not abate. After all, the beginning of all this was their expedition to the Southern Island Sect, wasn’t it?

“Abbott.”

Hyun Jong sighed deeply and spoke.

“I know you have reason to be angry, Abbott. But now is not the time to argue right and wrong. We must discuss how to help Sichuan somehow…”

“You say help?”

A voice like an icy blade came from Beop Jeong’s mouth.

“By what means?”

“…”

“The distance from here to Sichuan is no less than 1500 km. By what means do you expect to help a Sichuan more than 1500 km away? Even if you start running like mad now, do you think the Hegemony Lord will just sit idly by?”

Beop Jeong’s voice felt like it pierced the vitals, and Hyun Jong squeezed his eyes shut.

Hyun Jong knew this as well.

In retrospect, everything had already been decided. What Jang Ilso truly aimed for was not their annihilation. It was to cunningly drive Chung Myung’s party and the Southern Island Sect eastward, drawing the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Nine Great Sects to the east.

Whatever happened in Sichuan, they would be pulled to the eastern Central Plains where they could not intervene for days.

“If we join our forces…”

“Get a grip, Alliance lord!”

A thunderous roar burst from Beop Jeong’s mouth.

“Join forces? You mean fight them and go to Sichuan? Fine! Try to break through! And if you do break through, do you think those in Sichuan will hold out until we arrive? Against the Four Sects Alliance?”

“…”

“Do you think you’re the only one who wants to dash to Sichuan to save them? Sichuan houses the Qingcheng sect and Emei Sect! Do you not understand what that means?”

Hyun Jong was momentarily at a loss for words.

Qingcheng and Emei are sects responsible for one pillar of the Nine Great Sects. If they lose their strength, the power of the Nine Great Sects will no longer be what it once was.

No, it wouldn’t stop at just that.

Among the Nine Great Sects, Kunlun has long since become a mere name, and because of the Southern Island Sect disaster this time, Southern Island Sect has also lost much strength.

In such a situation, if Qingcheng and Emei in Sichuan, and perhaps even Dian-cang, are wiped out, the Nine Great Sects might lose over thirty percent of their forces because of this incident.

Thirty percent in one blow. No, if you include the Southern Island Sect, it would be even more. And all within less than a month.

For the Nine Great Sects, it might be a greater loss than the past Demonic Cult calamity. It would be presumptuous to even talk about recovery.

So even Hyun Jong could not blame Beop Jeong for losing his mind and raging half-mad.

“If you had not recklessly gone to the Southern Island Sect, Sichuan would not have been engulfed in this calamity! Do you not understand what I mean? This is the price of what you did. The world will blame you!”

Beop Jeong’s icy gaze fixed beyond Hyun Jong onto Tang Gunak.

Meeting the reproachful stare, Tang Gunak did not flinch and returned the fierce gaze steadily.

“That’s why I told you so many times!”

Beop Jeong ground his teeth and was about to scold again.

“…Did you just call us reckless now?”

Beop Jeong fell silent.

From behind, Geum Yang-Baek, the Sect leader of Southern Island Sect, whose face looked as if his breath might leave him at any moment, stepped forward. He staggered with every step.

Beop Jeong, boiling with rage, felt as if his whole body had been plunged into ice water and instinctively clenched his fist.

“That means…”

A cold, deadly light poured from Geum Yang-Baek’s eyes.

“Are you saying that the actions of those who ran all the way to the Southern Island Sect to save us were reckless and pointless? Say that to my face, Abbott.”

It was the spirit of a man facing death. The final vigor of someone who has accepted death crushes even the righteousness of the world.

“Sect leader, that…”

“Why would it be!”

Yelling fiercely, Geum Yang-Baek took another step toward Beop Jeong as if about to draw his sword.

“Why is that their fault!”

“…”

“If we speak of principles, isn’t it the Abbott’s fault for completely abandoning fellow members of the Nine Great Sects and ignoring the rescue pleas we sent countless times! If you had helped us even once before the situation reached this point, would this calamity have occurred today!”

Beop Jeong fell silent as if his mouth had disappeared.

He could not put forth any logical defense.

How meaningless and cruel it would be to logically explain to the abandoned why they had to be abandoned.

No matter how blinded by rage, he should have been able to exercise that level of discernment.

“Yet! You’re oblivious to what you yourselves did! And you call those who did your duty for you fools? And yet you are the Abbott of Shaolin? And yet you are a Buddhist! You?”

Blood spurted from Geum Yang-Baek’s mouth in his excitement.

“Gasp!”

“Sect leader!”

“Sect leader! You mustn’t get excited, Sect leader!”

“Stand aside!”

Geum Yang-Baek shoved away his disciples who tried to restrain him. A chilling coldness gleamed in his eyes.

“You said the world will condemn them?”

“Sect leader…”

“Yes! Perhaps so. But that won’t be the entire world! Even if everyone condemns and points fingers at them, the Southern Island Sect alone will stand by them! Even if the name of the Southern Island falls and not even ashes remain! Those who bear the Three Waves emblem on their chests will take the incoming stones and the spittle spat by others in their stead!”

A cry that seemed to squeeze out souls rang out.

Beop Jeong involuntarily sighed for a moment. Not only Geum Yang-Baek’s shout, but the venomous gazes sent by the Southern Island Sect disciples standing behind him were truly formidable.

Just by looking at those eyes one could tell: Southern Island and the Nine Great Sects had now crossed an irretrievable river between them.

In a situation where sects belonging to the Nine Great Sects in Sichuan might be annihilated, even Southern Island Sect, which might have been some help, had parted ways with them.

“Heh, hehe…”

Beop Jeong let out a hollow laugh for a moment.

“So it is. All of it is my fault.”

“…”

“Sichuan burns and innocent sects will be drenched in blood, and all of it is my fault! All of it!”

Beop Jeong glared again at Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak, but that gaze could not bear to reach Geum Yang-Baek.

At that moment, Meng So, who had been silently silent, spoke.

“Now is not the time for this. I know there are long-standing emotions between us, but now is not the time. We must first discuss measures to help Sichuan.”

“Y-Yes, Abbott. Now is…”

Jong Ri-Hyung, the Kongtong Sect leader, also supported Meng So. That did not mean he bore no ill will toward the Heavenly Friends Alliance, but now every moment was precious.

But Beop Jeong merely glanced at Meng So with a frigid stare.

“You say measures?”

“…”

“What meaning is there in you and I discussing measures? You have never once listened to what I said! When have you ever accepted a single proposal I made?”

“Abbot. How can you say such things…”

“Am I wrong?”

Faced with that, it was Hyun Jong who found himself at a loss for words.

Of course the circumstances were complex in detail, but looking at the outcome plainly, the Heavenly Friends Alliance had consistently rejected Beop Jeong’s proposals and always prioritized their own will.

So Beop Jeong was not wrong to speak as he did.

“And now? You expect us to assist you?”

“Those in danger include Qingcheng, Emei, and even the Dian-cang sect. Isn’t it the Abbot’s duty to protect them!”

“Yes, that’s right! That’s why I strove to protect them! I even ran here risking my life and crossed the river to try to save even you!”

“…”

“And the only thing you say is that all of it is the fault of me, the Abbot of Shaolin.”

Beop Jeong sneered and looked at the Four Sects Alliance troops who had stopped at a distance.

“It is impossible to break through them. No, it might be possible, but that is what the Hegemony Lord intends. We need not suffer greater losses when huge damage is already inevitable. I will return to the Northern land.”

“Abbot! But Sichuan needs help this very moment…”

“Do you still not understand that if we suffer even greater losses, it will truly be irreparable!”

Boom!

When Beop Jeong stamped his foot, the ground trembled.

With an expression unbefitting a Buddhist, Beop Jeong glared at the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

“If even we fall here, an era of usurpers will come upon the world! Jang Ilso will seize the world and manipulate it as he pleases!”

Everyone’s faces darkened instantly.

“No matter how the Heavenly Friends Alliance talks about principle or consultation, in the end you run to where the incident happened and just swing swords! Do you truly not expect Jang Ilso to move you like that?”

“…”

“In this situation it’s a meaningless talk, but still heed me. This is my final advice. Do not go to Sichuan. Unless you all wish to die there!”

Beop Jeong turned on his heel and left.

Behind him, the disciples of the Nine Great Sects who had been watching the Heavenly Friends Alliance also stared at them briefly before turning away.

For a moment, Hyun Jong realized.

‘The disciples…’

The Nine Great Sects’ leadership may have looked on the Heavenly Friends Alliance unfavorably until now, but those disciples had felt quite favorably toward them.

But not anymore.

The looks they showed as they turned were no longer those of allies.

The wall erected between the Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance had now become too strong to tear down.

Then Beop Jeong, who had turned away, spoke softly with a sneer.

“Nevertheless… of course you’ll go to Sichuan, won’t you?”

The person who answered was not Hyun Jong.

“We will.”

Baek Cheon answered in a calm voice without the slightest tremor. Beop Jeong glared at him with a cold face, then turned his head sharply as if a chill wind passed.

“Yes. You will.”

Watching Beop Jeong walk away, Hyun Jong squeezed his eyes shut.

‘Jang Ilso…’

He felt as if he could hear Jang Ilso’s laugh in his ear.

The manic laughter of that audacious scoundrel who had achieved everything as he wished.

Chapter 1417: Even If It’s Meaningless. (2)

Beop Jeong, who had turned and was walking away, bit his lip hard.

The vast Yangtze River spread before his eyes, but even that could not unclog the tightness in his chest.

“Beop Gye!”

“Yes! Abbot!”

“Send word to the Beggars’ Union and have them get this news to Sichuan by any means! Use whatever method you must!”

“A-Abbot… But it’s already…”

“I know that too!”

“…”

Startled by Beop Jeong’s outburst, Beop Gye closed his mouth with a pale face. Beop Jeong glared at him, took a deep breath, and spoke again.

“Qingcheng and Emei may already be too late, but if we hurry the news, Dian-cang might be able to withdraw. Now is the time to clutch at even a straw!”

“Ah, understood! Abbot!”

Beop Gye nodded vigorously.

As he hurried to prepare a dispatch to the Beggars’ Union, Jong Ri-Hyung quietly approached Beop Jeong’s side.

“Abbot…”

Beop Jeong turned to Jong Ri-Hyung with cold eyes. Startled, Jong Ri-Hyung mustered his courage and spoke again.

“Are you truly thinking of not going to Sichuan?”

“…”

“I know, Abbot. I know it’s likely too late even if we go. But as you just said…”

“You mean that if we rush there, there might still be a way to save them.”

“Y-Yes, that’s what I mean. No matter that the enemy is the Hegemony Lord or the Four Sects Alliance, aren’t they Qingcheng and Emei? They wouldn’t be defeated so easily.”

A sigh escaped Beop Jeong’s lips.

Despite suffering so much, Jong Ri-Hyung still couldn’t shake the bad habit of underestimating the Evil Sect.

Even after all that had happened.

Indeed, Qingcheng and Emei carry lofty names. But the power of the Hegemony Lord they face is no longer comparable. At best only a few survivors might remain. Even if they gather them, what would truly change?

“Sect leader.”

“Yes. Abbot.”

“It’s not that we won’t go — it’s that we cannot go.”

“What? What do you mean?”

Jong Ri-Hyung asked back with a blank face.

“Where do you think the Four Sects Alliance forces who withdrew from here will head?”

“W-Well. Of course they’ll head to Sichuan, won’t they?”

“What if they don’t?”

“Eh?”

“What will you do if they are not heading to Sichuan?”

A look of puzzlement appeared in Jong Ri-Hyung’s eyes. He couldn’t immediately grasp what Beop Jeong was saying.

Seeing Jong Ri-Hyung’s reaction, Beop Jeong shook his head and continued.

“They have withdrawn from Nanjing, but they haven’t headed to Sichuan yet. The Pirates’ fleet is the same. They’ve retreated from here, but they still occupy the Yangtze River.”

“…Then?”

“Yes.”

Beop Jeong nodded gravely.

“If we rashly head to Sichuan, they will surely push up into Henan.”

“T-That…”

Jong Ri-Hyung’s gaze turned toward the Four Sects Alliance army holding a distance.

Indeed, as Beop Jeong said, their pace of retreat had noticeably slowed, ready to change direction at any time.

“E-Even without Jang Ilso here?”

It was Peng Yeop, not Beop Jeong, who answered that question.

“There is no Hegemony Lord present, but there remain the remnants of the Ten Thousand People Clan who had headed for Southern Island with Black Ghost Fort, and the minor sects are here.”

“…”

“And with that level of force alone, sweeping away Henan — now a vacant territory — wouldn’t be particularly difficult.”

Jong Ri-Hyung stared at Peng Yeop as if his mind had gone blank.

“T-Then…”

He murmured as if he finally understood the situation.

“You mean that even though we plainly know they’re trampling Sichuan, we cannot withdraw from here?”

“…”

“W-What kind of situation has it come to? Really…”

It felt like sinking into an endless quagmire.

The trap Jang Ilso had set would not let them go easily. The cost of stepping into the snare he laid down was immense. Once a foot sank into the mire, no amount of struggle only buried it deeper.

“Amitabha.”

Beop Jeong muttered the prayer softly. Unbearable anger welled up on his face.

All of this was caused by the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s premature move. Yet the ones suffering for that foolishness were none other than the orthodox sects.

Qingcheng and Emei’s annihilation seemed certain, and even Dian-cang had become endangered.

But the damage to the Heavenly Friends Alliance would at most mean the Tang Family — missing its main forces — getting trampled, right?

Of course, given the Tang Family’s reliance on poisons and secret techniques for more than seventy percent of its power, having their clan trampled is no small matter, but one sect’s damage cannot be compared to the annihilation of three sects.

What tortured Beop Jeong most was that he could do nothing but watch those three sects be wiped out.

‘In the end.’

A hollow laugh slipped from Beop Jeong’s mouth.

‘We, despite having done nothing wrong, must lose, and they, having done what they shouldn’t, gained Southern Island.’

How on earth was he supposed to accept this absurd situation? Since they ultimately benefited, should he say they were right?

“Then…”

Jong Ri-Hyung asked with a pale face.

“A-Are you saying there’s nothing we can do? Abbot?”

Hearing that, Beop Jeong turned his head and looked at the Four Sects Alliance army.

After watching them for a long time, his gaze finally shifted to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Things that could be done.

What they could do…

“There are things we can do.”

Beop Jeong’s eyes sank low.

At that moment, everyone fell silent. The aura emanating from Beop Jeong made them hold their breath. After staring impassively at the Heavenly Friends Alliance for a while, Beop Jeong finally opened his mouth slowly.

“What we… can do.”

Hyun Jong, who had been silently watching the departing Justice faction, turned his head weakly.

‘It hurts.’

The words were like a blade, honed to wound. What made Hyun Jong even more despondent was that there was nothing particularly untrue about them.

All of this might ultimately have been caused by their arrogance.

Therefore, he could not blame them for not helping. He could only clutch his burning chest.

“Don’t make that face, Alliance Leader.”

Hyun Jong turned toward the voice. Southern Island’s sect leader, Geum Yang-Baek, was looking at him with a deep gaze.

“Sect leader…”

“If it weren’t for the Heavenly Friends Alliance, if no one had rushed to Southern Island’s aid — not a single Southern Island disciple present here would have survived.”

Geum Yang-Baek looked at his disciples and shook his head.

“And yet if you, Alliance Leader, blame yourself, won’t we become those who survived for nothing?”

“That… was not my intention. I’m sorry, Sect leader.”

“No. It’s just…”

Geum Yang-Baek gathered his strength and spoke again.

“Even if the outcome didn’t go as desired, there is not a single person in the world who would say the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s intent was wrong.”

“…”

“And now is not the time for self-blame. Shouldn’t we help Sichuan?”

Hyun Jong looked at Geum Yang-Baek in astonishment.

His life was already on the edge; even those unversed in martial arts could see it. And yet Geum Yang-Baek spoke not of his own condition but of Sichuan.

“Sect leader. First, your health…”

“No.”

Geum Yang-Baek shook his head.

“I’ve already lived long enough. What’s important now is not me, but Sichuan.”

“No. Even so…”

“Because I know.”

Geum Yang-Baek looked at Hyun Jong with determined eyes.

“Because I know all too well how those in a desperate position with no one to help feel. The Heavenly Friends Alliance reached out to them. But now, isn’t Sichuan being left without aid?”

“…”

“Go, Alliance Leader. We will help as well. Even if it’s only a meaningless struggle, isn’t there someone who should flail on behalf of another’s death?”

Hyun Jong’s head eventually nodded.

He knew.

No matter the situation, what they must do was set. Even if meaningless, they could not refrain from rushing to Sichuan.

Hyun Jong steadied himself and turned to look at the heads of the other sects.

“Now that the Justice factions have stepped out, we must find some way to reach Sichuan even on our own. How…”

“Impossible.”

At that moment, Im So-Byeong sharply cut off Hyun Jong’s words.

“Green Forest King.”

“I understand your intention, but there is no way. Unless the Justice faction sects fight alongside us, it’s difficult for us alone to break through that defensive line. Even if we claw our way through, we’ll be attacked all along the route to Sichuan and suffer losses, and the moment we reach Sichuan we’ll be overwhelmed by joint attacks from all sides and be annihilated.”

A cold statement, but a starkly realistic one. To those who had already suffered the same fate to arrive here, it sounded all the more chilling.

“T-Then can’t we just cross the river?”

At Namgung Do-Wei’s suggestion, Im So-Byeong’s eyes turned cold.

“Do you think they have completely withdrawn?”

“…”

At that, Namgung Do-Wei turned to look at the Four Sects Alliance. Those retreating had already halted, keeping a distance from which they could charge again at any moment.

“Ho Ga-Myung has probably already joined them over there. He’ll try to buy as much time as possible. If we attempt a reckless crossing, they’ll certainly charge again. Then we’ll be attacked from the rear.”

Seeing the Pirates’ fleet still arrayed on the Yangtze River, those present bit their lips.

“Then…”

“We cannot move until they withdraw.”

“There must be some method! Are you saying we should just stand by and watch like this?”

Namgung Do-Wei shouted in frustration, but Im So-Byeong didn’t bat an eye.

“They cannot initiate the attack either. And if the Shaolin Abbott calls in support including the Beggars’ Union, they’ll realize the disadvantage and withdraw. At most a day…”

“But that’s too late!”

Bang!

Jo Gul stamped the ground and emitted a powerful ki-wave in every direction. But Im So-Byeong merely looked at everyone with a calm face.

“Reality is reality…”

“Not everyone needs to go.”

At the voice that cut him off, Im So-Byeong turned his head. Standing there with a pale face was Baek Cheon.

“Not everyone has to go. Nor can they.”

“Taoist.”

“Cover the rear. I will cross the river. I’ll lead those with swift feet to Sichuan.”

Im So-Byeong stared at Baek Cheon in disbelief.

With a face as if he might enter a coffin at any moment, what ridiculous talk was this?

“Is that even possible?”

Im So-Byeong glared at Baek Cheon, barely restraining his anger. How far would this man go before he was satisfied?

“Really!”

“Whether it’s possible or not.”

“…”

Baek Cheon looked at Im So-Byeong with a firm expression.

“That’s the only thing I need you to say, Green Forest King.”

“…”

A few people approached and stood behind Baek Cheon, their faces saying that no matter how much anyone protested, it would be useless.

The fact that those standing behind them were the most severely wounded among them made Im So-Byeong sick to his teeth.

“…Impossible.”

“Green Forest King!”

“Sichuan is not Southern Island. A few of you alone can do nothing. If you go, you’ll only die pointlessly!”

“We must at least try…”

“Try or not, if it’s impossible, it’s impossible!”

“…”

Just as Baek Cheon bit his lip until it bled,

“Amitabha.”

A prayer came from not far away. The tone was clearly not Hae Yeon’s voice, and everyone turned in surprise.

Beop Gye.

The Shaolin second-in-command had quietly approached them. The heated conversation had been so intense they hadn’t noticed him.

“Ah, no?”

“Amitabha. I bring the Abbot’s message.”

“A message?”

Everyone stared at Beop Gye in blank astonishment. What more could Beop Jeong possibly have to say to them now?

“If you truly intend to go to Sichuan.”

“…Yes?”

Beop Gye said sharply, his eyes deep.

“We, the Justice faction, will use full strength to hold off the Four Sects Alliance remnants here. You select your elites and cross the river.”

Everyone’s eyes widened.

“W-What did you just say…”

“We will firmly guard your rear. However…”

A sonorous voice flowed from Beop Gye’s parted lips.

“Even if Qingcheng and Emei are already lost, you must at least definitely save Dian-cang.”

“…”

“Can you do that?”

Hyun Jong’s face stiffened.

Chapter 1418: Even If It’s Meaningless. (3)

“Did you say Dian-cang?”

“Yes.”

Hyun Jong stared intently at Beop Gye, as if trying to gauge the truth.

‘What intention is this?’

It wasn’t that Beop Gye’s request was unreasonable.

If there were people suffering under the Four Sects Alliance’s oppression, the Heavenly Friends Alliance would do its utmost to save them. Beop Jeong surely knew that, so Hyun Jong couldn’t immediately guess why he singled out “Dian-cang.”

“The Abbot…”

“I am only conveying the Abbot’s words. If the Heavenly Friends Alliance will do that, we will risk our lives to open the way.”

Before Hyun Jong could reply, Beop Gye added with a slightly stern face.

“However, if too many fighters leave this place at once, it becomes dangerous. You understand that, don’t you?”

Hyun Jong nodded slowly.

If, as Beop Gye said, everyone here suddenly left, Ho Ga-Myung would naturally lead those remaining and target the survivors.

To prevent that danger, as many warriors as possible must remain here.

“At most, the number that can leave will be two sects.”

Hyun Jong flinched at Beop Gye’s words.

“That’s all we can do.”

“…Two sects, huh.”

“What do you think?”

Hyun Jong clenched his fist hidden inside his sleeve.

In truth, considering the enemies they faced, “only” two sects seemed little. But if they could join forces with those in Sichuan, there was still some hope.

Putting everything else aside, Hyun Jong was bothered by their motive. Why would Beop Jeong offer such favor now?

At that moment, Beop Gye spoke.

“Do not doubt the Abbot’s intentions too much.”

“…Elder.”

Beop Gye quietly shook his head.

“I understand the Alliance Leader’s suspicion of the Abbot. But this crisis is not solely the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s affair. We too must save those in Sichuan by any means.”

Beop Gye laughed bitterly.

He found it quietly absurd as he spoke. Since when had Shaolin divided those who must be saved and those who could be left?

Sensing that something was wrong, Beop Gye tried to turn away from that feeling.

“We cannot move directly, so we ask to borrow the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s strength. What will you do?”

Hyun Jong closed his eyes tightly.

He couldn’t know. Beop Gye said this, but Hyun Jong didn’t believe Beop Jeong helped without motive. People don’t change that easily.

But even if there was another motive, could he pass up this opportunity?

Hyun Jong opened his eyes and looked at his disciples; their gazes all said the same thing.

Then Hyun Jong calmly said to Beop Gye.

“We will go.”

“I thought you would. Which sects will you bring?”

“One sect will, naturally, be the Tang Family.”

It was practically obvious. To tell the current Tang Family they cannot go to Sichuan would be cruel. Even if they arrived late and only saw the burned ruins, the Tang Family should be the first to confirm those remains with their own eyes.

“Then the other sect… no, there’s no need to ask. I was foolish.”

Beop Gye shook his head.

Although the Heavenly Friends Alliance is united, only one sect always rushes first to the most urgent place.

“Of course, Mount Hua.”

“Yes.”

Beop Gye nodded.

“I will inform the Abbott. With that, I will take my leave.”

Beop Gye bowed in the Shaolin manner and turned to leave, or attempted to. Pausing as if something troubled him, he looked deeply at Hyun Jong and said.

“…Sect leader.”

“Speak.”

“Not as a Shaolin disciple but as a fellow Buddhist, I ask: if there are others suffering there besides Dian-cang… please help them.”

Hyun Jong looked quietly back at him.

This man crossed the river by his own will to help the Heavenly Friends Alliance, to prevent the murim world from falling into ruin. Could there be any ulterior motive in that heart?

The relationship between the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Justice factions had long since frayed, but with people like this, perhaps it could one day be mended.

“Of course we will.”

When Hyun Jong clasped hands, Beop Gye bowed deeply again and turned.

A deep emotion settled in Beop Gye’s eyes as he turned away.

‘One cannot know.’

He did not view the Heavenly Friends Alliance or Mount Hua with blind favor, not because he followed Beop Jeong, but because he believed the Alliance’s extremism and excessive zeal could ruin things.

But in the moment he sent Mount Hua to Sichuan, Beop Gye realized something.

When someone was in danger, the sect one most wanted at that place was none other than Mount Hua, even Beop Gye felt that way.

“Amitabha. If you move first, we will respond.”

As Beop Gye dispersed his light footwork and began to leave, Im So-Byeong’s sharp voice rang out.

“Do you really think the Shaolin Abbot will send us out of pure kindness?”

“…Green Forest King.”

“If our Heavenly Friends Alliance can create a situation where we go to Sichuan and rescue Dian-cang, Shaolin loses nothing. They won’t have blood on their hands but will get the credit; it’s a profitable deal for them either way.”

Im So-Byeong glanced toward Shaolin as he continued.

“And even if we fail to rescue Dian-cang, he can claim he did everything he could. And above all!”

As Im So-Byeong’s voice grew heated, Hyun Jong calmly interrupted.

“You mean it would be perfect if Mount Hua and the Tang Family fight the Hegemony Lord and counterattack together, correct?”

Im So-Byeong fell silent for a moment with a grim face, then asked.

“Did you know?”

“I am not that foolish. I may not know the plots, but I do not misunderstand people’s hearts. However…”

Hyun Jong cast a sidelong glance at Tang Gunak. Though he showed no emotion, clearly his insides still burned.

“I have never heard of a parent calculating losses when saving their child.”

At that moment Im So-Byeong saw Tang Gunak clench his pale fist even tighter.

“And Mount Hua… The Heavenly Friends Alliance is not a place to weigh gains and losses when helping friends.”

All of Mount Hua’s disciples nodded.

“What will you do? Will you still block the kids?”

Im So-Byeong shrugged at Hyun Jong’s question and looked to one side.

“If that damned man were dead, maybe. But he’s still alive; how could I stop the Alliance Leader? He’ll be rioting to slaughter them the moment he opens his eyes.”

“…”

“Understand that I must cause trouble. It’s my role. By the way, aren’t you taking me with you? I could be useful.”

Hyun Jong shook his head at Im So-Byeong’s banter.

“This place is equally dangerous. Without you, wouldn’t it be hard to handle them?”

Im So-Byeong nodded silently. He had to observe Ho Ga-Myung’s actions here since they did not know what he would do.

Then, with a suddenly serious expression, Im So-Byeong spoke in a low voice.

“If it’s dangerous, you must withdraw by any means necessary.”

“I will remember.”

Hyun Jong turned his head and looked at everyone.

“Palace Lord.”

“Yes, Alliance leader.”

“Please take care of this place.”

Meng So let out a heavy sigh ill-fitting his size, as if receiving a heavy burden.

“Yes. Do not worry. We will cross the river without sacrifice. As soon as we cross, we will head to Sichuan, so consider us going ahead.”

“Thank you.”

Hyun Jong turned to look at Geum Yang-Baek, but before he could speak, Geum Yang-Baek opened his mouth first.

“I will guard this place.”

“…Sect leader.”

Geum Yang-Baek’s face was extremely pale, but the smile at his mouth was gentle.

“I regret we may not meet again. Please take care of Southern Islands, Alliance leader.”

Hyun Jong closed his eyes and nodded.

There were so many things he wanted and needed to say, but there was no time.

At this very moment, Sichuan would be drenched in blood.

Even if it might be futile, the struggle did not matter.

‘We are the ones who make those futile struggles.’

Hyun Jong cast a determined look at Tang Gunak.

“Tang Family lord, let us go. Time is of the essence.”

Tang Gunak nodded. Only then did the Tang Family’s followers, who had been desperately suppressing their emotions, let out low, growling moans.

“Mount Hua!”

“Yes, Former Sect Leader!”

“Hurry! We go to Sichuan!”

“Yes!”

Hyun Jong glanced at the status of the Five Swords, squeezed his eyes shut, and spun his body.

“Move out!”

And they ran straight for the Yangtze river.

After them, core figures of Mount Hua, the Tang Family, and the Heavenly Friends Alliance dashed forward emitting blue killing intent.

“Commander! They are moving!”

The report flew into his ear. Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes coldly stared ahead. He saw familiar figures heading for the Yangtze.

“The pirates are now…”

“Leave them be.”

“Yes?”

The startled aide looked at Ho Ga-Myung. A look of disbelief flitted across the aide’s face, but Ho Ga-Myung shook his head to prove the aide’s ears were not deceiving him.

“There is no need to stop them.”

“C-commander…”

Without further explanation, Ho Ga-Myung simply watched Mount Hua and the Tang Family leap into the Yangtze, and the Justice faction moving as if to guard them.

“…Beop Jeong, huh.”

Ho Ga-Myung murmured softly.

What he had to do was to hold onto as many people here as possible without suffering losses. If Beop Jeong’s and Ho Ga-Myung’s interests aligned, there was no need to refuse.

If by sending Mount Hua and the Tang Family they could secure a truce, it would not be a loss. On the contrary…

“What a ruthless man. In a situation like this…”

A rare smile appeared on Ho Ga-Myung’s lips.

What meaning is there in dividing Justice and Evil factions?

In the end, only the skin they wear differs; their deeds are the same. As Jang Ilso said, human nature is uniformly ugly.

Chung Myung still had not regained consciousness and lay in Tang Gunak’s arms. Watching this, a complex emotion passed through Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes.

‘Plum Blossom Sword Ghost.’

In the end, Chung Myung slipped from his grasp like this.

From Southern Island to here, he had let the enemy he vowed to kill slip away.

But Ho Ga-Myung’s heart was not entirely pained.

Because it was not a failure.

If he had not pushed the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost to the limit, if that man had been conscious, the situation would never have come this far.

He did not want to admit it, but the author was Jang Ilso’s only true rival in the world. He would have noticed the dissonance at some point.

So he had to judge that his sacrifice, and the Ten Thousand People Clan’s sacrifice, had value.

Still, he felt regret.

Grit.

Ho Ga-Myung ground his teeth softly.

If the hell they were breaking through led to Sichuan, Ho Ga-Myung would never see that man again.

No matter how much the Plum Blossom Sword Ghost ascended to the heavens, the ghost-blossom conflagration Jang Ilso unleashed would surely cover even that sky.

Chapter 1419: Even If It’s Meaningless. (4)

Emei Sect.

“You villains of the Evil Sect!”

“Do not belittle the Emei!”

The shorn-headed bhikkhunis in monastic robes swung their sharp swords fiercely.

But the members of the Evil Sect, having already tasted blood, lunged like starving wolves.

A grotesque weapon from the Blood Palace Sect, wrapped in a cloak of red blood, flew from behind and pierced one bhikkhuni after another.

“Aaaaah!”

“Master!”

“Th-this fiendish…”

Despite blood gushing from her mouth, the bhikkhuni raised her arm to swing her sword. In that moment, the weapon that had pierced her twisted first and shredded her entrails.

Splat!

Another life expired in a fountain of blood.

Tears of anguish poured from the eyes of Emei Sect bhikkhuni Ok Dang-Hyang.

‘Master!’

The body of Geum Seo, the master she had served all her life, was torn apart by the blade of the enemy.

‘Why…’

Why did this happen?

They had only tried to live following the duties of the faithful at the foot of Emei Mountain; why must they endure such a brutal assault?

She had never once hated or opposed anyone in her life; she had lived carefully to avoid even taking the life of the smallest creature.

That was the Buddha’s teaching and the life a bhikkhuni must uphold.

What are those who so readily snatch others’ lives and mock life and death? How will they bear all this karmic retribution?

“You scoundrels!”

Kraaaang!

With a thunderous command, Emei Sect’s Sect leader Gwan Hae slammed her staff hard into the ground.

A formidable internal qi spread instantly in all directions. At once, everyone’s gaze focused on Gwan Hae.

Grit!

Gwan Hae’s face, which had drawn everyone’s attention, was contorted fiercely like a heavenly guardian.

It was almost unbelievable that such power flowed from an old woman wrinkled with age; she cried out with a force that seemed to split the sky.

“Are you not afraid of heavenly punishment!”

Even the Blood Palace Sect and the Lower District Sect, who had been rampaging without fear, faltered for a moment and eyed her.

Sensing the change, the Red Dogs licked their lips and slowly began to tighten their encirclement at the appearance of a formidable opponent.

At that moment, a voice suddenly drifted in.

“Sect leader Gwan Hae herself intervenes.”

Step. Step.

A man walked slowly toward Gwan Hae’s front.

“What an honor.”

“Who are you?”

Gwan Hae frowned slightly and asked coldly.

In the martial world, age doesn’t necessarily reflect status or ability, but the man who stepped forward still looked far too young.

He looked hardly the sort who should dare stand across from Gwan Hae in such a situation.

Yet the man standing opposite remained completely composed.

“I doubt you would take kindly to me speaking.”

“You are not Jang Ilso! Summon Jang Ilso at once! I will hold him accountable for this slaughter!”

The young man who had stepped forward snorted at Gwan Hae’s exclamation.

“Jang Ilso…”

A dark gleam flashed sharply in the man’s eyes.

“The old bhikkhuni must be so buried in the mountains she can’t grasp the situation… The Alliance lord is not someone you could speak to privately.”

“How dare you!”

“I am more than sufficient to face Emei Sect’s Sect leader Gwan Hae.”

Gwan Hae, about to shout another command, suddenly fell silent; she sensed that the pale young man’s presence was not ordinary.

Though his face was extremely young, the aura he emitted was not something that could come from a youth. Wrath shadowed Gwan Hae’s face.

“…You! You are the Thousand Faced Scholar!”

“Ho.”

A strange smile crossed the mouth of the Thousand Faced Scholar, who wore the young man’s face.

“Of all the world, Sect leader Gwan Hae knows even this fool’s sobriquet and I am moved…”

“You cur!”

Kwang!

Gwan Hae slammed her staff down again as if to drive the enemy away.

“What on earth are you trying to do! Why do this! What do you gain from this? How can you bear so many sins from these fleeting lives…”

“Ah.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar waved his hand as if weary of the conversation.

“There are only filthy creatures here unfit to accept your exalted teachings, so spare me the needless words.”

“You…”

“But one thing does bother me. You asked what we would gain?”

A chilling, tutting laugh escaped, at odds with the youthful face.

“For you, the murim world has been nothing but pleasure; no one dared covet what you had.”

“…”

“But all we obtained were the crumbs you dropped while eating. You call it peace when the world is like this.”

“So you plan to spill such innocent blood!”

The Thousand Faced Scholar shook his head.

“Sect leader. You have enjoyed enough; you may fear about the next life and hell, but that doesn’t matter to us. What matters is what we can gain right now.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar’s hands slowly lowered.

“That’s enough talk. The one behind me is not as patient as he appears. If I delay further, I might lose my head.”

Gwan Hae’s hand trembled as she gripped her divine staff.

She was not afraid of the Thousand Faced Scholar; she could handle him alone.

But the Evil Sect members lined up far behind the Thousand Faced Scholar could never be handled by the Emei disciples alone.

They had to face countless such people unprepared; how could they possibly cope?

“What exactly did we ever do to you…”

“Let’s drop the pathetic talk.”

The Thousand Faced Scholar approached Gwan Hae leisurely.

“Until now you were stronger, so we bore it in silence. Now we are stronger, so you die. Isn’t that enough for the martial world?”

Gwan Hae squeezed her eyes shut.

‘In my generation, Emei will meet its end.’

There was no way out. If one death could make it end, she would give it, but those blood-starved jackals would never be satisfied with Gwan Hae’s life alone.

‘If I had known…’

Regrets surged, but it was all pointless now.

“Geum Hwa.”

“Yes, Sect leader.”

“Take the children and escape.”

“But, Sect leader! That…”

“This is not the time for arguing!”

Gwan Hae’s command was unyielding. Geum Hwa bit her lip and nodded.

“I will obey.”

“Go! Quickly!”

“Yes!”

As Geum Hwa ran off, Gwan Hae let out a deep sigh.

‘Is this also the Buddha’s will?’

Everything is merely karma.

If Emei is paying for karma, then the sins they committed are returning to them.

The sins Gwan Hae had committed were failing to properly look after those suffering and groaning in the world.

And the sins of Emei are…

A bitter smile passed Gwan Hae’s lips.

‘I cannot even resent them.’

It was the sin of turning away those who long ago shed blood fighting for the world.

‘If this is karma, I must accept it. But…’

How can newly initiated young disciples shoulder that sin? It is too cruel.

“ Merciful Bodhisattva…“

If Gwan Eum extends mercy to save sentient beings, she would surely reach out to Emei’s young disciples.

Gwan Hae could only cling to that belief.

“Come then, fiends of the Evil Sect. Emei will never fall so easily.”

“Old bhikkhunis have nothing but mouths left! Strike them!”

The Thousand Faced Scholar produced hundreds of warrior-forms and surged toward Gwan Hae.

At the same time, the Lower District Sect and the Blood Palace Sect renewed their momentum and began to press the Emei bhikkhunis.

“Alliance lord!”

One person prostrated behind Jang Ilso, who was calmly surveying the Emei.

“Emei will be dealt with within the hour.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso tilted his head slightly as if puzzled.

“How easy.”

“…”

“It was simpler than I thought. It’s almost humiliating. I’ve been bowing even to creatures like these all this time.”

Jang Ilso chuckled.

It was absurd.

It was true they had prepared thoroughly, but even so, the Emei sect was not supposed to collapse so easily.

By his calculations it should have taken longer, and the Four Sects Alliance should have suffered greater losses.

But when the lid was lifted, the battle became one-sided to the point of anticlimax.

It meant the power of the Emei sect did not live up to its name at all.

While the Evil Sects fought and tore each other apart countless times, those in Sichuan lived the world leisurely as if in celestial play—like sheep who never feared wolves.

“My stomach turns in disgust.”

Jang Ilso smiled quietly, his expression twisted and chilling like that of a fiend.

The world is not fair.

Some are born into great houses and believe that a splinter in their finger is the worst thing in the world.

Others are born in filthy dung-pits and must drive a knife into a person’s throat to survive one meal.

Not that he necessarily resented that fact, but…

“Well, whatever. It doesn’t matter.”

Jang Ilso murmured quietly and then asked.

“And Thousand Faced Scholar?”

“He is facing Sect leader Gwan Hae. The Red Dogs are supporting him, so he’ll soon have the old woman’s head.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso nodded and gave an order.

“Go tell the Thousand Faced Scholar.”

“Yes!”

“Once Emei is dealt with, we will split our forces in two. Tell the Thousand Faced Scholar to lead the Blood Palace and the Lower District Sect to clear the garrison houses and return.”

“The Lower District Sect and the Blood Palace… Both of them?”

“Yes. Is there a problem?”

At Jang Ilso’s question, the man prostrated and bowed his head deeper in haste.

“Ah, no! I will convey it.”

“Good, good. That will do.”

Jang Ilso smiled gently.

“And tell our men to prepare separately.”

“…Where to?”

“We’re going to collect payment for the liquor.”

“…Yes?”

Jang Ilso’s gaze turned north.

“The liquor we gave the Poison King turned out to be more expensive than expected.”

If he had wanted, he could have passed through Qingcheng and dealt with the Tang Family on his way down.

But it was a priority to strike Emei, whose strength was still intact, rather than the Tang Family, which had lost its main force. If news of Qingcheng reached them and they prepared, a surprise attack would be less effective.

Now that Emei was somewhat dealt with, Jang Ilso finally had leeway.

“Tang Gunak, Tang Gunak…”

A predatory gleam darkened Jang Ilso’s eyes as he murmured softly.

He could still see Tang Gunak’s figure in his mind—the one who had come alone as the Ten Thousand People Clan and threatened him before turning away.

“I’ve always been curious.”

“…”

“Whether the Poison King’s expression will stay unmoved even when the Tang Family’s heirs are all dead and the line is gone. Whether he could still spout those nauseating words about friendship then.”

The man who had prostrated trembled violently.

“I just want to confirm it this time. Hahahah. Ahahahahah! Ahahahahahahahaha!”

A licentious laugh rang out, as if celebrating the fallen renown of the Emei sect—so loud it made one want to cover their ears.

Chapter 1420: Even If It’s Meaningless. (5)

“…What did you just say?”

“I-I report again!”

The man bowing cried out with a deathly pale face.

“Currently Jang Ilso, leader of the Four Sects Alliance, has brought the Alliance’s full force and burned Qingcheng and Emei! Their next target seems to be the Tang Family!”

“Jang Ilso?”

The color drained from Tang Sang-Su’s face, the general of the Sichuan Tang Family.

“T-the Hegemony Lord—why would he suddenly appear in Sichuan?!”

No good answer came to that furious question. Everyone knew it wasn’t really a question.

“W-where are they now?”

“According to the information from the Beggars’ Union, they appear to be on Emei Mountain right now! We’ve sent scouts, so precise information will arrive soon!”

Tang Sang-Su suddenly felt his legs give out but forced himself to stand.

‘What should I do?’

He was the general of the Sichuan Tang Family. With the Lord and the Young lord absent, it was his duty to protect the retainers and the family’s assets left here.

But with things like this, the burden felt unbearably heavy.

The Four Sects Alliance, and Jang Ilso. They were not opponents a mere chief steward could dare face.

“Has there been no contact from the Lord? Didn’t the Beggars’ Union bring any words from him?”

“Th-there was nothing like that…”

The tips of Tang Sang-su’s fingers trembled.

The distance from Emei to here was by no means short. But if the opponent was the Four Sects Alliance, they could arrive in as little as half a day.

‘We must flee!’

The first thought that came to his mind was crystal clear.

There was no force here worth calling an army. Those responsible for the Tang Family’s military power had mostly been led away by the lord to the Yangtze river.

Of course, there were some who could fight among those left, but they alone could not stand against the Four Sects Alliance and defend the Tang Family.

How could those remaining here possibly deal with an Alliance that had reduced even the fully intact Qingcheng and Emei to ashes?

“Inform all the elders at once! Abandon the halls and flee—”

“General.”

At that moment, someone called Tang Sang-Su in a low voice.

Tang Sang-Su flinched as he turned toward the sound.

An elderly man with a cold gaze, who hadn’t been here moments before, had appeared and was staring straight at him.

Tang Sang-Su bowed his head.

“I pay my respects to the Elder.”

A cold sweat began to break out.

It wasn’t facing an enemy. How could the Tang Family’s Elders be called enemies? But neither were they on friendly terms.

‘The Elder Council…’

The Elders had been completely pushed out and lost power in the power struggle with the head. And yet one of those Elders had suddenly appeared now.

“I have heard the situation.”

“Yes? Ah… Yes! Elder. We must lead everyone away from the enemies right now! If we hesitate—”

“Calm down.”

“…Yes?”

Tang Sang-Su blinked in panic. Tang Byeok, an Elder of the Tang Family, clicked his tongue and looked at him.

“Can the general take responsibility?”

Tang Sang-Su stared blankly at Tang Byeok. Responsibility—was this the time to discuss responsibility?

“What do you mean…?”

“No matter how much the lord entrusted family affairs to the general, he would not have assumed a situation like this. Would he?”

“…Th-that may be so, but…”

It was not an incorrect statement.

If Tang Gunak had considered even the slightest possibility of this happening, he would never have entrusted all authority to the general Tang Sang-Su. At least Tang Pae, the Young lord, would have been left to prepare for such a situation.

Tang Byeok, with a chill in his eyes, spoke again.

“If you know, then hand that authority to someone suitable. The current situation is beyond the chief steward’s capability.”

Tang Sang-Su swallowed hard.

“I-I understand what you mean. I admit the current situation is too much for me. But Elder, someone suitable? Are you saying there is someone within the family capable of handling this now?”

“There is.”

“Who would that be…?”

Tang Byeok frowned.

“Such needless suspicion. Of course it is not me—I’m not fit. Who else besides the Elder Council Lord is there who can decide the family’s fate in this situation?”

Tang Sang-Su’s face changed several times in a short moment.

The Elder Council Lord, Tang Wei.

No, the former Elder Council Lord Tang Wei. Although the Elder Council has been dissolved and he cannot use that title anymore, he once wielded influence within the family equal to—no, greater than—Tang Gunak.

Had he not been defeated by Chung Myung in their duel and completely lost power, he would still have been manipulating the family.

“B-but the Elder Council Lord had all his positions stripped…”

“Are you worrying about that in this situation?”

“…”

“So? Do you have the confidence to bear all the consequences that will follow your decision, general? Even if your wrong judgment plunges the Sichuan Tang Family into an irreparable abyss?”

Tang Sang-Su’s hands shook.

He could not bear it. The burden was too great for Tang Sang-Su, who kept his position as the general on the basis of lineage alone.

“What will you do?”

But handing decision-making back to Tang Wei was also impossible. After all, Tang Wei was the family’s criminal whose authority had been stripped and who was imprisoned on Tang Gunak’s orders. How could such a person be given that heavy authority again?

“General!”

“…”

“Whether the one who wields the sword is righteous is secondary. The important question is whether he has the strength to swing the sword. General, do you have the courage to wield the sword that is the fate of the Tang Family?”

Tang Sang-Su’s shoulders trembled.

“The courage you must show now is to recognize your own shortcomings and step back. Do not commit any more sins against the Tang Family!”

“…the Elder Council Lord…”

In the end, Tang Sang-Su’s head fell helplessly.

“I will go see the Elder Council Lord.”

Only then did Tang Byeok nod with a resolute face.

“That is right.”

Tang Sang-Su squeezed his eyes shut in despair, forcing himself to ignore his shaken heart, unable to do either.

‘For the Tang Family.’

Everything was, simply, for the Tang Family.

“Hm.”

And so, in the deep heart of the Tang Family.

The old man sitting on the bed in the room looked at those standing before him with a strange gaze.

Tang Sang-Su swallowed dryly.

It was strange when thought about; the man before him was just a powerless old man long stripped of martial arts skill. Someone who had diligently trained in martial arts had no reason to fear him.

Especially since the old man had committed crimes, been imprisoned, and only by the head’s mercy been permitted to live in the back room.

But Tang Sang-Su could not bring himself to meet the old man’s eyes.

The immense power and ability Tang Wei had once possessed, and the ruthless scheming he showed in seeking to control even Tang Gunak, still remained in memory and pressed down on Tang Sang-Su.

Tang Sang-Su glanced up briefly, then lowered his gaze again.

‘What kind of gaze is that…’

Generally, a martial artist who has lost his skill becomes a wreck or at least loses his former vigor.

But Tang Wei’s gaze did not seem weaker than in his prime; if anything, it shone even sharper.

“So…”

“Yes, Elder Council Lord.”

Tang Wei’s wrinkled face twitched for a moment.

“I am not the Elder Council Lord now, call me Grand Elder.”

“…Yes, Grand Elder. Right now, Jang Ilso of the Four Sects Alliance is about to attack the Tang Family with his subordinates. We must quickly lead the retainers and escape this place…”

“Tsk tsk.”

At that moment Tang Wei clicked his tongue softly. Tang Sang-Su, shrinking instinctively, could not continue.

“Abandon the family… Do you know what that means?”

“…Sorry?”

“The Tang Family does not abandon its land. We would rather accept annihilation than turn our backs.”

“B-but we cannot handle them with just us!”

“Why is it impossible?”

Tang Sang-Su snapped his head up.

Hearing such an absurd statement, he wondered if Tang Wei was being perverse out of resentment.

But Tang Wei’s eyes remained calm and sharp.

“Why? Do you think this old man has gone senile?”

“N-no, that’s not it…”

“General. Do you know why the Tang Family does not retreat?”

“Well… isn’t it because we are of the Tang Family?”

“You speak like a child. That’s wrong.”

“Yes?”

Tang Wei said calmly.

“The reason the Tang Family does not retreat is because if we retreat, everything literally ends.”

“…”

“Other sects, if their people survive and their martial knowledge is preserved, can somehow rebuild their foundations. Over time they might even surpass their former glory. But not us. Do you know why?”

Tang Sang-Su was about to answer that he didn’t know, but he could not.

The reason flashed through his mind the moment he heard the question.

“W-white Furnace…”

“That’s right.”

Cold sweat ran down his spine.

The Tang Family’s one and only furnace is the White Furnace. That furnace contains all the techniques the Tang Family has accumulated for centuries.

That means once the White Furnace is destroyed, no matter what is done, it cannot be recreated.

“Do you understand what losing the White Furnace means?”

“…”

And without the White Furnace, the creation of the Tang Family’s finest secret weapons, built by consolidating all their techniques, becomes impossible.

“It’s not just the White Furnace. Even creating a new Blue Furnace is not easy.”

Tang Wei paused and shook his head.

“And compared to what really matters, the White Furnace is a small part. If we abandon this place, the Tang Family’s poison vault will burn. We will lose every poison accumulated and crafted since the founder’s time, and the rare toxins the family unearthed through sweat and blood will all become ashes. Do you understand what that means?”

He could not stand.

If people survive, the name Sichuan Tang Family may remain. But that Tang Family could never be the Tang Family of old.

How can a Tang Family that has lost its poisons and secret manuals still be the Tang Family?

Everyone who can fight survives? What meaning does that have if there are no secret weapons to throw and no poisons to scatter?

Who would fear a Tang martial artist who cannot use poisons or secret weapons?

Martial knowledge does not vanish from a person’s body. If the formulae remain in their heads, they can always be rewritten into secret manuals.

But burned poisons cannot be recovered, and the rare toxins painstakingly gathered from across the land cannot be simply obtained again through effort.

To recreate the Tang Family’s poisons as they are now might take hundreds of years.

Or perhaps they may never again reach the same level.

Seeing Tang Sang-Su’s dismayed expression, Tang Wei clicked his tongue.

“Do you finally understand? Why we cannot retreat. Why our ancestors did not retreat and instead protected this land even unto death.”

“B-but Grand Elder. In the past we clearly avoided the Heavenly Demon…”

“The Demonic Cult had no interest in mere halls. It was odd that they burned Mount Hua. But do you think the Hegemony Lord would leave a Tang Family emptied of people as it is?”

…Of course not. If it’s Jang Ilso, he would utterly destroy this place, so the Tang Family would never again strike fear across the land.

“Do you understand? Our ancestors knew this, so they made the Sichuan Tang Family a fortress of the world—digging traps, setting absolute defenses so outsiders could not dare touch it! They did all this because they knew that the moment we retreated, the Tang Family would be finished.”

Tang Sang-Su bit his lip.

“So you’d end the Tang Family’s name here just to save a few lives? Is that something someone bearing the Tang surname could say?”

A cold blue killing intent poured from Tang Wei’s eyes as he glared at Tang Sang-Su.

Faced with that reproachful gaze, all Tang Sang-Su could do was fall silent.

“Gather the elders.”

“Grand Elder…”

“Gather every person bearing the Tang surname, without exception. Whoever the enemy may be, the Tang Family will not retreat. We will fight to the end—until the last person falls, until the last tile burns. Of course…”

Tang Wei rose from his seat.

“I, too, will do the same.”

Those words were like a wedge to Tang Sang-su. Even the old man who had lost martial skill was prepared to risk his life; on what face could he speak of retreat?

Tang Sang-Su clenched his fist and finally nodded.

“I will gather the elders.”

“Hurry.”

“Yes.”

Tang Sang-Su bit his lip hard.

Yes, this is all for the Tang Family. Therefore it cannot possibly be wrong.

…Never.

Chapter 1421: Is This The Sichuan Tang Family? (1)

A bolt of fire struck the Tang Family.

“Where are the thunder cannons!”

“Th-the thunder cannons are forbidden armaments, aren’t they? They can’t be taken out without the family lord’s order!”

“Do you plan to keep spouting that when the Tang Family becomes a sea of fire? Open them at once!”

“Ah, understood!”

The sealed doors of the Forbidden Armory opened. Craftsmen of the Tang Family who had devoted their lives to creating the finest hidden weapons.

Even those craftsmen had forbidden some devices because their power was too terrible; after decades those hidden weapons now revealed themselves to a world flooded with light.

“Open the Heavenly Poison Vault as well!”

“E-Elder. The Heavenly Poison Vault is…”

“Do you want to die by my hand?”

The door to the poison vault, the most important poison repository in the Tang Family, which could not be opened without the family lord’s permission, was flung wide.

People whose eyes burned with icy resolve rushed into the vault without hesitation and stuffed vials containing the Tang Family’s potions into their sleeves.

Tang Byeok watched and nodded gravely.

There is no one here capable of fighting properly now. In such circumstances, facing the Four Sects Alliance would be nothing but a futile struggle if this were an ordinary sect.

‘But we are the Tang Family.’

They could fight because they were the Tang Family. Weapons capable of killing unrivaled masters, even if wielded by women unfamiliar with martial arts. The Tang Family had dedicated itself to crafting such arms.

“Elders, check that the formations have no faults! Not an inch of error is allowed!”

“Understood!”

On Tang Byeok’s command, the Elders ran through the clan, checking that the defensive formations throughout the Tang Family would activate properly against invaders.

“All who can fight, step forward! No—those who cannot fight, come as well! This is a battle to defend the Tang Family. If the Tang Family ceases to exist, neither will you exist. Use your lives to protect the Tang Family!”

At Tang Byeok’s hoarse cry, everyone bearing the Tang surname rushed out.

Those who had spent their lives in dark rooms tending poisons, those whose gait was warped by the side effects of research, and even women surnamed Tang who had never been acknowledged as true Tang Family members—each wetted their sleeves with poison and gripped the cold hidden weapons.

The same was true for craftsmen who had never put down their hammers.

“W-what on earth is going on?”

An older man spat out the answer to a young craftsman’s question.

“The Four Sects Alliance is attacking.”

“T-the Four Sects Alliance? At a time like this?”

There is no family lord. There are no fighters. And of all times, the Four Sects Alliance attacks now?

“Qingcheng and Emei have already been struck. Next is our turn.”

“Us…”

The young craftsman’s face drained of color.

“T-then shouldn’t we flee? How can we possibly handle those who even Qingcheng and Emei couldn’t?”

“Where would we run to?”

“…What?”

One of the elders clicked his tongue and spoke.

“You might live if you run. But this place will be gone—the workshops of the Tang Family we’ve devoted our lives to.”

The young craftsman turned blankly to look back. The vast workshop came into view.

“Do you think we can rebuild this workshop once it’s destroyed?”

“That…”

This workshop contains all the Tang Family techniques that no other workshop can imitate. Rebuild it from the ground up?

“…It would be difficult.”

“Indeed.”

As craftsmen they understood how arduous it would be to rebuild a workshop that condensed centuries of Tang Family craftsmanship.

Some things they didn’t even know how to recreate. The techniques might survive, and relics remain in use, but some materials can no longer be obtained.

How could they possibly rebuild this workshop?

If this workshop falls, the Tang Family falls. The legacy of their ancestors who devoted their lives to these workshops would be reduced to ashes.

Can they accept that? Allow it to happen?

“You may flee.”

“What?”

“Young ones like you should live. But we’ve lived our lives. We cannot watch these workshops be destroyed with open eyes. We’d rather die.”

An elder reached out and grabbed a sword that hung as decoration in the workshop.

Clang!

A sharp gleam spilled from the drawn blade. It was clearly no ordinary sword. The craftsman who stared at it nodded gravely.

“We must fight. Even if it’s in vain, we must fight. That is the duty of those with the Tang surname.”

“…”

“Our ancestors fought like that to protect the Tang Family. Now it’s our turn.”

The young craftsman bit his lip at those seemingly matter-of-fact words.

“Where would I go?”

“Hmm?”

“I am a Tang Family craftsman too! And even swinging a sword, wouldn’t a younger man like me do better than the elders? They’re down to using only small hammers now.”

“Haha. Such a fellow.”

The young craftsman reached out and grabbed the bundle of finished flying needles he had just made.

“We must fight. If it’s to protect this workshop, we must fight. This is the Tang Family—this workshop is the Tang Family. Where would a Tang person go? A Tang person must be with the Tang Family.”

The older craftsmen looked warmly at the young man whose resolve hardened.

“Yes. That’s how a Tang Family person lives.”

“Shall we go? That way?”

“You go first. We’ll follow soon.”

“Yes!”

As the young craftsmen poured out of the workshop, one craftsman who had watched them rose and walked toward the deepest part of the workshop.

Clang!

Clang!

The steady ringing of small hammers.

What had sounded faint as if about to stop grew louder. The craftsman bowed his head to an old man hammering at a small forge in the innermost workshop.

“Divine Hand, elder.”

Clang!

The softly spoken name was swallowed by the hammer’s blows.

“Divine Hand, elder.”

He called again, but the hammering did not stop. The craftsman watched the old man and spoke again.

“Grandfather.”

Only then did the hammer’s blows soften slightly.

“We will fight to protect the workshop. Grandfather, you should withdraw your body…”

Clang!

The hammer’s blows rose again and the craftsman fell silent—the small hammer striking the heated metal, sending up red sparks.

The craftsman watching the old man quietly nodded slowly.

The old man’s eyes lacked focus. Their blur made one doubt whether he even knew what he was doing.

Where would such a man go? Even if his mind were clear, he would not leave the workshop—everything he had devoted his life to was here.

This old man was the very soul of the Tang Family. Shouldn’t he be where he rightfully belongs?

“Do as you wish, grandfather.”

The deeply bowing craftsman turned away.

After the craftsman left, the lone old man turned the iron he had lifted with tongs and shoved it into the forge. Sparks flew up into the air.

The old man’s blurred eyes—Tang Jo-Pyeong’s—stared vacantly at the blazing forge.

Jo-Pyeong.

“…”

Those damned fools will end up dying buried under the name I forged. Idiots who don’t know what comes first or last. Those who value what I made over me cannot walk the right path. When you truly become a Tang Family craftsman, change these things first. Do you understand?

‘Grandfather.’

Words the Dark Saint once said to him, while stroking his head.

Tang Jo-Pyeong gripped the tongs and pulled the heated iron from the forge.

‘Did I promise I would?’

He couldn’t recall clearly. The only memory was that the hand that stroked his head had been warm.

So what did Tang Jo-Pyeong do?

Had he upheld his grandfather’s admonitions? Or had he become the very sort his grandfather despised?

Clang.

The small hammer struck the glowing iron.

Melt, heat, cool, strike.

All those processes eliminate the extraneous, leaving only the pure and proper; what remains is then made even stronger by human hands.

That is the Tang Family craftsman. What he has done his whole life.

Clang!

The small hammer strikes the heated iron.

Clang!

The hammer that had been striking without pause slowly lowered. Tang Jo-Pyeong, lowering the hammer weakly, raised his blurry gaze.

The Tang Family workshop.

Everything of his bore the imprint of his hands.

Tang Jo-Pyeong, who had silently guarded the workshop, took up the hammer again.

Clang!

The regular hammering echoed—yet strangely it felt as if it might stop at any moment, softly and repeatedly.

“All Tang Family members are preparing to resist the forces under the orders of the elder.”

Tang Wei nodded slowly. Tang Byeok glanced at him and spoke.

“Former lord, once you finish this, restoration of your original position won’t be impossible. Even if the family lord opposes, the entire clan will support you.”

Tang Wei had lost martial authority. Normally, regaining his former position would be impossible. But the situation was different. If Tang Wei could lead the remaining people to repel the Four Sects Alliance, his loss of authority would be irrelevant.

Then reestablishing the Council of Elders would not be just a dream…

“Former lord.”

“…Yes?”

Tang Wei’s sudden voice made Tang Byeok look at him.

“Do not call me Former lord. I am not the former lord.”

“…Yes, Great Elder.”

“If by luck we can repel them, I will retreat again to the Tang Family’s inner rooms.”

Tang Byeok opened his eyes wide.

“W-what do you mean? Great Elder! You are saving the Tang Family.”

“Do you think I am doing this for mere power?”

A sharp expression passed over Tang Wei’s face.

“Power? It’s important. But more important is the Tang Family. Even that power comes from the Tang Family. My dislike of the family lord was only because I thought he wasn’t fit to lead the Tang Family, not to satisfy my own desire.”

“Great Elder…”
