“To protect the Tang Family even at the cost of my life, even at the cost of others’ lives. That’s all I intend. The rest is not for an old man like me. Even if I doubt them, I must accept it now.”

Tang Wei closed his eyes slightly.

He still did not like Tang Gunak—indeed, he might regard him more as an enemy. But such personal relations were trivial; before the name Sichuan Tang Family, they meant nothing.

“We must protect it. We must protect it somehow. I am a Tang person. That is the duty and belief of one who has lived with the Tang surname all his life.”

“…I understand what you mean, Great Elder.”

It was at that moment.

“Elder! A dispatch from the Family Lord!”

“What?”

Tang Byeok snapped his head around.

“It’s addressed to the general first… but this…”

“Bring it here! At once!”

“Yes! Here!”

The runner burst in and produced a green-lettered dispatch from his bosom.

“Give it here.”

Tang Wei stopped Tang Byeok from reaching, grabbed the dispatch himself, tore the seal, and as he read the paper his head slowly shook.

“May I ask what it says?”

Tang Wei chuckled and showed the dispatch to Tang Byeok.

“The family head orders you to abandon the family house and flee.”

“…What?”

Tang Byeok looked at Tang Wei in disbelief.

“The family lord is still weak. This is why I cannot rest easy.”

Tang Wei held the dispatch over a lamp. The paper caught fire and burned away blackly and slowly.

“To send word to the Four Seas Merchant Guild in the meantime… What does the family head think he is?”

Shaking his head, Tang Wei gently put the burning dispatch down. The paper bearing the family lord’s order turned to ash in an instant.

“The enemies will arrive soon, so finish preparations. Today will be a long day.”

“Understood, Great Elder.”

As Tang Byeok bowed and left, Tang Wei coughed and sat on the edge of his bed.

‘…Tang Family.’

He would not let the Tang Family fall.

Even if he remained a failure, he would be the Tang Family’s failure. That was Tang Wei’s final resolve after a lifetime spent for the Sichuan Tang Family.

‘Ancestors, watch over us.’

He would be a Tang Family member until his dying moment. That would never change.

Chapter 1422: Is This The Sichuan Tang Family? (2)

“All the weapons needed for throwing have been prepared.”

“Great Elder! The Illusory Poison Maze array installed by the Tang Family main gate has been checked. It’s operating normally without issue!”

“The forbidden-tier assassin needles have been distributed! The Elders will use them!”

Those who had rushed in urgently poured reports to Tang Wei one after another.

“We’ve summoned every branch family member staying at Tang Family extensions into the clan. Except for those we can’t reach, everyone is assembling without exception!”

It was a literal all-out war the likes of which one might doubt ever existed in the history of the Sichuan Tang Family. Not only the Tang Family’s direct line living within the clan, but even the branch families living at Tang Family premises were rushing in, ready to stake their lives.

Yes. This is what a Tang Family should be. I don’t know how great Jang Ilso is, but at least they will not be passively overrun.

“And the enemy?”

“They still need time to arrive.”

“Hmm.”

The distance from Emei to here was 200 km. For an ordinary person it would take at least four full days of running to get here.

So no matter how much the Four Sects Alliance wants it, they couldn’t arrive instantly just by deciding to. At least there was time until sunset today.

“Check every situation one more time. And…”

Tang Wei ordered firmly.

“Distribute poisons and needles to the family’s women as well.”

“G-Great Elder! That…”

The Elders flinched in shock.

This is the Tang Family. But not everyone here bears the Tang surname.

Those who belonged to the Tang Family but did not carry the Tang surname. Women married into the Tang Family were proud members of the Sichuan Tang Family, yet they could not touch weapons or poisons.

Not because it was too dangerous. The Sichuan Tang Family was a prestigious clan and a leading house in Sichuan. So those marrying into the Tang Family were inevitably women from notable houses.

Out of fear they might leak the Tang Family’s secrets to their houses, talismans and poisons had not been issued to them for centuries; it had become a tradition.

It wasn’t even a rule only for those married in. Even girls born with the Tang surname couldn’t touch poisons and weapons deemed ‘secret techniques’ in the Tang Family. It was a rigorously enforced protocol.

But now Tang Wei was ordering that protocol to be broken. He, who was the most stubborn and conservative within the Tang Family.

“…B-But to outsiders…”

Tang Wei coldly asked the Elder who mumbled that he couldn’t bring himself to hear such an order.

“Who is an outsider?”

Tang Wei glared at the Elders as he spoke.

“Everyone here has pledged their life to defend the Tang Family. Among them, who is an outsider? Are you calling someone an outsider just because they don’t use the Tang surname?”

At that, the Elders all fell silent.

Of course Tang Wei might feel differently inside. It’s hard to change a lifetime of entrenched thinking in an instant. But even so, no one could refute that statement in the current situation.

“I will take the blame for breaking the protocol. You merely followed my orders. So hand them over. You can’t expect me to tell them to fight without supplying weapons.”

“Yes, Great Elder. We understand what you mean.”

He would take responsibility. That was enough. Since Tang Wei said he’d bear the blame, Tang Gunak would not come to punish others.

“Hurry! The fate of the family is at stake. Recheck everything! If anything is unfinished, finish it by any means! Time is of the essence!”

“Yes!”

Watching the Elders dash off with grim faces, Tang Wei suddenly wavered.

“Koff!”

He covered his mouth with his hand and coughed several times. His body convulsed violently. His dry hand trembled.

“Oh my…”

It wasn’t that he had a particular illness. His body had simply become far weaker than before. A martial person whose dantian is destroyed can become so feeble they can’t compare to ordinary people. Tang Wei was too old to withstand that change.

To be honest, it wasn’t easy for him even to stand his ground here. Yet he straightened his back by force.

If any martial skill remained in him, he would fight the invading villains without regret. But now he had neither the internal strength to scatter poisons nor the muscle to throw talismans.

Still… he was the Tang Family.

Tang Wei stared blankly at the Tang Family pavilions. After staring for a long time, a murmuring voice escaped his lips.

“How absurd…”

At one time he had considered these pavilions monstrous.

Things that could have been his but ultimately never became so. His life had been a journey to bring these cursed things back into his embrace.

A life denied by his father, living in the shadow of his brother, now even punished by his nephew. It was this very Tang Family that had given him that life full of suffering.

Yet Tang Wei sought to defend this place to the end. Horrible as it was, it was his life. Everything about him was soaked into this place.

He might have been a failure. But if so, at least he’d remain the Tang Family’s failure. If the Tang Family vanished, wouldn’t he truly become nothing?

‘They’re all the same.’

Because the Tang Family exists, everyone here has meaning. If the Tang Family disappeared, not only these people but also the blood and sweat of all who made the name Sichuan Tang Family echo across the world would be gone.

Therefore!

‘I will protect it. Even if I must give everything.’

Tang Wei bit his lip and shouted in a crisp voice.

“Bring more weapons! Don’t spare them!”

“Yes, Great Elder!”

“Tang Byeok!”

“Yes!”

“In the family lord’s office, at the back, is the family lord’s poison vault! Gather the poisons stocked inside and bring them!”

“G-Great Elder. But that place can’t be opened without the family lord’s key…”

“Break it!”

“…Are you serious?”

Tang Byeok asked pale-faced, and Tang Wei glared at him lividly.

“Do I look like I’m joking right now?”

“…”

“If you won’t go, I’ll go myself.”

“Ah, no, Great Elder. I’ll obey your order!”

Tang Byeok bit his lip. Tang Wei, having lost his martial might, couldn’t possibly break that safe himself. So if it was to be done, Tang Byeok should do it.

The Great Elder truly seemed to have no intention of watching his back. Perhaps he even had no thought of surviving beyond this battle.

If the Great Elder had resolved himself to that degree, Tang Byeok couldn’t simply back away.

“Use everything possible. If we can at least preserve the poison vault and the furnace, the family can be revived no matter what damage we suffer. Remember! At all costs protect the poison vault and the furnace!”

“Yes!”

Just as Tang Wei once again instilled his grim resolve into everyone,

“F-Fireeeee!”

A scream burst from behind. Tang Wei snapped his gaze around and looked quickly to his surroundings.

Fire!

‘Th-They already?’

Had the Four Sects Alliance already infiltrated the Tang Family? That should be impossible.

“Is it an attack?”

“I-I don’t know! The flames suddenly…!”

Now even Tang Wei could see clearly. The sparks once faint on the pavilion roofs had instantly swelled into tremendous flames.

That could never be a natural fire.

“Confirm if it’s an enemy attack! Quickly!”

“Yes!”

A few terrified Elders rushed to check the camp. While Tang Wei trembled anxiously, news that might ease him soon arrived.

“It… doesn’t seem to be an attack! There’s no sign of intrusion.”

“Ah…”

For a moment Tang Wei almost collapsed, but he barely steadied himself.

Then it was likely a fire that broke out during the emission of a fire-type items. It’s uncommon, but not impossible. It would have been better if it hadn’t happened, but it wasn’t an unmanageable accident.

“Tch. At a time like this.”

Tang Wei clicked his tongue unhappily and shouted.

“Quick, extinguish the flames!”

“Yes, Great Elder.”

Tang Wei, about to turn away because this wasn’t the time for trifles, paused.

‘Wait…’

Those flames…

“Where, where is it?”

“Yes?”

“The fire! Where is that fire burning?”

Tang Wei suddenly shouted as if screaming. The Elders, blinking at his words, turned pale in an instant.

“Y-Yang… Yang Hall!”

“What are you waiting for! Go! Put out the fire! Hurry, hurry!”

“Yes!”

Yang Hall. A name that feels gentle, but in reality it was no warm place.

A place that manufactured the Tang Family’s secret poisons based on countless toxins from across the world. All the poisons and toxic substances the Tang Family had gathered were stored in the laboratory at Yang Hall. It was literally the heart of the Tang Family.

Of all places, that was where the fire had started.

“This…!”

Tang Wei couldn’t just leave it to subordinates and ran at full force. His body was already not fit for running, but he couldn’t merely watch if Yang Hall was burning.

‘If we can extinguish the fire, that’s all. Fire is…’

He forced himself calm and ran, but the scene that met him at Yang Hall was more dreadful than he expected.

The Tang Family Elders had rushed in trying to extinguish the blaze, but it showed no signs of being contained.

“G-Great Elder! The fire won’t go out!”

“What did you say?”

Tang Wei’s face went ashen. And in that instant,

“Fire! The workshop is on fire!”

“There’s a fire in the Celestial Poison Repository! Hurry and summon people! This is not an ordinary blaze!”

Crimson flames leapt up from all corners of the Tang Family.

Yang Gall, where the poison research chamber was located; the workshop that housed White Furnace, which the Tang Family guarded with their lives; and the poison vault storing the secret techniques produced by those two.

Flames rose simultaneously in places that could be said to be the Sichuan Tang Family itself.

“Put it out, put it out.”

In that truly horrific situation, even Tang Wei looked stunned and half lost his wits.

“Put out the fire! By any means, extinguish it! Hurry!”

A hoarse voice burst out.

His Sichuan Tang Family — the Sichuan Tang Family he vowed to protect with his life — was burning at that moment. And the Four Sects Alliance’s blade hadn’t even struck.

“It won’t go out! Damn it! This fire won’t die!”

“We must collapse the building immediately!”

“Are you even being sane? If you collapse the poison vault, all the toxins will be ruined! You madman!”

“Then what do you suggest? The fire won’t go out! Are you going to let it burn everything?”

Even the Tang Family Elders who had lived as martial men with dignity and clarity all their lives lost their composure in this moment. Harsh words flew.

“The fire… won’t go out?”

A dazed voice escaped Tang Wei’s mouth.

If trained martial men couldn’t put it out, that meant it wasn’t an ordinary fire.

Tang Wei knew.

Of the single substance in the world that could create such flames.

“White Furnace… Blood.”

White Furnace’s fire must not be extinguished. Even after fulfilling its purpose, at least the embers must be preserved.

The white furnace that never goes out. It was the Tang Family’s symbol and its soul.

Therefore the Tang Family’s ancestors searched the world and produced an oil that could sustain an unquenchable flame.

As long as White Furnace’s fire did not go out, the Tang Family would not vanish. Thus it was named White Furnace Blood. That oil was White Furnace’s blood, the Tang Family’s blood. The quantity was so minuscule it couldn’t even be used as a talisman; only the clan’s greatest craftsmen were permitted to handle it.

“Why…”

The furnace that had been burning the Tang Family suddenly glowed white. Seeing that sight, a desperate roar tore from Tang Wei’s throat.

“Why! Whyyyy! Whyyyyyyyyyyyy!”

Amid the roaring flames, an old man carrying a hammer walked out slowly.

The Divine Hand, Tang Jo-Pyeong.

His eyes, usually dim, gleamed coldly with an unusually clear blue light.

Chapter 1423: Is This The Sichuan Tang Family? (3)

This included Tang Wei and the other Tang Family members; they all had no choice but to immediately understand.

Why this unquenchable blaze had flared up, and who had done such a thing.

Countless suspicions bloomed in everyone’s eyes and then quickly turned to resentment.

“Why…”

Tang Wei’s trembling voice pierced through the crackling sound of the flames consuming the wood.

“Why did you do this? Elder Divine Hand… Why?”

If someone else within the Tang Family had done this, no one would have questioned the reason.

They would first have shattered his whole body with lethal poisoning and only then begun.

But that could not be done now. The one standing before them was none other than Tang Jo-Pyeong.

Though age had eroded him and his consciousness was often clouded, he was still the greatest elder of the Tang Family and the head of its core workshop.

There was no one within the Tang Family who could raise their voice against him.

Not even Tang Wei, the former council head and great elder, was an exception.

“Why? Why!”

As Tang Wei asked desperately, Tang Jo-Pyeong slowly turned to look at him.

Tang Wei’s eyes, full of bitter poison-like grief, met Tang Jo-Pyeong’s cold, indifferent gaze.

Tang Jo-Pyeong opened his mouth.

“…I merely burned it.”

Grit.

Tang Wei bit his lips until they bled.

“Who is asking that out of ignorance after seeing it now!? Don’t you know what you burned, Elder!? Why on earth did you set fire to the Poison Workshop and the Furnace! To the place that was practically the entire Tang Family! Even using the White Furnace blood!”

“…”

“Why! Why! Why did you do this! No matter how cloudy your mind may be, aren’t you Elder Divine Hand!? Why!”

His voice seemed soaked in blood. Resentment and pain came through plainly. The Tang Family that had been his everything was burning. It felt as if red tears of blood would spill from his eyes at any moment.

But Tang Jo-Pyeong’s gaze remained cold and detached; it was impossible to guess what he was thinking.

“Elder Divine Hand, what on earth…”

Other elders tried to speak in Tang Wei’s stead, but Tang Jo-Pyeong’s calm voice flowed out as if to silence them.

“There is nothing here.”

“…What?”

“Not anymore. Nothing worth protecting or preserving remains.”

“…”

“So go. All that remains are halls rotting and decaying.”

The elders turned their heads and stared, stunned, at the raging flames.

Tang Jo-Pyeong was not wrong.

What they had tried to protect here was the facility—the source—that produced the toxic substances and hidden weapons the Tang Family boasted about across the land.

But if that place was all going to become ash, there was no reason to cling to it any longer.

The very reason to risk their lives fighting the Four Sects Alliance had vanished.

“What, what are you all doing?”

Tang Wei shouted in a fit.

“Put it out! Put that fire out right now!”

“G-great Elder.”

“What are you standing there watching for!? If you can’t put out the fire, at least get the items inside out! Now! Move!”

Tang Wei raged desperately, but no one moved.

Surely even a blaze kindled with White Furnace blood was not impossible to extinguish. If it were possible, White Furnace blood itself would have made the hidden weapon world sing of its name.

But even if extinguishing it were possible, it was extremely difficult. To suppress flames of that scale would take ages, even if everyone present threw themselves at it.

And by then nothing would remain. And even if they salvaged unlit furnaces and toxins, that alone would never be enough to rebuild the Tang Family.

Tang Wei knew this too. But he simply could not give up.

As the elders remained still, Tang Wei, who had bitten his lip, cried out.

“Move! I’ll do it myself…”

But then Tang Jo-Pyeong spoke.

“Is that something you are meant to do?”

Tang Wei flinched and glared at Tang Jo-Pyeong, trembling.

“…You are sane, then.”

He had wondered if Tang Jo-Pyeong had lost his mind and done this, but his consciousness seemed clear.

“…So, are you relieved now?”

A scathing cry that seemed to tear his soul burst from Tang Wei’s mouth.

“Are you satisfied now that you’ve reduced everything the ancestors guarded for centuries to ashes? Are you glad you’ve made the name Sichuan Tang Family vanish from the world? Are you pleased to have handed those who have lost honor and skill a mere chance to cling to life?”

“Tang…”

“Inside there!”

Tang Wei’s hand violently pointed at the workshop; his thin fingertip trembled.

“Everything Elder Divine Hand dedicated his life to is right there! Are you happy after turning it all to ash!? Both Elder Divine Hand and I have lived our lives and could die, but because of your unilateral decision, the remaining children of the Tang Family will live persecuted for the rest of their days!”

Living in the martial world meant carrying countless grudges on your back. Because the Tang Family was strong, those who had been holding back would surely reveal themselves the moment it weakened.

Now Tang Jo-Pyeong had pushed his family’s descendants into that cycle of resentment.

“How can you be so indifferent!? How can you be so cruel!? Is this really for the Tang Family? If you were truly a great elder of the Tang Family, you should have done whatever it took to protect it! How do you not understand that!?”

Everyone’s faces darkened at Tang Wei’s vitriol.

Though his words were uncontrolled and crude, the meaning behind them resonated with everyone.

“Why did you do something like this…”

“This place is…”

“…What?”

Tang Jo-Pyeong slowly looked around.

He saw the Tang Family he had lived in his whole life: every small hall, every floor he stood upon—there was nothing untouched by his hand.

The Tang Family was his everything. Tang Jo-Pyeong was no different from Tang Wei in that regard. But…

“Is this the Sichuan Tang Family?”

Tang Wei bit his lip. What nonsense was this all of a sudden? Had he lost his mind now?

“Answer me.”

“Elder!”

“Am I not asking whether this is the Sichuan Tang Family!?”

At that moment a rough shout burst from Tang Jo-Pyeong.

Tang Wei flinched at the incomprehensibly fierce authority; poison flickered in Tang Jo-Pyeong’s eyes.

“You idiots! At best, they are just daggers and lumps of poison! At best a weapon that looks like it was forged from a lump of iron!”

“…Elder?”

“Is that truly the Tang Family? Is that really the Sichuan Tang Family? Answer me!”

“…”

“If there is no poison, it cannot be the Tang Family! If there are no hidden weapons, it cannot be the Tang Family! You idiots who don’t know what comes first and what comes after!”

At that moment Tang Jo-Pyeong roughly threw the hammer he had been holding.

Thud!

The small hammer that had been embedded in the floor bounced up and then fell back down.

As the noise subsided, the Tang Family was instantly swallowed by silence.

How much had that meekly tossed small hammer created? It was Tang Jo-Pyeong’s pride and the Tang Family’s pride.

And yet Tang Jo-Pyeong had just thrown away such a hammer.

“Risk your life to protect a lump of iron like that?”

“Elder!”

“If only people remain!”

Rage rose in Tang Jo-Pyeong. The frail old man’s body looked like a small furnace spitting forth red flames.

“If people remain, things like this can be remade endlessly! If only people remain!”

“…”

“Even if we never enjoy the same glory again, what difference does that make!? If keeping the Tang Family as the Tang Family requires throwing away all those lives, then such a Tang Family had better vanish!”

“You…!”

Tang Wei convulsed. The force of it was so violent that a startled Tang Byeok unconsciously grabbed Tang Wei’s arm. Tang Wei looked ready to lunge at Tang Jo-Pyeong at any moment.

Tang Jo-Pyeong, quietly watching Tang Wei, slowly looked around and spoke.

“Go.”

“…”

“There is nothing left for you to protect. If there is anything to guard now, it is only one thing: your lives.”

“…Elder Divine Hand.”

“So go. Time is urgent. Leave before the enemy arrives.”

His words finished, Tang Jo-Pyeong turned his body. Tang Wei’s desperate cry poured out toward his back.

“Do you think you could die and face our ancestors after this? What will they say to you!? You will remain as a sinner in the Tang Family’s history never seen before! No, all who carry Tang blood will spit on your grave and curse you! Elder Divine Hand! No, Tang Jo-Pyeong!”

Tang Jo-Pyeong simply walked in silence toward the burning workshop.

‘Everyone will spit and curse me…’

A faint smile, tinged with red, brushed his lips.

‘Then that would be a relief.’

At least it meant there would still be descendants of the Tang Family left to curse him. That was enough. After all, what he had sought to protect were the descendants—so in that sense he had succeeded.

“Elder…”

At that moment the craftsmen from the workshop who had been watching from afar approached him. Unlike before, there was distance and hesitation in their eyes. Tang Jo-Pyeong glanced at them and said indifferently.

“You should leave too.”

“…”

“And you, Elder…?”

He gazed quietly at the burning workshop. The workshop turning to ashes by the fire he had set.

“I will remain here.”

At his words, the craftsmen opened their mouths to speak but then shook their heads.

The route fleeing from the Four Sects Alliance would be extremely arduous. Tang Jo-Pyeong no longer had the strength to take that path.

“…Be safe.”

The craftsmen bowed deeply to Tang Jo-Pyeong.

They could not all agree with what he had done, but they nonetheless paid their respects sincerely. It was their final tribute to the craftsmen who had protected the Tang Family.

Tang Jo-Pyeong, having received their respects, walked without answering toward a corner of the burning workshop. The workshop burned more fiercely.

The place where he had lived for over a hundred years. The place where he had breathed and guarded.

‘Dark Saint, grandfather.’

A mist of tears filled his eyes—pride at having honored his grandfather’s wish and the pain of having burned everything he had with his own hands.

‘Is this enough now?’

Perhaps, as Tang Wei had said, Tang Jo-Pyeong had severed the Tang Family’s soul. Perhaps they would henceforth live forever as the Tang Family in name yet not in essence, suffering always.

Was it still right? Was there truly no other way? Was this the best?

He did not know. He probably would not know until the moment he died.

But one thing he could be certain of.

‘Grandfather.’

His grandfather’s image rose clearly—the touch of Tang Bo’s hand gently smiling and stroking his young head. That hand had been warm and comforting.

If Tang Bo were here now, he would surely have patted his head once more.

-Well done.

A single tear ran down Tang Jo-Pyeong’s wrinkled eyes.

The old man, now white-haired, smiled brightly like the child he had once been.

Chapter 1424: Is This The Sichuan Tang Family? (4)

“G-Great Young Master! Th-The Four Sects Alliance… The Four Sects Alliance has just reached the city gate!”

At the Third Chief’s words, Jo Woong’s face hardened with tension.

‘Of all times, Father had to be away on official business!’

Jo Woong swallowed dryly as if to calm his burning throat, snapped his head around, and looked ahead.

“Did you relay my orders?”

“Yes. I ordered everyone to be taken into the hall and remain cautious.”

“Tell them not to make a sound!”

“Yes!”

Large beads of sweat rolled down Jo Woong’s forehead.

News that the Hegemony Lord was coming here had only just reached the Four Seas Merchant Guild. It was already too late to flee. All Jo Woong could do was hold his breath and pray that Jang Ilso’s reach would not extend to the Four Seas Merchant Guild.

“W-Won’t that be a problem?”

“…It will be fine.”

Jo Woong swallowed again and continued.

“From what I’ve heard, the Hegemony Lord is feared throughout the land, but he rarely harms commoners who haven’t trained in the martial arts. If we remain as quiet as mice, he won’t bother persecuting us.”

His reason for thinking so wasn’t solely based on the Hegemony Lord’s reputation.

The Sichuan Tang Family is in this city. If Four Seas Merchant Guild, would he?

“B-But… w-wouldn’t there still be some connection between the Hegemony Lord and us?”

At that, the Third chief spoke in a trembling voice. When Jo Woong glanced at him, he hesitated a moment before speaking.

“I’ve heard the Four Sects Alliance treats Mount Hua like an enemy. And y-you are a Mount Hua person, aren’t you, Young Master?”

“…”

“Would they still leave us alone? We’re the family of their enemy, and aren’t those bastards from the Evil Sect heartless and merciless?”

Jo Woong’s hand trembled slightly.

His younger brother Jo Gul was not just any Mount Hua disciple. He was one of Mount Hua’s famed Five Swords, renowned even within Mount Hua.

If the rumor were true and the Four Sects Alliance truly harbored such deep resentment toward Mount Hua, who knew what they’d do to the Four Seas Merchant Union, Jo Gul’s family home?

“That won’t happen.”

“But…”

“I said it won’t happen. Stop worrying and go secure the households! Quickly!”

“Y-Yes… Yes, Great Young Master.”

Watching the chief reluctantly move, Jo Woong bit his lip. He knew his worry wasn’t entirely unfounded, but there was nothing else to do now. Panicking would only spread fear.

They were mere merchants. If the Four Sects Alliance set its mind on the Four Seas Merchant, what use would fleeing be? They would be slaughtered before they could go ten li.

So now they could only pray to heaven that Jang Ilso would pass through unaware that this was Jo Gul’s family home.

It was at that very moment.

“Oh, he’s coming!”

A terrified voice rang from outside. Startled, Jo Woong ran instinctively toward the still-open main gate.

Peering out, he saw a procession walking down the middle of the wide road far off.

‘…The Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso.’

He could tell at a glance.

There couldn’t possibly be two people in the world who dressed so ostentatiously. Behind him came men wrapped in ominously dark crimson robes, and among them a tall man wearing a red long coat, his chalk-white face chillingly locked in Jo Woong’s sight.

Snapping out of his daze, Jo Woong gave an order.

“Lock the gate! Quickly!”

“Yes! Yes! Great Young Master!”

Screech!

The large gate of the Sahae Trading Company slammed shut.

Bang!

A heavy bolt slid down and the door was firmly locked. Seeing the securely closed gate, Jo Woong instinctively clasped his hands together.

‘Please…’

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso strolled leisurely along the city’s main road, looking around. Despite this being the largest and most famous city in Sichuan Province, there wasn’t a single ant in his eyes.

“Oh dear. I didn’t expect a warm welcome, but this snub is a bit much. I didn’t think Sichuan folk were so stingy.”

Jang Ilso snorted and cast his gaze into the distance. The quickest route to the Sichuan Tang Family was through this city, but that wasn’t the only reason he had chosen this path.

He wanted them to know who had come here, who would rule this place from now on.

As he took a step with one corner of his mouth lifted, a subordinate’s voice whispered to him.

“Hm?”

Jang Ilso’s steps slowly halted. Those following him stopped in unison.

“…Oh?”

Jang Ilso, standing still, turned his head very slowly. His gaze fixed on a large pavilion bearing the signboard ‘Four Seas Merchant Guild’.

A crooked smile bloomed at the corner of Jang Ilso’s mouth.

Kwang!

“Ugh—!”

The firmly bolted gate shattered in an instant as if struck by an explosive cannon. Fragments scattered everywhere, and the ground shook as if it might collapse.

Everyone covered their heads and collapsed to the ground. Jo Woong could not. Not because he wasn’t afraid, but because he had seen something before the fear could fully take hold.

Beyond the broken gate stood a man.

A man wearing an ornate long coat embroidered with dragons, bedecked with ostentatious jewelry that seemed exaggerated. His face was unnaturally white and his lips were red as blood. But more than anything, what seized Jo Woong’s gaze were the man’s eyes.

Thanks to his status as the head son of the Four Seas Merchant Guild, Jo Woong had lived all over the land and had seen the world’s rarest things with his own eyes.

Yet there was no way to liken that gaze to anything he knew.

A snake? Ridiculous. Even the deadliest serpent would be frightened by those eyes. Even a creature incapable of understanding their meaning would be terrified.

Those two eyes, inscrutable in their contents, were staring straight at Jo Woong.

“Hmm…”

Jang Ilso emitted a faint hum. With that small sound, the world, which had momentarily frozen, began to move again. For Jo Woong, it was an unwelcome sensation.

Jang Ilso tore his gaze from Jo Woong and slowly, suffocatingly slowly, scanned the trading house from left to right, then spoke.

“Is this… the home of Mount Hua’s Five Swords, Jo Gul?”

Jang Ilso’s eyes, having stopped, returned once more to Jo Woong.

“Is that right?”

Jo Woong could not answer.

Not just because the answer might strangle him. Even if he had an answer that could perfectly defuse the situation, he could not bring himself to speak.

His whole body froze like a mouse confronted by a snake. The back of his neck prickled and his hairs stood on end.

Seeing Jo Woong so petrified, Jang Ilso raised the corner of his mouth.

“So it seems.”

Jang Ilso regarded Jo Woong as if probing and asked.

“Then you… are his sibling, right?”

Jo Woong’s Adam’s apple bobbed. His throat, parched from nerves, ached even when swallowing saliva.

“You do resemble him a little. Hmm.”

Jang Ilso slowly approached Jo Woong.

Step. Step.

His walk was brimming with ease.

Jo Woong didn’t even know what he was feeling. The word ‘fear’ seemed inadequate. Even if the grim reaper approached, he wouldn’t tremble like this; death was frightening, yes, but everyone must accept death eventually.

But the sensation this man conveyed was different. It choked the breath from him and constricted his body. He could not foresee what would happen or what he would have to endure. Everything felt swept up into chaos.

‘Is Geol… fighting someone like this?’

With such a monster?

At that moment, Jang Ilso, having reached right in front of Jo Woong, stared at him like a snake eyeing its thickly wrapped prey.

“Do you know who I am?”

“…”

“Answer me.”

Jo Woong’s body trembled uncontrollably.

“Hurry.”

“You are Jang Ilso…”

“…”

“T-The Hegemony Lord… Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso…”

“You know.”

Jang Ilso smiled as if pleased.

“Then you also know that your brother and I have quite a history.”

A thousand thoughts flashed through Jo Woong’s mind.

He had to deny it. Even if it was filial, he had to deny the connection. Only then could everyone survive.

Gul had already left the family. Our ties with him were severed. He is a brother in blood but not in tie. Therefore it would be unjust and pointless for the Hegemony Lord to unleash his wrath on us.

But all those thoughts failed to become words. Unable to deny his brother, Jo Woong kept silent, trembling violently.

“Ho?”

Seeing him, Jang Ilso faintly nodded as if he roughly guessed his thoughts.

“Yes. Brothers are a good thing. That’s what family should be. Family implies the resolve to share fate. Isn’t that so?”

Jang Ilso stepped close and lifted his hand to lightly grasp the nape of Jo Woong’s neck. His nails pricked Jo Woong sharply.

If that large grip tightened even slightly, Jo Woong’s neck would collapse like rotten straw. That would be the signal that none in the trading house would survive.

Facing this gruesome crossroads, Jo Woong’s face turned as white as paper.

Jang Ilso leaned in and, very quietly, whispered into Jo Woong’s ear.

“So…”

His voice was sly and concise, almost devilish.

“Convey my regards.”

Jo Woong’s eyes widened of their own accord.

“Tell him I stopped by. Understand?”

Jang Ilso pulled his head back, wide-eyed, and smiled faintly at the frozen Jo Woong.

“Your answer?”

Jo Woong couldn’t bring himself to speak; summoning all his strength, he desperately nodded. Jang Ilso took a step back and nodded in return.

“Good. A dutiful child.”

Chuckling at the stunned Jo Woong, he then spun on his heel as if he had no lingering regrets.

“Work hard. Pay your taxes on time. Hahahaha.”

As if waving to a close friend, Jang Ilso waved his hand over his head, stepped out the gate, and led his group away.

As if bewitched, Jo Woong watched the scene until his legs finally gave out. He collapsed on the spot, sweat pouring from his entire body, soaking his clothes in an instant.

“G-Great Young Master!”

The Third Chief, who had run up belatedly, quickly tried to support him, but Jo Woong pushed him away with trembling hands, refusing help.

“…I’m fine.”

Jo Woong couldn’t get up and stared blankly at the now quiet scene outside the gate as if nothing had happened.

Where there was no trace left of Jang Ilso.

Chapter 1425: Is This The Sichuan Tang Family? (5)

Jeok Ho, who had been looking around the Four Seas Merchant Guild, turned his head forward again and fixed his gaze on the back of Jang Ilso, on a back he couldn’t even begin to guess the thoughts of.

“Alliance lord.”

“Hm?”

“Might I ask the reason you left them be?”

The Jang Ilso whom Jeok Ho knew was not afraid to see blood. Actually, it might be better to say he was less reluctant to see blood than anyone else in the world.

That such a man didn’t touch commoners wasn’t due to some great principle; it was simply because there was no gain to be had from doing so.

If there was something to be gained, whether from commoners or royals, Jang Ilso would trample it without hesitation.

So it was puzzling.

Just burning that Four Seas Merchant Guild could inflict pain on Mount Hua’s disciples. It may not be a great suffering, but slaughtering them wouldn’t be much trouble either.

“If it’s because you don’t want to waste time, I’ll go quickly and check.”

“A reason…”

Jeok Ho spoke cautiously, but Jang Ilso muttered to himself as if he hadn’t heard. A strange smile spread across his lips.

“Do I really need a reason?”

“…Yes?”

“Then let’s say it’s simply a whim.”

A complicated expression crossed Jeok Ho’s face. He couldn’t readily understand that answer. Still, he didn’t ask further.

Jang Ilso asked, as if surprised.

“You won’t ask again? You look like you didn’t understand.”

“I’ve already heard the answer.”

To demand an answer from the Alliance lord that would satisfy him would be impious. If he heard an answer, that was enough. Jeok Ho considered that his duty.

Jang Ilso glanced at Jeok Ho and twisted his red lips.

“I know you dislike Ga-Myung.”

Jeok Ho’s face stiffened slightly. It felt out of the blue. What reason was there for such a remark now?

“This is why I keep Ga-Myung close. If Ga-Myung had been here, he would have stopped me from toying with them. He would have kept them intact without touching a hair on their heads.”

“…Why is that?”

Jeok Ho had no question about Jang Ilso, but it was different if the other was Ho Ga-Myung. Jeok Ho’s face tightened with displeasure.

Seeing that, Jang Ilso laughed as if amused.

“If someone were your mother’s enemy, what would you do?”

“I’d tear them to pieces. By any means necessary.”

“Such filial devotion. Tsk, how dull.”

Jang Ilso shook his head and continued.

“Alright. Then what if someone held your mother hostage?”

Jeok Ho couldn’t answer for a moment.

One must kill an enemy of one’s mother. But how does one deal with someone who has taken your mother hostage?

The more you value your mother, the less you can do. At least, it’s hard to act rashly.

“That’s why Ga-Myung would have left them alive.”

“…”

“Especially with people like Mount Hua, it’s meaningful. As long as that merchant guild is in our hands, they won’t move recklessly. Understand? Clinging to hatred is petty. What matters is whether someone is useful.”

Jeok Ho pressed his lips together. It was surely true.

Qingcheng and Emei had already fallen. Now the Tang Family and Dian-cang would be dealt with. That meant the entire Sichuan would fall into the Four Sects Alliance’s grip.

Everything in Sichuan would belong to the Four Sects Alliance. Rather than killing them off, it’s better to use them as playable cards.

He saw the present, but Ho Ga-Myung would have seen the future.

That observation cut Jeok Ho to the bone.

“…Then wouldn’t it be better to send some forces now so they can’t escape?”

“Leave them be.”

Jeok Ho could only look at Jang Ilso with questioning eyes again.

“Tsk tsk. You don’t understand people. I merely said that Ga-Myung would have done so.”

“Then… Alliance lord, you?”

“I said it. It’s simply a whim.”

“…”

“I just wanted to. Is that not allowed?”

Jeok Ho let out a hollow laugh for a moment. Now he understood what mistake he’d been making.

Jang Ilso was simply Jang Ilso; one should never dare try to understand his thoughts and actions. From the start, this man couldn’t be interpreted by ordinary minds.

Jang Ilso smiled gently.

“That’s not what’s important right now.”

“Indeed, you are right.”

What mattered to them now was, of course, the Sichuan Tang Family.

A chilling look tinted Jang Ilso’s eyes.

“If you want to see a sight, you can see it at the Tang Family.”

At least, there would be none of those things there.

No whim, no luxury called mercy.

❀ ❀ ❀

A huge gate stood before him.

Sichuan Tang Family.

The calligraphy on the grand signboard was truly bold and fierce. Jang Ilso, who had been staring at that gate for a long while, slowly shifted his gaze to the side.

A pattern both unfamiliar and oddly familiar was engraved on the gate.

‘Is that a Plum Blossom?’

A Plum Blossom on the Sichuan Tang Family’s gate, how terribly out of place.

But the peculiar gleam in Jang Ilso’s eyes wasn’t because of the pattern’s incongruity.

“Is this… the Sichuan Tang Family? This quiet place?”

A hollow laugh slipped from Jang Ilso’s mouth.

It was so quiet it bordered on silence.

Of course, the Sichuan Tang Family was always quiet. Those greeting Jang Ilso wouldn’t have shouted their hostility; even if full of fighting spirit, they’d hold their breath and watch him.

But this silence didn’t come from solemn resolve. It was the desolation that comes when no people remain.

And moreover…

“Lo, Alliance lord. Smoke…”

Jang Ilso gave a hollow chuckle.

Beyond the great gate, black smoke billowed from all over the Sichuan Tang Family, as if a firestorm had already swept through.

Another enemy? That couldn’t be.

On the way here, they hadn’t encountered a single enemy since leaving the Tang Family outpost. From that alone, they could guess what had happened here.

Jang Ilso’s expression twisted slightly.

‘Sichuan Tang Family…’

He had thought members of the Tang Family couldn’t leave their domain, so had his judgment been wrong?

“In any case, we should check.”

Jang Ilso nodded toward the front.

“Open it.”

“Yes!”

Kraang!

The ancient gate that had guarded the Sichuan Tang Family for ages shattered to pieces.

At the sight revealed, everyone couldn’t help but show stunned expressions.

Inside the Tang Family compound not a single ant remained. The halls that should have gleamed were half-burned and collapsing, soon to be mere ashes.

“…This is…”

If one only saw this scene, one could never believe this was the Sichuan Tang Family famed throughout the Central Plains.

“Hrm.”

While everyone struggled for words, Jang Ilso shook his head as if incredulous.

“Did they get hit once and run? Surely those great Tang Family folks wouldn’t just take to their heels.”

“…”

“And they even set their own house on fire. This makes me feel pointless.”

Jang Ilso chuckled as if amused. The others, however, couldn’t bring themselves to laugh.

Who could have imagined the Tang Family would do such a thing?

“This is absurd…”

Jeok Ho bit his lip.

Other sects might do that, but not the Tang Family. Given their nature, could the Tang Family abandon their family and flee?

“Search!”

A sharp voice burst from Jeok Ho.

“It might be a ruse. They wouldn’t abandon their poisons and forges and run. Find where they set fires, check if anyone’s hiding! Now!”

“Yes!”

Except for the Red Dogs escorting Jang Ilso, all others dispersed in every direction.

“Tsk.”

Jang Ilso clicked his tongue briefly.

‘Strange.’

Of course he was no god, so his prediction being off wasn’t especially surprising.

But this situation felt different. A strange sense of discord twisted inside Jang Ilso.

‘I don’t understand.’

The Sichuan Tang Family burning their house and fleeing was a situation that could occur.

What bothered Jang Ilso was that the scene before him showed a sense of urgency.

If they’d truly decided to abandon this place, they would have burned it more thoroughly. They wouldn’t have wanted to leave anything for the Four Sects Alliance.

Then what was the confusion left throughout the halls?

Didn’t it look like the traces left by people pushed to leave despite not wanting to?

“Uh, there’s nothing!”

“All of what appeared to be their poison storerooms are burned! All manner of toxic substances inside have burned to death!”

“Th-the weapons in the armory are all scorched and unusable. It seems they truly set the warehouses on fire themselves.”

Panicked voices rang from all around.

Jeok Ho’s face contorted. Jang Ilso, however, simply smiled, just as he was about to order them to stop the pointless work.

“A-Alliance lord, here…!”

Jang Ilso’s eyes narrowed.

“Th-there are people!”

The face of the one who ran to report looked bewildered, as if at a loss how to explain the situation. Jang Ilso took a step forward.

“Let’s go see.”

Kang!

It wasn’t a loud sound.

Kang!

But the sound spread widely across the silent workshops, feeble as if about to fade, yet steady and far-reaching.

Kang!

In the half-ash workshop, about to collapse, an old man was swinging a hammer in the center, working over a tiny furnace like his own body.

The hammer that had risen into the air came down in one blow, striking the heated iron on the anvil.

Kang!

The old man, holding the iron with tongs, inspected it closely near his face with eyes that seemed both clouded and clear, both shallow and deep.

Kang!

The old man brought the hammer down again.

As if drawn by the sound, members of the Ten Thousand People Clan gathered around him one by one, but the old man continued hammering as if only he and the furnace existed, not even turning his head.

It was strange.

Even though they weren’t the strongest, the elite of the Ten Thousand People Clan were known as vicious; yet they didn’t lay a hand on the old man standing before them.

They, too, seemed unsure how to handle the situation and merely kept their distance, watching the old man’s hammering.

Then Jang Ilso looked at the old man, Tang Jo-Pyeong, and spoke.

“What… what is he doing now?”

His face twisted as if cracked.

Chapter 1426: So Is This Dagger A Failure? (1)

The indifferent hammer kept striking the iron on the anvil, as if utterly unrelated to everything happening around it.

Kahng!

Each time the small hammer fell, the blazing-hot iron spat sparks in every direction.

Tang Jo-Pyeong, who had been hammering for so long, peered intently at the iron he lifted with tongs, then thrust it back into the furnace that belched searing flames.

His face, watching the iron engulfed by flames, flickered with a ruddy shadow.

Jang Ilso, who had watched Tang Jo-Pyeong in silence, finally parted his blood-red lips.

“Old Master.”

A chill-laden voice washed over the old man.

“Where did the rest of the Tang Family go?”

It was a voice that would make anyone look up; no one could remain calm while a snake coiled around them.

But Tang Jo-Pyeong showed no particular reaction to Jang Ilso’s words. As if deaf, he simply fixed his gaze on the furnace.

A chill entered Jang Ilso’s eyes. Before he could act, Tang Jo-Pyeong, with casual hands, pulled the heated iron out and began hammering again.

Kahng! Kahng!

Each strike on the glowing iron released an uncanny, strange force.

“Hmm.”

At that, Jang Ilso watched Tang Jo-Pyeong with a smile, as if intrigued.

“It’s been a long time since anyone dared to snub me like this.”

For a moment the faces of the Red Dogs guards who stood watch went pale. Those who had escorted Jang Ilso nearby could keenly sense the twisted anger hidden in his slow tone.

One of the Red Dogs stepped forward hastily.

“This old man…”

“Stand aside.”

But Jang Ilso coldly barked and blocked them. The Red Dogs who were about to move stepped back.

Thud.

Jang Ilso slowly approached Tang Jo-Pyeong. Standing within arm’s reach, he looked down at the tiny, frail old man as if he might crumble at any moment—his gaze transparent and emotionless.

It was just a pointless waste of time anyway.

Whatever this old man did mattered not to him. He could swing his hand and kill him easily, then pursue those who fled. He knew that better than anyone, yet he had stopped here out of simple curiosity. Yes, nothing more.

“What are you making?”

There was no answer this time either.

Jang Ilso snorted. A clear murderous intent bloomed in his delicately curved eyes.

But at that moment, a murmured voice slipped from Tang Jo-Pyeong without him turning his head.

“A dagger.”

“Hmm?”

“I’m making a dagger.”

It was more a soliloquy than an answer. Jang Ilso furrowed his brow slightly.

A dagger… in this situation?

Kahng!

Tang Jo-Pyeong struck the hammer again. A relentlessly sharp light swirled in Jang Ilso’s staring eyes.

“A lifetime’s devotion… has collapsed.”

A voice, almost scoffing, rang out.

“Everything I forged with my spirit has turned to ashes. Still, this is all I can do. So even at the end of life, I repeat what I’ve always done.”

Jang Ilso curled the corner of his mouth.

“If that’s the life of craftsmen… then it’s nothing but pitiable, pitiful indeed.”

Kahng!

Tang Jo-Pyeong’s hammering grew slightly harsher. Jang Ilso twisted his mouth into a deeper smile as the iron was shoved into the furnace.

“Well?”

“…”

“Do you think a magnificent sword will come of it?”

It was not a mere question but a blade driven into the soul.

“Is a sword being made great enough to justify that dreadful end? Enough to console that the miserable life wasn’t wasted?”

“…”

“Reality is brutal. The tale of a craftsman pouring his soul into a masterpiece at his last moment is nonsense found only in stories. In reality, you just pound iron that hasn’t even properly melted and produce worthless scrap.”

For a moment Tang Jo-Pyeong glanced at Jang Ilso with an inscrutable look, then pulled the dagger from the furnace and placed it on the anvil.

Jang Ilso asked.

“How is it? The feeling of having everything you are denied? Of clinging to a useless task despite it all?”

His stomach twisted. Those who have lost everything often pretend largesse at the end, as if they never wanted any of it to begin with.

He had seen countless fools feign enlightenment at death, and each time a wave of disgust rose in Jang Ilso.

Better those who cry and beg; they’re honest. Those who crawl and claw for life are pure compared to losers who ascribe false meaning to the meaningless and insist they actually wanted what they did not.

“Answer me. Is that dagger worth being the last object of your life? For such a paltry life…”

His voice seemed to twist and squeeze the soul.

“Do you really have to cling to it? Huh?”

Under his wide sleeve Jang Ilso’s hand twitched, showing he didn’t mean to threaten with words alone.

But Tang Jo-Pyeong, aware Jang Ilso could end his life at any moment, seemed unconcerned about death and merely inspected the heated iron.

Kahng!

Tang Jo-Pyeong resumed hammering. Red sparks flew as if the old man’s soul were scattering.

The iron on the anvil took on more of the short sword’s shape; Tang Jo-Pyeong, after carefully inspecting it with tongs, shoved it back into the furnace.

Tang Jo-Pyeong briefly turned his head to look at Jang Ilso. The old man’s indifferent gaze and Jang Ilso’s flickering eyes met in the air.

A surge of revulsion rose in Jang Ilso; just as he was about to swing his hand, Tang Jo-Pyeong spoke.

“I gather the purest iron from among countless pieces.”

“…”

“I melt it in a furnace fierce enough to be cruel.”

Kahng!

Once more Tang Jo-Pyeong struck the short sword with his hammer.

“I strike it hundreds, thousands of times, and again and again.”

Kahng!

“Until it reaches the most perfect form and strength. It is twisted, broken, bent countless times…”

Kaaang!

Tang Jo-Pyeong slammed the hammer down hard and plunged the short sword into a bucket of water beside him.

Ssssss!

“Thrown into filthy, filthy water.”

White steam burst violently from the water that cradled the blazing blade.

“Only after being ground and ground, having its flesh taken away until the final and very final moment, will it—at last—become a true sword.”

For a moment Jang Ilso’s gaze became strangely calm.

There was a peculiar resonance in Tang Jo-Pyeong’s voice—a weight that even Jang Ilso could not easily dismiss.

“All my life I’ve done those things. I forged stronger, sharper, greater swords, crafted excellent weapons, and if they fell short by even a bit, I broke them and melted them down again.”

Tang Jo-Pyeong took the short sword from the bucket and placed it on the anvil.

A dark, blotchy color and a bumpy surface. Though unpolished, it was hardly something one could call a masterpiece.

Made with a battered little furnace and gnarled, aged arms, it was still a sword the Tang Family’s craftsman had made at the end of his life. Yet its appearance was so humble it was hard to believe.

Tang Jo-Pyeong studied the unbearably humble blade with careful attention, as if it were a once-in-a-lifetime masterpiece.

Squeeze.

Tang Jo-Pyeong gripped the still-hot short sword in his hand.

Ssssss!

As his flesh tore, blood flowed and stained the sword. Yet the old man seemed not to feel the pain; he simply stared at the blade and spoke.

“Then is this short sword a failure?”

Tang Jo-Pyeong slowly set his hammer down.

He left the sword—still seeming to need countless more strikes—abandoned. A shabby, low-grade short sword.

But there was no real regret in the way he placed the hammer down.

Turning the dagger slowly in his hand and examining every corner with a composed look, he nodded slowly.

Then he extended the dagger in his hand—specifically—to Jang Ilso.

Silence fell. As Jang Ilso opened his mouth at the old man’s inexplicable action, Tang Jo-Pyeong’s indifferent voice came first.

“Take it.”

For a moment a clear expression of bewilderment crossed Jang Ilso’s face. Glancing between the offered dagger and Tang Jo-Pyeong, he slightly contorted his eyes.

“… What are you doing?”

No matter how close to death the old man was, anyone bearing the Tang name would know who stood before them.

Yet the old man was offering Jang Ilso the last weapon of his life. He couldn’t possibly be begging for his life with this, could he?

As if answering Jang Ilso’s question, Tang Jo-Pyeong spoke.

“Iron has no predetermined owner. The one who holds it is its owner. You’re simply here now.”

Jang Ilso looked down at the bumpy sword as if incredulous.

“…This dagger might even be driven into the Tang Family’s neck, you know?”

Even so, Tang Jo-Pyeong did not withdraw the offered sword.

He simply stared at Jang Ilso. Jang Ilso’s face had become expressionless. After that silence, he slowly reached out and seized the offered short sword.

Squeeze.

Heat still radiated. Tang Jo-Pyeong’s dried blood on the sword’s surface stained his hand dark red.

Staring at the misshapen, ruined sword, Jang Ilso gripped it tight and raised his head—preparing to cut down Tang Jo-Pyeong in one stroke.

But.

“This old man…”

Jang Ilso’s face contorted.

Tang Jo-Pyeong’s head sagged down. In that moment, the old man’s breath stopped.

Pathetic.

The hammer he set down at his last moment, his twisted, aged body, even the shabby furnace before him—all were simply pathetic.

Yet the faint smile at Tang Jo-Pyeong’s lips was something even the great Jang Ilso could not mock as pathetic.

As the furnace died, Tang Jo-Pyeong’s body cooled. Jang Ilso, staring at the Tang Family craftsman who passed without a sound, flapped his long cloak and turned away.

“Just wasted time.”

The cold voice rang out.

“They’re not here. Pursue them. They can’t have gotten far.”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan members and the Red Dogs, who had been holding their breath nearby, scattered in all directions. They would likely track the fugitives’ trail soon.

Crackle.

Just as Jang Ilso turned to walk away without hesitation, a sound brushed his ear and he stopped. It was the sound of the furnace burning down. A strange light crossed his face.

“…Hmph.”

Whoosh!

Jang Ilso’s hand swung. As force poured out, the entire workshop collapsed in an instant, burying Tang Jo-Pyeong’s body.

Rumble!

Staring coldly at the ruined workshop, Jang Ilso shoved the sword he held roughly into his garments.

“I detest going into debt.”

Leaving words no one could hear, he turned and walked away.

The workshop collapsed completely.

On the ruins of that workshop—now meaningless and abandoned—one small, battered hammer stuck up like a gravestone for someone who had finally found rest.

Chapter 1427: So Is This Dagger A Failure? (2)

A leisurely footstep sounded. It was Jang Ilso walking through the center of the Tang Family.

They had been delayed briefly by one old man, but it was a minor thing. Thoughts of Tang Jo-Pyeong had already been completely wiped from his mind.

Instead, what caught his eye were the half-collapsed pavilions of the Tang Family. Intact halls and burned ones scattered about looked like a miniature of the current Central Plains.

A strange sensation washed over him.

“Alliance lord! We’ve searched everywhere, but there don’t seem to be any survivors here.”

“You’re wasting effort. It’s pointless anyway.”

At Jang Ilso’s rebuke, Jeok Ho bowed his head deeply.

In a fifth-rank family like this, they’d typically prepare at least one hiding place within the clan. He had suspected they might have burned buildings to disguise and hide somewhere, but it had been a wasted effort. Confusion passed across Jeok Ho’s face.

‘If that’s the case… did the Tang Family really abandon their clan and flee?’

The Sichuan Tang Family?

Surely even Jang Ilso did not predict this. If he had foreseen this even a little, he would not have sent them on a wild goose chase.

“Hmm.”

As if knowing his thoughts, Jang Ilso emitted a low hum.

“…Which is it?”

“Yes?”

“Has the Tang Family gone mad? Or has my fame risen higher than I thought? Could the Sichuan Tang Family be so cowed as to abandon their home and flee?”

Probably both.

The Sichuan Tang Family they knew would never abandon their clan. That they fled means the Tang Family now is not the Tang Family they knew.

And even if they had changed, if the one crushing them weren’t Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso, they wouldn’t have chosen this. They would have fought to the death here.

A gloom settled into Jang Ilso’s eyes.

Whatever the reason, an unexpected event displeased him. It felt like pouring ice water on the exhilaration from toppling Emei and Dian-cang. An indescribable unease twisted in his gut.

“The Sichuan Tang Family… all those tangled with the Heavenly Friends Alliance are a headache. Or rather, should I say those tied to that kid?”

A chuckle escaped Jang Ilso’s mouth.

It probably had nothing to do with them. From this distance, what could Mount Hua’s Sword Saint do?

Yet Jang Ilso couldn’t shake the feeling that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had some influence in this.

“Such a troublesome child.”

He had herded them strictly to the east in case something like this happened, yet even from this distance they made trouble.

Jang Ilso slowly licked his lips. A predatory glint flickered in his eyes.

“Alliance lord!”

One of the Red Dogs darted up and kowtowed before Jang Ilso.

“We’ve found traces of their movement. To the north.”

Jang Ilso nodded. Naturally north, there was no other way for them to survive.

“What is the scale?”

“Everyone. Even those at the Tang Family compound seem to have moved at once.”

Surprised, Jang Ilso’s eyes narrowed.

“All at once?”

“Yes!”

His red lips twisted for a moment.

“Hmm. So… you’re saying even those at the Tang Family compound were gathered and fled together?”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

Jang Ilso laughed as if incredulous.

“That’s peculiar. I thought the Tang Family strictly separated direct and collateral lines… yet they’ve gathered not only non-direct members but even women and children and fled together? Even children without martial training?”

“…”

“Ha, if they’re going to change anyway…”

Jang Ilso’s gaze turned north.

“They could have been a bit smarter about it. Couldn’t they?”

Jeok Ho nodded.

If they had resolved to accept about half the casualties and scattered the family members everywhere, it would have been hard for Jang Ilso to respond. He wouldn’t know who to seize and who to let go until he had a hold.

But to flee deliberately in a single direction is like shaking your rear and daring someone to chase you.

“Chasing someone isn’t really my taste, but to tempt me so openly and not oblige would be mean. Prepare yourselves. We must catch them before they leave Sichuan.”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

The Red Dogs were about to carry out the order at once.

“Ah, before that.”

“…Yes?”

Jang Ilso slowly looked around the half-ruined pavilions of the Tang Family and spoke.

“If someone made a great decision… helping that decision is also a kindness a benevolent person can grant, isn’t it?”

A radiant smile spread across Jang Ilso’s face.

“Set everything ablaze. Leave not a single thing bearing the Tang Family name in this Sichuan land. Burn them to ashes so the wind scatters them and they vanish.”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

They all bowed once and ran off in unison. Jang Ilso’s lips parted slightly.

“I wonder… Tang Gunak.”

How will he accept this outcome?

With this, the Sichuan Tang Family has lost its roots. Given the nature of the Tang Family, they will never again regain their former prestige.

Even so, will Tang Gunak truly say this choice was right?

The descendants of the Tang Family will curse and reproach Tang Gunak for generations; can he nonetheless praise those who abandoned the clan and left?

Even though the Sichuan Tang Family will never be called the Lord of the Sichuan again?

“Hypocrisy that great is a disease.”

Jang Ilso chuckled.

Pitiful. Regrettable. So perhaps one might grant him a little mercy?

Whoosh!

Flames rose up on all sides.

The slowly kindling flames quickly swelled like a demon’s maw and devoured the remaining pavilions of the Sichuan Tang Family.

Everything of the Sichuan Tang Family — a famous house of Sichuan and the clan’s ancestral root famed across the land — was burning.

A blaze visible from across the capital. It was like a beacon announcing that Sichuan had come under the Four Sects Alliance’s dominion.

The firelight cast deep shadows across Jang Ilso’s face. Watching the burning halls with a strange smile, he turned away without regret.

“Let’s go.”

“Yes!”

The mercy he could grant Tang Gunak.

Was to let Tang Gunak let go of attachments easily. If those who fled all died, he could vent anger and blame them without hiding his true feelings.

Otherwise, by needlessly trying to save doomed lives, he’d end up watching the Tang Family’s seat burn and shedding bitter tears while cursing.

“Really, I’m in such a good mood I could burst.”

Jang Ilso laughed as if embarrassed and took a light step forward.

Their destination was north.

Where the Tang Family’s elders had fled.

❀ ❀ ❀

Fwoosh!

Tang Gunak, who had been pushing off the ground hard, flinched and slowed for a moment.

When he glanced back, the distance to those behind had widened again. Even though he knew they needed to keep pace together, his steps kept rushing ahead.

He bit his lip hard.

He knew.

They were already exhausted. Even if they weren’t, few could keep up with Tang Gunak’s full-speed lightness skill.

So there was no need to keep showing this and revealing his anxious heart. It was meaningless and only made him fret.

Even knowing this clearly, his heart kept rushing ahead and causing mistakes.

Tang Gunak glanced down at Chung Myung tucked at his side.

‘Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.’

Tang Gunak also knew how much of an unreasonable burden he was imposing. Mount Hua’s Sword Saint and the Five Swords were not in normal condition. Anyone with a bit of medical knowledge would foam at the mouth insisting they must rest.

But Tang Gunak’s stubbornness was making it hard on them, perhaps for something already too late.

But…

“Family Lord!”

At that moment Hyun Jong, who had caught up beside him, panted as he spoke.

“Family Head, I’m sorry. Your heart must be impatient, but our inability…”

Tang Gunak bit his lip with even more despair.

Why is he apologizing?

It’s not only Mount Hua who can’t keep up. The Tang Family’s elites can’t follow either. So why is Hyun Jong bowing his head to him?

Tang Gunak spoke with a heavy heart.

“I’m sorry. But please muster a little more strength. We are nearly in Sichuan; Chengdu is within reach.”

“Yes! Don’t worry. Even if our legs burst and we die, we will reach the Tang Family and die there.”

At those words, Tang Gunak glanced back.

As expected, the Five Swords following Hyun Jong also wore resolute expressions. Deep down they probably wanted to collapse right now.

‘If…’

If even one person of the Tang Family could be saved by this forced march, the Sichuan Tang Family would owe Mount Hua an unpayable debt. Tang Gunak etched that truth into his heart.

And at that moment.

Screeeee!

At the sharp cry of a bird from the sky, everyone hastily looked up.

“It’s the Beggars’ Union! It’s definitely a message rocket fired from the Beggars’ Union in Sichuan!”

Thought was short and action quick.

Tang Gunak immediately pushed off the ground and shot into the air. He reached out and grabbed the flying message rocket.

Smack!

Landing again, with lightning-fast hands he tore open the message tube hanging from the rocket’s leg. Reading the slip inside, Tang Gunak’s face visibly hardened.

“Family Lord, what does it say?”

“…Read it.”

Tang Gunak handed the slip to Hyun Jong. After reading it, Hyun Jong’s face also hardened.

[The Lower District Sect and another sect are moving south towards Dian-cang. The Ten Thousand People Clan is moving north pursuing the Sichuan Tang Family survivors.]

The message conveyed the situation concisely, but the implications were anything but simple.

“Are you saying they’ve split into two groups?”

“…It seems so.”

Embarrassment clouded Hyun Jong’s eyes.

If they had moved together, they could have countered whichever side they targeted. But split into two forces… helping both is impossible.

Dividing already insufficient forces into two could lead to the worst, Mount Hua and the Tang Family being defeated separately.

Hearing this from behind, Yoon Jong shouted as if there were no question.

“What are you thinking? Then we must go north! That’s where the Tang Family is!”

Of course, that’s the obvious conclusion.

But the confusion and anguish etched on Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak’s faces deepened.

“…So that’s it.”

Hyun Jong muttered in a slightly trembling voice. Tang Gunak bit his lip.

You must at all costs save Dian-cang.

Those were Beop Jeong’s words, urgently relayed by Beop Gye. Only now did they painfully grasp his intent.

Chapter 1428: So Is This Dagger A Failure? (3)

It’s frightening.

That was the first thought that came to Hyun Jong’s mind.

It’s hard to explain precisely why, but his spine went cold. The one who filled Hyun Jong’s mind was none other than Beop Jeong, the Abbott of Shaolin.

‘In that situation…’

Could it be that he foresaw things this far ahead?

No, no. That can’t be. No matter how great Beop Jeong is, he couldn’t have predicted the Tang Family abandoning their house and fleeing, or the Four Sects Alliance splitting in two.

Yes. That’s the reasonable way to think.

But…

‘Then isn’t that an even bigger problem?’

If all of this wasn’t calculated, then Beop Jeong had only one intention: to get them away somehow and at least save Dian-cang, wasn’t that the plan?

But if Mount Hua and the Tang Family end up turning their backs on Dian-cang, how would Beop Jeong react?

“Catastrophe…”

Words he hadn’t finished forming spilled from his tongue first.

There was no other word to describe the outcome than ‘catastrophe.’ The Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance would become a relationship that could never walk the same path again.

‘What should be done about this…’

It’s a terrible crossroads.

Even if there was intent, it doesn’t change that Beop Jeong risked danger to give them a chance to escape. To throw away Beop Jeong’s expectations is not something a person should do.

But that doesn’t mean they can abandon the Tang Family. Abandoning the Tang Family would be like abandoning the foundation of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. Isn’t the Heavenly Friends Alliance defined by never abandoning your friends?

No, even if the Heavenly Friends Alliance permitted it, Hyun Jong could not allow it.

Hyun Jong’s hand trembled.

If he chose the Tang Family, he’d choose friends but forsake his oath. If he chose Dian-cang, he’d keep his oath but turn a blind eye to the sacrifice of his friends.

How could he possibly choose either side?

But there wasn’t even time to ponder deeply. The Ten Thousand People Clan had begun pursuing the Tang Family, and if the remaining Four Sects Alliance members were heading for Dian-cang, they too had to immediately change course and chase after them. Otherwise, both sides would be sacrificed.

“Fo- former Sect leader. This is…”

Una A’s voice trembled as if he understood the situation. Just then, Hyun Jong, who could not bring himself to choose anything, squeezed his eyes shut.

“Huu…”

A low sigh.

A small but piercingly clear sigh pricked their ears. When Hyun Jong turned his head, he saw Tang Gunak with an untroubled expression.

“There is… there is no way.”

“Family lord…”

“We must go to Dian-cang, Alliance Leader.”

Hyun Jong’s eyes widened involuntarily.

“F- Family Lord. What are you saying! Right now the Ten Thousand People Clan, that Jang Ilso, is chasing the Tang Family’s members! If we don’t help, they will…”

“As I said.”

Tang Gunak answered in a voice as calm as could be.

“The Tang Family is not that weak. Even the Ten Thousand People Clan cannot catch and kill them all. Those who are to live will live.”

But Hyun Jong didn’t miss the faint tremor in Tang Gunak’s voice. Perhaps it was even obvious enough that the most oblivious could notice the tremor.

“Family Lord…”

“If we don’t go to Dian-cang, we’ll give them justification. The Abbot who has quietly disliked the Heavenly Friends Alliance will shout loudly and condemn the Alliance for lack of principle.”

Hyun Jong squeezed his eyes shut. That scene played vividly in his head.

But…

“Of course loyalty is important. However… it’s not everything. Family lord, how…”

“Alliance Leader.”

Tang Gunak cut off Hyun Jong’s words.

“We must do whatever it takes to prevent a rift with the Nine Great Sects. Especially at a time like this!”

“…”

“We must go to Dian-cang.”

Hyun Jong turned around in a daze. Those he looked at were not his disciples but the Tang Family’s elite who had been following Tang Gunak.

Those Jang Ilso was chasing were, broadly speaking, Tang Family household members, but more narrowly they were brothers, parents, and children.

Tang Gunak’s words meant leaving those people to die screaming in the Evil Sect’s clutches. It was so cold, so heartless, and so heartbreaking.

Yet none of the Tang Family present protested Tang Gunak’s words. They simply bit their lips and clutched their thighs, enduring the situation.

Because they understood.

Right or wrong, they knew that Tang Gunak—the one who had to make this decision—suffered the most, and so they gritted their teeth and endured the gut-wrenching pain.

Even Tang Pae, the Tang Family’s young lord, was among them.

“How…”

“You must go to Dian-cang! Former Sect leader.”

Hyun Jong looked at the one who spoke with trembling eyes.

Tang Soso.

The youngest of Mount Hua and Tang Gunak’s daughter, she trembled at her chin but managed to continue speaking.

“That’s what we have to do.”

“Soso…”

“This isn’t something to agonize over…”

At that moment, someone grabbed Tang Soso’s shoulder and pulled her back.

“…”

Surprised, Tang Soso turned her head and saw Yu Yiseol standing there with an expressionless face.

“Stop.”

The words sounded cold, but the voice was full of worry. Tang Soso’s shoulders trembled at that voice.

“No. No, Sago… We must go to Dian-cang. To Dian-cang.”

“I know, so stop.”

“We didn’t come here by our strength alone. But we can’t cause harm. The Tang Family isn’t that kind of place…”

Yu Yiseol gently pulled Tang Soso close and patted her shoulder. Unable to continue, Tang Soso bowed her head deeply.

Silence.

A deep silence settled among them.

“Former Sect leader.”

It was Baek Cheon who spoke up then.

“I understand the situation, but… is it truly impossible to divide our forces?”

Hyun Jong’s face contorted at Baek Cheon’s words.

Not out of anger, but because a terrible temptation had come to him for a moment. Perhaps dividing the troops could give them a chance to save both sides—an opportunity to not miss either.

Even if both fail, couldn’t he at least tell himself he had tried the minimum?

It was a very sweet temptation—enough to shake even Hyun Jong for a moment. But he pushed away that temptation desperately.

“That cannot be done. If we make a mistake, Mount Hua and the Tang Family could be defeated piece by piece. If that Jang Ilso prepared a trap knowing we’d move like that, there’d be no way to survive it.”

Baek Cheon bowed his head deeply.

His thoughts were not that different. He had only been shaking off lingering attachments.

“I understand what you mean, former Sect leader. But then isn’t going to the Tang Family the right thing to do?”

“Baek Cheon…”

“Not just the Tang Family. Timing suggests no major catastrophe, but in Sichuan there’s also Gul’s house. If we go to Dian-cang, something could happen in there too.”

At those words, Hyun Jong flinched and looked at Jo Gul.

Then Jo Gul shouted at Baek Cheon with an angry face.

“Why are you suddenly talking about my family!”

“It’s important.”

“Why is that important! Everyone else is risking their lives fighting, and you’re telling me to go defend my household? If I go there, do you think my father will praise me? He’ll just be angry at having such an idiot son!”

“Gul!”

Jo Gul snapped his head around to look at Hyun Jong.

“Do not think of my house, former Sect leader. They are not martial people.”

Hyun Jong bit his lip.

He understood Jo Gul’s meaning. But that didn’t mean he could just leave them.

“But I also agree with going to the Tang Family. We must protect them! They have no one to fight! Protecting the weak is our duty.”

“Dian-cang’s situation isn’t necessarily any better.”

“Sahyung!”

At Yoon Jong’s reply, Jo Gul burst out shouting.

“If we must help the weak, the same applies to Dian-cang. Just because we have no ties, or because they’re not part of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, we can’t relegate them to a lower priority…”

“You’re saying it’s not a negotiation?”

“No.”

Yoon Jong looked at Jo Gul with resolute eyes.

“It’s not the Tao.”

“…”

“Only gathering those you prefer is not the Tao. There is no high or low among people. Whoever they are.”

“Damn it! What a great sage you’ve become!”

Jo Gul growled and snapped angrily.

“Then be the sage, Sahyung! I’ll be a friend of the Tang Family!”

“Gul!”

“No, from the start…”

Just as their argument was about to escalate, Tang Gunak spoke.

“We don’t have time for this.”

Those words doused everyone like cold water.

“Jo Gul, Taoist.”

“… Family Lord.”

“Thank you.”

“…”

Jo Gul shut his mouth tightly.

“And Baek Cheon, Taoist as well. But now we must not go to the Tang Family. What we would lose is far too great.”

“Things like loyalty…”

“It’s not just loyalty. Above all is the world’s perception. If the belief that the Heavenly Friends Alliance will help those in danger collapses, even for groups not part of it, then…”

Baek Cheon flinched at Tang Gunak’s resolute words.

Tang Gunak was deliberately bringing up what Baek Cheon had tried not to think about.

“It means the covenant that the world believed the Heavenly Friends Alliance upheld would be shaken. People would think that, when pressed, we’d save our own first. Who would trust such a place? If that happens, the Heavenly Friends Alliance would lose what it must not lose above all. Compared to that…”

Tang Gunak spoke with a cruel edge.

“The lives of those who cannot fight are a small part.”

“…”

He wanted to be angry.

He wanted to shout how could he say such things. But no one could; the one who spoke was Tang Gunak himself.

“The threat of the Four Sects Alliance is no longer hypothetical. They are pressing a blade into the heart of the Central Plains. If the Nine Great Sects and the Heavenly Friends Alliance split under such circumstances, it will be over. Jang Ilso will widen that gap and set the world ablaze.”

“…”

“Do as I say.”

“Family Lord…”

“If you respect me as the Tang Family’s Family Lord, this once heed my words. At least let me choose the fate of my own family.”

Everyone bit their lips and looked at Tang Gunak.

“I beg you.”

Tang Gunak bowed his head.

It was an utterly desperate plea. Who would dare ignore such a request?

Jo Gul who had been shouting, the resolute Baek Cheon, and even the wavering Hyun Jong all averted their gazes before that nod.

But only one person.

“…Ugh.”

It wasn’t just one person.

“Damn… It’s filthy noisy.”

“Chung Myung!”

“Hey, you!”

Chung Myung, whom Tang Gunak had briefly put aside, sat up. Scratching the back of his head with one hand, he clicked his tongue at those rushing toward him.

“You all right, Chung Myung?”

“Why? You wanted me dead?”

“No, you bastard. What are you saying…”

“I know, so move aside. This is no time for that.”

At that, the Five Swords flinched and stepped back. Space opened between Chung Myung and Tang Gunak.

“I wondered what had happened in the meantime… So Jang Ilso is indeed formidable, it seems.”

“…you.”

“You’ve even made the Family Lord of the world spout nonsense.”

Tang Gunak’s face hardened.

“Making a fuss over something that needs no thought. Hey, sasuk.”

“Yeah.”

“Move out.”

“Where to?”

“Where? For goodness’ sake, it’s obvious.”

Chung Myung sprang to his feet and grinned.

“We’re going to the Tang Family. While we’re at it, we’ll take that Jang Ilso’s head.”

It was a moment when everyone’s mood changed completely.

Chapter 1429: So Is This Dagger A Failure? (4)

Tang Gunak stared at Chung Myung with a hard expression.

Chung Myung’s face was calm. He seemed truly unbothered now. But that couldn’t be. Tang Gunak, who knew his physical condition best, could not be unaware of how much Chung Myung was overexerting himself.

So there was only one thing he could say.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. I appreciate the intention, but I must decline your offer.”

Chung Myung, rubbing his aching back, glanced over at Tang Gunak.

“What is it?”

“……We won’t go to the Tang Family.”

“Ha.”

Chung Myung exhaled shortly and asked nonchalantly.

“Why?”

“Because it’s the right thing to do.”

“The right thing?”

Tang Gunak nodded heavily.

“We are grateful for your intentions toward the Tang Family, but the Tang Family cannot bear that burden. Might not the lives of a few in the Tang Family lead to even more bloodshed?”

“……”

“……Go to Dian-cang. That is the right thing.”

Tang Gunak’s tone was firm. But it felt as if he were cutting his own heart with a knife.

He knew. Now that Chung Myung had stood and said this, no one would oppose going to save the Tang Family. By staying silent alone, they could go rescue their families.

But Tang Gunak could not bring himself to do that. Because he was Tang Gunak.

“Fa-Family Lord. The Mount Hua’s Sword Saint spoke so forcefully…”

“Shut your mouth!”

Tang Pae, who tried to sway Tang Gunak’s resolve in that moment, bowed his head deeply at the cold glare Tang Gunak shot him.

“My decision stands. So this time, to Dian-cang…… Are you listening to me now?”

“Yes, well. Go on. I’ll listen.”

Tang Gunak spoke with utmost seriousness, but Chung Myung’s response hardly matched the gravity.

Chung Myung picked at his ear with his little finger, blew on the finger he pulled out, then turned to Tang Gunak with a sulky face.

“Didn’t you have something else to say?”

Tang Gunak’s face trembled for a moment.

He had forgotten. No, he hadn’t really forgotten…… He had remembered that this man was always like this, yet it slipped his mind.

“Are you finished?”

“You…”

As Tang Gunak was about to speak, Chung Myung sulkily called for Baek Cheon.

“Hey, sasuk.”

“Huh?”

“Did anything strange happen while I was asleep?”

“What do you mean by that?”

“No, it’s not like someone put poison in the Tang Family’s Family Lord’s head; why are you all taking such a heavy tone?”

“Ah, it’s not about poison or anything. The Family Lord has always been like that… Ah. I’m sorry.”

Scolded, Baek Cheon hastily coughed and averted his gaze. Tang Gunak’s face twisted miserably.

‘That……’
