How reliable Baek Cheon had been when the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had lost consciousness—Tang Pae had thought he would be content if Baek Cheon led only half as well.

But the moment the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint regained his wits, that dependable image vanished, replaced by a loafer-like man standing there.

The others were the same.

The instant Chung Myung came to, the blade-like momentum that had surrounded Mount Hua slackened as if lying, and even Jo Gul, who had looked like he would slash at any moment, was smirking.

“So what is the conclusion?”

“I’m telling you, we must go to Dian-cang!”

Tang Gunak snapped irritably in that instant.

“If we don’t go to Dian-cang, Beop Jeong will surely exact a price from us. Someone of your standing cannot be unaware of that, can you?”

“I don’t know the exact situation…… But, I suppose so?”

“Then why are you being so stubborn?”

“No, I didn’t know you could also make people laugh.”

“What?”

Tang Gunak stared blankly at Chung Myung.

“Think about it. A man who knows that great bald one so well would say such a thing?”

A look of confusion crossed Tang Gunak’s face. What on earth was this guy saying now?

Then Chung Myung spoke.

“So if we go to Dian-cang and save them as that bald man wants, do you think the great bald one will be so moved he cries, saying ‘I’ve regained faith in the Heavenly Friends Alliance! Now we are brothers bound by blood! Let’s stand together against the Four Sects Alliance!’? Do you think that’ll happen?”

Tang Gunak was momentarily at a loss for words.

And the answers to that question came not from Tang Gunak but from others.

“No.”

“As if that would happen.”

“Amitabha…… He is not that kind of man.”

Tang Gunak looked at the Five Swords as if he’d been punched in the face. Even Hae Yeon was shaking his head.

Chung Myung clicked his tongue.

“At best, you’ll hear they’ll overlook it this one time. So what do we gain? The Nine Great Sects looking a bit more kindly on the Heavenly Friends Alliance? What are we supposed to give up for something like that?”

“……Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

“Snap out of it, you fool.”

A sharp presence surged from Chung Myung for a moment. Startled, Tang Gunak stepped back without thinking.

But that intensity from Chung Myung faded as if it were an illusion, leaving the usual mischievous smirk.

“If blood’s rushing to your head, cool it down. There’s plenty of spit around to cool you. I’ve been stabbed before; it works wonders.”

Chung Myung chuckled and glanced at Tang Soso. Tears streamed silently from Tang Soso’s large eyes as she bit her lip.

Startled, Chung Myung turned his head and muttered.

“……Why is she making a fuss and crying again?”

Clearing his throat, Chung Myung this time looked at Hyun Jong instead of Tang Gunak.

“Sect leader.”

“Ah, no, I’m the retired…”

“Don’t stand there listening to idle talk like a dazed man; let’s go. You should know how to scold the child if she throws a tantrum. Are you going to indulge every little thing? Such a waste of time.”

“……I’m sorry.”

Faced with Chung Myung’s blunt scolding, Hyun Jong apologized before he knew it. He wasn’t sure what he’d done wrong, but Chung Myung’s scowling face made him feel compelled to apologize.

“Anyone else have something to say?”

No one spoke. Tang Gunak bit his lip tightly.

“This is……”

“Persistent, aren’t you. If that’s the case, take whoever wants to go to Dian-cang can go. But I doubt anyone here will follow the Family Lord.”

At those words, Tang Gunak involuntarily looked to his own people.

The Tang Family’s elite all averted their eyes as if refusing to ever go to Dian-cang. These were the very people who a moment ago would have followed Tang Gunak into hell.

“……These bastards……”

“I will accept punishment.”

Tang Pae spoke on their behalf.

“Even if disobedience costs me arms and legs, I will endure it. But Family Lord. No, Father.”

Tang Pae met Tang Gunak with a firmer gaze than ever.

“I remember what you told me, Father. You clearly said the Family Lord of the Tang Family must be someone who, for the Tang Family, can do whatever is necessary, right or wrong.”

“……”

“So now I cannot follow your words. I will go save my family. That is my decision as the Young lord, as a member of the Tang Family.”

Tang Gunak bit his lower lip. After glaring at Tang Pae for a long moment, he looked at the one who had caused all this. He panted as if he might shout, then let out a sigh.

“How do you intend to bear this?”

“……”

“Your words may be right. No doubt of it. Even if we go to Dian-cang, there will be no day when Beop Jeong looks on us kindly. But that’s incomparable to truly becoming their enemies. And the way the world looks upon us will no longer be what it once was. How will you bear that? Who will pay that price……”

“Me.”

Tang Gunak’s eyes widened. Chung Myung, meanwhile, remained unruffled.

“You’ll ask who will bear it, right? I’ll bear it. It’s obvious.”

“……”

“If you want to curse me, go ahead. Since when have I only done things to look good for others? I always lived as I pleased. No need to be dramatic about it.”

“That’s true.”

“He went too far. No, he went way too far.”

“A person should care somewhat about others’ eyes. How can anyone live like that?”

“Seriously these bastards?”

When Chung Myung glared, the Five Swords flinched and looked away. After scolding them with his stare, Chung Myung returned his gaze to Tang Gunak.

He knew too. Why Tang Gunak wanted to go to Dian-cang now.

But Chung Myung would never agree to that.

He knew how a life turns into hell when one sacrifices what should not be sacrificed to achieve a cause. He understood how painfully agonizing that was.

So he could not push Tang Gunak into that hell.

“‘I will die for the world.’ That’s a hero. I admire such people. But……”

Chung Myung’s face contorted.

“Anyone who shouts that you must die for the world is just a fool and an imbecile. Especially if it’s their own family on the line.”

Beneath his wide sleeve, Tang Gunak’s hand trembled faintly.

“And if a friend acts like an idiot, you should rattle him awake by beating some sense into him. Isn’t that right?”

Under Chung Myung’s unwavering gaze, Tang Gunak finally closed his eyes gently.

“You.”

“Yes.”

When he opened his eyes again, Tang Gunak’s gaze burned like fire.

“Do you truly have the resolve to bear all of this?”

“……”

“Is this not simply out of pity for us, but with the determination to shoulder all responsibility? Is that really so?”

Instead of answering, Chung Myung shouted.

“Sasuk!”

“Yes!”

Baek Cheon threw the Dark Fragrant Plum Blossom Sword he had been carrying to Chung Myung. Chung Myung caught the sword, drew it in an instant, and swung it. The blade traced a clear line and touched Tang Gunak’s throat.

The green tassel hanging from the halted tip of the blade shook violently.

Chung Myung growled as he spoke.

“If I intended to shirk responsibility, I wouldn’t have taken up the sword.”

Clang!

The sword was cleanly sheathed and slid back into its scabbard. Chung Myung’s sword, made by the Tang Family, hung again at his waist—exactly where it belonged.

“So stop the pointless talk and say what you need to say again. Say what you should have said as the Family Lord of the Tang Family.”

A complex, strange light flickered in Tang Gunak’s eyes. His fist, clenched beneath his sleeve, turned white at the knuckles.

The thing he wanted to say most—yet couldn’t bring himself to say.

Tang Gunak bowed his head deeply.

“Go to the Tang Family. The Tang Family……”

He swallowed the rising emotion and spoke again.

“Help the Tang Family.”

“Anytime.”

Chung Myung gripped Tang Gunak’s shoulder tightly, spun on his heel, and shouted toward Mount Hua.

“Did you hear that, you brats!”

“Oh!”

“No time to waste! Let’s go!”

A blade-like momentum burst forth once more. The Tang Family elite, finally allowed to go where they needed to, lifted their spirits with all their might.

The exhausted gathered regained vitality like a taut bowstring. It was a change too great to be the work of a single person.

“Hurry up, Chung Myung!”

“You were such a pain, but you pulled yourself together! You’re alright now, right?”

“It won’t be too late if we go now, right?”

While people smiled and spoke to Chung Myung here and there, Jo Gul also ran up with a bright face. Seeing him, Chung Myung grinned.

“Before that, sahyung.”

“Huh?”

“Carry me on your back.”

“……Huh?”

“My legs went weak.”

“……”

“Quick.”

……Is this guy really okay? Jo Gul’s shoulders slumped lifelessly.

Chapter 1430: So Is This Dagger A Failure? (5)

“Ugh…”

“…”

“Uuuugh…”

“…”

“Uuuuuugh…”

“Oh, really! You’re disgustingly loud!”

Chung Myung, who was being carried, snapped and slapped the head of Jo Gul in front of him.

“Is carrying one wounded person such a problem? Huh?”

“I- I’m wounded too, you bastard!”

“Judging by how you’re shouting, you’re perfectly fine!”

“Uuuugh.”

Tears welled in the corner of Jo Gul’s eyes.

“Why me, why! There are plenty of perfectly fine people over there!”

“They can’t. They’re inconvenient for me. They’re running at full strength and must be exhausted, too, people should have conscience, how could they be asked carry me when they are like that? Wow, sahyung’s character.”

“Then what about me? Is it not inconvenient for me? Is it fine for me to suffer?”

“Yeah.”

Jo Gul’s head dropped limply as if shot down in one breath. Chung Myung snickered.

“Sahyung can have some hardship. You grew up comfortable.”

“If I suffer any more I’ll actually die, you bastard!”

“Enough! If you have time to mouth off, run faster! We’re busy right now!”

“Uuuugh.”

Jo Gul swallowed his tears and dashed forward.

It’s irritating, but not wrong. There’s no time to complain now. Even at this moment, those Ten Thousand People Clan guys are probably chasing the fleeing Tang Family people.

Haven’t they already painfully experienced how relentless and horrific their pursuit is? Now just the sight of Ho Ga-Myung’s face can make one shake with fear.

“Heave-ho!”

Jo Gul pushed off the ground and shot forward in an instant.

“But, sahyung.”

“Why?”

“Are you okay? There’s the Four Seas Merchant Guild in Chengdu. How about at least you going to Chengdu?”

“Not really…”

Jo Gul twisted his face.

“What kind of bastard is Jang Ilso, if he was going to cause trouble, he’d have done it already!”

“…”

“And don’t underestimate our merchant guild! Do you know how hard it is to make a living trading across the Central Plains? They’ve weathered crises like this many times!”

“How proud.”

Chung Myung snorted. Actually, he wasn’t that worried either. The report the Beggars’ Union delivered surely came under the orders of Hong Dae-Kwang or Neung Sam. If it were them, they’d surely have included information about the Four Seas Merchant Guild.

The lack of any mention of the trading house meant there likely wasn’t anything unusual there. If there had been even a single character about the trading house, Chung Myung would have sent Jo Gul to Chengdu even if he had to beat him into going.

Jo Gul added.

“And anyway, if they can’t be stopped then no one in Chengdu will be safe. Evacuating only my family won’t be the end. If you’re going to protect people, do it properly!”

Chung Myung, who had been quietly listening, looked down at Jo Gul. Somehow Jo Gul’s shoulders carrying him looked noticeably broader.

At that moment, Baek Cheon quietly approached Chung Myung.

“Chung Myung.”

“Yeah?”

“It’s not too late, right?”

At those words, Chung Myung’s gaze darkened slightly.

To be honest, Chung Myung didn’t know either. If he’d remained conscious and had been monitoring the situation he could have roughly calculated it, but from what he’d heard now the information was too scarce.

Maybe those bastards had already caught up to the Tang Family, or maybe there was still plenty of time.

But either way, what they had to do didn’t change.

“If it’s late, should we turn back?”

“That’s nonsense.”

“Right. That’s settled then. Clear your head and just run. Whether we’re late or not we can check when we arrive.”

Baek Cheon nodded. Hearing those words made the noisy thoughts in his head seem to vanish.

Then Chung Myung, who had been staring at Baek Cheon, spoke.

“By the way… your face is awfully pale. You’re going to collapse soon, aren’t you?”

“Huh? Sasuk’s face is naturally pale, isn’t it?”

“Sahyung’s noisy. Just run quickly.”

Baek Cheon glanced at Chung Myung and smiled.

“Who’s worrying about whom right now? You weakling.”

“…Wow, look at that pathetic face.”

Chung Myung grumbled but smiled and turned his gaze away.

“You won’t listen even if we try to stop you. Just hold on as long as you can. If you can’t, then just collapse.”

“Worry about yourself, you damned fool.”

A bitter smile formed at the corner of Baek Cheon’s mouth.

‘He’s like a ghost, really.’

That guy’s body probably isn’t normal either. Tang Gunak poured the family’s treasure into his treatment, but objectively it looked like they barely kept a corpse breathing. Didn’t Tang Gunak himself say that he had barely saved Chung Myung?

And yet in the meantime he assessed the situation, made decisions, and even worried about others…

It was a strange feeling. The situation hadn’t really changed. They were still heading to the Tang Family, and the Tang Family was still in danger. In fact, the situation had probably become more urgent.

Yet despite that, the feeling was noticeably different from before.

Staring blankly at Chung Myung on Jo Gul’s back, Baek Cheon clicked his tongue.

‘I can’t handle this, really.’

Everyone here would know: Chung Myung right now could not display even a quarter of his true ability.

No, that’s an overestimate. Perhaps the current Chung Myung is no different from an ordinary commoner who cannot wield a sword.

Yet confidence rose. Simply the fact that that damned bastard had regained consciousness.

Not only the disciples of the Five Swords and Mount Hua, but even Hyun Jong and the elders of Mount Hua, and even Tang Gunak—the atmosphere had changed.

Still urgent, but not frantic. The intense anxiety that had enveloped them seemed to have noticeably lifted.

“No, run faster! Haven’t you even eaten porridge? You bastards, I told you to pay attention to your pacing technique! If you end up late, you lot, when I get back…”

Baek Cheon squeezed his eyes shut.

‘I wish I could just shut that mouth of his.’

Of course, that was a wish that could never come true.

“That’s why I’m saying, huh? From the start…”

“Come on! Run faster, more!”

“Yes!”

When Baek Cheon quickly cut off Chung Myung’s nagging and shouted, Mount Hua and the Tang Family increased their speed.

Chung Myung snorted and looked ahead. His gaze darkened and felt differently than before.

❀ ❀ ❀

Tang Byeok looked back at Tang Wei with worried eyes.

“Elder! Are you, a-are you okay? Let me carry you!”

And for good reason: Tang Wei’s face was soaked in sweat pouring like a waterfall, as if he could lose consciousness and collapse at any moment. But Tang Wei rebuffed Tang Byeok with a stubborn expression.

“Don’t do that useless stunt.”

“B-but…”

“If you’ve got energy to spare, take care of the women and children! I’m fine!”

Tang Byeok looked at Tang Wei with slightly startled eyes.

To anyone’s eye, he didn’t look fine. But once Tang Wei insisted on coming out like this, Tang Byeok could do nothing.

“We must go faster! Those Evil Sect bastards are probably chasing us even now. We have to reach Shaanxi before they catch up.”

“Yes!”

After staggering once, Tang Wei pushed away Tang Byeok’s hand trying to help and looked back. Many of the Tang Family’s elders were following behind him.

“You stupid fools! Don’t leave the children to their mothers, carry them yourselves!”

“W-we’re already doing that.”

“Do the elders have no hands? No backs? Are you saving that precious strength to use in the afterlife? Why aren’t you moving?”

“U-understood!”

Watching Tang Byeok dash off, Tang Wei drew a deep breath. Wiping his face, sweat remained as if he’d plunged his hand into water.

But he absolutely could not stop moving.

He still believed this choice was wrong. The place where Tang Family members should die is within the Tang Family’s Central Plains. The Tang Family’s poisons, the Tang Family’s workshop, these must never be lost.

But since it had come to this, they had to protect whatever remained. If they couldn’t, they’d be nothing but people who threw everything away with a foolish choice.

At least when he died and faced his ancestors, he wanted to be able to say he did his best.

“Huk! Huk!”

Tang Wei spat rough breaths and desperately moved his trembling legs.

‘Shaanxi…’

Jang Ilso probably already treated Sichuan as his own land now. And in fact it had become hard to deny that.

But Shaanxi was different. Shaanxi’s remaining land to the Four Sects Alliance would be hard to invade. If they could only reach there, they’d be able to shake off the pursuit.

There’s one key point. Can they reach Shaanxi before those Evil Sect scum catch up to their backs? That’s the only thing.

‘More…’

Tang Wei bit his lip and shouted in a clear, ringing voice.

“Not much left! We just need to get to Shaanxi. Everyone, muster your strength!”

He didn’t expect answers. It’s better to gather the strength to take another step than to shout back.

‘How far is it? 150 km? 250 km?’

If they had all been Tang Family men, they could have broken through that distance quickly. But most of those left were children and women who had not learned martial arts.

Even Tang Family women, even those of direct lineage, don’t learn martial arts. The children of the family head’s line are taught simple palm techniques and mind methods for self-defense, but that’s all.

‘Customs.’

Yes, that’s the Tang Family’s code, ancestral rules passed down generations that must never be broken.

But… if these Tang daughters had trained in martial arts like men, would they have had to undertake such a desperate flight?

No, even if that could not have helped them much, did they have to flee in such a frantic way?

For a moment Tang Wei’s mind conjured the face of Tang Soso, Tang Gunak’s daughter, the girl who rejected the Tang Family’s code and left for Mount Hua.

When he heard of that girl’s choice, Tang Wei had poured scorn and contempt on her. He had thought she was exactly the sort of daughter Tang Gunak would have.

But the reputation that girl carved out as a member of Mount Hua resounded across the land and soon reached Tang Wei’s ears.

What if that girl hadn’t made that choice and had remained with the Tang Family?

She probably would have been nothing more than someone helping these exhausted women now.

Was she special?

Tang Wei looked at the Tang daughters anew.

Maybe not. Perhaps those here could all have lived like Tang Soso. If so…

“…No.”

But Tang Wei shook his head soon after.

The ancestors’ choice was not wrong. Women would eventually leave the Tang Family and become members of other houses. If they were taught the family’s poisons and stealth techniques, the powerful families of the land would eventually develop immunity to the Tang Family’s poisons.

Isn’t avoiding such a situation only natural?

Do you think the ancestors had no doubts? Their conclusion was reached after deep thought. Judging that choice wrong because circumstances changed would be the arrogance of later generations.

Yes, what he must do is the Tang Family…

“Eh?”

At that moment, Tang Wei’s outstretched foot caught on a rock. His old body lurched forward. Just as he was about to fall, someone grabbed his arm tightly.

“Are you alright, Elder?”

Tang Wei turned his head and looked at the person holding his arm.

‘Who is it?’

He didn’t know. He knew the face, but not the name.

There were so many people in the Tang Family. He only knew she was someone’s daughter.

He felt the hand gripping his arm tremble slightly, not from fear, but from exertion. Even though she was too exhausted to steady herself, she had been watching Tang Wei and reached out.

“I-I’m fine.”

With the strong pull that helped him up, Tang Wei steadied his legs again. He let out a small breath and stared blankly at the Tang daughter. Her face looked simple and honest.

Chapter 1431: You Will Have To Kill Me First. (1)

“Lean on me.”

“…I’m fine.”

“I’ll help you.”

A firm strength was felt in the hand that gripped his arm. A strange impulse to resist rose for a moment, but in the end Tang Wei could not pull the woman’s hand off his arm.

He knew that if he weakened and delayed, others would be endangered as well.

“Cough, cough.”

Tang Wei let out a small cough and gave a bitter smile.

‘Well, this is just fine.’

Since the previous Family Lord died… no, perhaps even before then, Tang Wei had been the de facto ruler who held real power in the Tang Family. And yet now he was being supported by a woman who had never trained in martial arts.

Would the situation have been different if his martial arts hadn’t been sealed?

Could he at least have prevented the absurd scene of the world’s Sichuan Tang Family fleeing from the Evil Sect bastards?

‘No, probably not.’

Tang Wei shook his head without realizing it.

This situation wasn’t something one person could stop. Even if he hadn’t lost his martial prowess, nothing would have changed.

‘Pathetic.’

He had believed that everything he did was for the Tang Family. He thought the current Family Lord—stern in appearance but weak at heart—would never be able to revive the Tang Family.

Yet in the end he fled, having lost the Tang Family’s foundations: poison and the workshop. Where had it all gone wrong?

“Cough! Cough!”

“Are you all right, Elder?”

“…I’m fine.”

Tang Wei weakly lowered the hand that had been covering his mouth. There were no clear traces of coughing up blood, but he could sense instinctively that his life wouldn’t last much longer.

What will happen to the Tang Family when he dies? In the coming wars that will sweep the land, can a family that has lost its roots survive?

Even if survival is somehow possible, could they ever regain their former glory? The Tang Family without poison and its secret arts?

It was suffocating. It felt as if a huge lump of iron pressed down on his chest.

Then the hand on his arm pulled him a little more strongly. Tang Wei couldn’t help but turn his gaze.

“Lean on me a little more.”

“…Alright.”

“I still have strength left. Don’t worry.”

For a moment Tang Wei was lost for words.

‘She still has strength?’

That couldn’t be.

Tang Wei had lost his martial arts and was left weak, but this woman, who had never trained in martial arts in her life, should be just as exhausted as him.

At least Tang Wei had endured pain while training; the Tang Family’s daughters hardly ever left the inner chambers.

Yet the woman calmly spoke these words.

‘She’s strong.’

Somehow a strange feeling seeped into Tang Wei’s chest.

“Thank you.”

“…Yes?”

A bewildered voice answered, but Tang Wei said nothing more and gently closed his eyes.

‘Elder.’

The image of Tang Jo-Pyeong came to his mind.

Tang Jo-Pyeong had said that what the Tang Family must protect is not poison or the workshop, but people themselves.

Honestly, Tang Wei still couldn’t fully agree with that. But he understood what Tang Jo-Pyeong meant: even with the world’s finest poisons or unrivaled secret arts, they’re useless without people to wield them.

Perhaps what had protected the Tang Family until now was the resilience of these nameless people.

“Whose daughter are you?”

“My father’s courtesy name is Go-Wei.”

“Tang Go-Wei…”

It sounded unfamiliar, so she must be from a branch line. Tang Jo-Pyeong glanced at the woman’s face.

Was she only about eighteen? Her features were ordinary, but her expression was unmistakably resolute.

But Tang Jo-Pyeong noticed an anxiety she couldn’t fully hide.

“Don’t worry.”

“What?”

“The Tang Family will rise again.”

Tang Wei forced strength into his voice and spoke. Perhaps this wasn’t to comfort the girl so much as to convince himself.

“Even if the clan burns, even if we lose our poisons and secret arts, the Tang Family is still the Tang Family.”

Tang Wei hesitated for a moment, then continued.

“The Family Lord is a fine man; he will surely restore the family.”

When he finished, he gave a laugh in his heart. He never expected he’d say such words while still alive.

‘Family Lord.’

Yet he had no regret about speaking those words aloud.

Now Tang Gunak would have to endure long suffering.

Thinking of the hardships he would face, even Tang Wei—who wasn’t particularly fond of Tang Gunak—couldn’t help but sigh.

“If you continue to trust and follow the Family Lord, one day the Tang Family will once again be the ruler of Sichuan.”

Even Tang Wei, who had spoken, didn’t think it was especially persuasive. But he believed such words were what the girls needed in this moment.

However… the reply he received was a little different from what he expected.

“The Tang Family… yes, the Tang Family will be alright.”

“That it should.”

“But… I won’t be able to help the Family Lord, Elder.”

A little taken aback, Tang Wei looked at the woman and asked.

“What do you mean by that?”

“Because there’s no way I can help. I’m of marriageable age now.”

Tang Wei’s face twisted oddly. She’s of marrying age. Saying that now meant she would become an outsider and the Tang Family would matter little to her?

Everyone looks after themselves first. But hearing this from a member of the Tang Family was quite a shock.

Tang Wei stared at her and said.

“Of course that is a woman’s life, but… shouldn’t the family come first now?”

“If I marry, I will not be a Tang Family person anymore.”

“…”

“That’s how I was taught. When you marry, you must forget the Tang Family entirely. Even the smallest memories from here must be wiped away.”

Tang Wei was momentarily at a loss for words.

“Well…“

What she spoke of was the Tang Family’s rites. It was their effort to preserve the Tang vision, and the one who most revered and upheld those rites since the ancestors was Tang Wei.

But the very words he had repeated so often were now coming back to him.

“…Of course, but aren’t you still a member of the Tang Family?”

“But… ah.”

The woman who had been about to continue looked at Tang Wei with a little start, then lowered her head slightly.

“I’m sorry. I…”

It seemed awkward to reveal her feelings before Tang Wei. The usual Tang Wei would have clicked his tongue in displeasure and turned away.

But for some reason Tang Wei wanted to hear the words she held back. Perhaps because he had little time left, or because she still supported him steadfastly.

“My child.”

“…Yes?”

“Can you tell me what you were about to say?”

“I…”

“Please.”

The woman, seeming to ponder, sighed and opened her mouth.

“Elder, you may not know, but when things get hard, girls like us are quickly given matches. They reduce the useless mouths to feed.”

Tang Wei frowned slightly. But hearing it, it wasn’t entirely wrong.

Women in the Tang Family are of little practical help, so it’s hard to lead them to revive the clan. Usually they’d marry them off even to houses they’d normally not consider, in return extracting support for the Tang Family.

“And even if that doesn’t happen, girls like us are of no use.”

“Of no use?”

“All we can do is cook and wash clothes, at best.”

“…”

“Women married in from great houses could at least swing a sword or throw a punch in a crisis. We can’t even do that.”

At a loss for words, Tang Wei looked at her and barely managed to ask.

“…Do you resent us?”

She shook her head at that.

“No. I’m not saying this out of resentment. We also want to help the Family Lord. But there really is no way we can help.”

Tang Wei felt a weight in his chest. He wanted to say that even small tasks were help, but he couldn’t—he knew better than anyone that such tasks wouldn’t really help Tang Gunak.

Household dependents—an unsuitable term for a prestigious family, but in this situation the Tang Family daughters were merely dependents.

It was the Tang Family itself—and Tang Wei—that had made them that way.

“So… are you planning to look for a match right away?”

The woman smiled brightly at Tang Wei’s question, though the smile looked forced.

“That could be an option, but who would willingly take in a branch-line daughter from a struggling Tang Family?”

Tang Wei’s throat tickled and he was about to cough dryly.

“So… once we reach Shaanxi, I’m thinking of leaving the family.”

“Leave the family? What do you mean?”

“I plan to go to Mount Hua.”

“…What?”

For a moment Tang Wei forgot to cough and his eyes widened.

What did she mean? Mount Hua?

“Other places won’t take us, but Mount Hua might. Sister Soso is already there, too.”

“You mean to entrust yourselves to Mount Hua?”

“No. I… want to learn the sword at Mount Hua.”

“A sword…?”

At that, the woman nodded. Her eyes sparkled.

“You wanted to learn the sword?”

“Not exactly…“

The woman hesitated a bit before speaking.

“Still, if I join Mount Hua and learn the sword, I could do something with my own strength. If needed, I could protect myself. Sister Soso has already shown that.”

“…”

“If sister Soso had stayed in the Tang Family, by now she would have married into a powerful family in Sichuan. She would have lived a completely different life. Everyone thought of her as strange, yet secretly envied her.”

Her calm words left an impression on Tang Wei’s mind.

“Could we live like that if we went to Mount Hua? Could there be a day we’re called by our own names, not just ‘daughter of the Tang Family’? Until now I lacked the courage. We’re not like sister Soso.”

“So…“

“Yes. This time I’ll try to be brave. I know Mount Hua is strict and training is hard, but there I think I could try something. The Family Lord wouldn’t stop us if we said we were going to Mount Hua.”

With the free arm not holding Tang Wei, the woman made a motion as if swinging a sword.

“You never know. I might have a talent for the sword and become a famous heroine!”

As she spoke, her face was lit with bright hope. Then she glanced back at Tang Wei, flinched, and fell silent again.

“I’m sorry. I’ve talked too much, haven’t I?”

“…Were there many?”

“What?”

“Are there many children in the clan who think like you?”

“Well…“

“Don’t be afraid; speak. What could you fear from an old man who has lost his strength and authority?”

The woman scratched her forehead with a finger and said.

“Not that many, but not so few either. The direct-line sisters can still find other paths, but for us there’s no other way now.”

Suddenly Tang Wei looked up at the sky in silence.

It was truly crystal clear. The vast blue sky felt especially bleak today.

“What is your name?”

“Me…? I…”

“I’m not going to punish you. I just want to know.”

Reassured, the woman nodded.

“I’m Sobo like the little treasure. But my mother calls me Sobo like the baby steps.”

“Sobo… A small step.”

Tang Wei’s mouth twitched slightly. After a long silence he was about to say something.

“Great Elder!”

A frantic shout came from behind. Startled, Tang Wei turned around hastily.

“Rear! The Ten Thousand People Clan is at the rear! They’re coming!”

All color drained from his wrinkled face.

Chapter 1432: You Will Have To Kill Me First. (2)

“Distance! How far are they!”

“I‑I can’t measure it precisely, but they’re not far!”

“Damn!”

Tang Wei bit his lip.

If his martial arts hadn’t been sealed, he could have gauged the enemies’ skill and distance, but now he was as blind as one with open eyes and no sight.

“Hurry! We must put distance between us!”

“G-great Elder!”

The Elders looked at Tang Wei with alarmed faces.

“Elder! We have people with us who haven’t trained in martial arts. How do you expect to put distance between us and them? They’ll catch up soon!”

“So? Do you have any other plan?”

Tang Wei shouted at the top of his lungs.

“We are the Sichuan Tang Family! We resist and fight to the very end! Do you not know that?”

The Elders bit their lips. Tang Wei’s gaze turned icy in an instant as he noticed their lack of resolve.

“Why? You’re all old now and suddenly value your lives? Will you abandon the family and just run away yourselves?”

“W‑what are you saying? Great Elder, that’s not it…”

“If it’s not that then what?”

One of the Elders hesitated, then spoke.

“Wouldn’t surrendering be better?”

“…What?”

Tang Wei’s face went blank as if he’d been struck.

Surrender, a word he’d never once considered in his life. Now it had come from the mouth of a Tang Family Elder.

“What did you say just now?”

One of the Elders who’d taken Tang Wei’s rising fury head‑on averted his eyes slightly and continued.

“As far as I know, Hegemony Lord is cruel, but he doesn’t harm those who haven’t trained in martial arts. Would he really slaughter us?”

“…”

“If we fight a half‑hearted battle here, we’ll give them an excuse to kill. It’s better to surrender…”

“Have you finished spouting nonsense?”

“…Great Elder.”

“You idiot!”

Tang Wei’s face flushed crimson.

“Is that what an Elder of the Tang Family should say?”

But this time the Elder did not cower at Tang Wei’s rebuke.

“I say it as an Elder of the Tang Family! You know as well as I do, Chief Elder, that we cannot stop them with only us!”

“W‑well…”

“If there is something to protect, we’ll fight with our lives! But there’s nothing left to protect in the Tang Family, is there? Why risk lives? Shouldn’t we try to save at least one who carries the Tang name?”

Tang Wei, who had been staring at the Elder, suddenly felt his strength drain.

It wasn’t anger or disappointment. The words—that there was nothing left to protect in the Tang Family—struck his core.

If they’d fought within the Tang Family before, there’d have been a just cause. But now, as was said… there is no reason. Do women and children who never trained have any reason to risk their lives?

If the point is to survive, shouldn’t one choose the path with the highest chance of survival?

“Hegemony Lord is cruel, true… but he is distinctly different from the Evil Sect men we’ve known. So perhaps he’ll spare them…”

“No!”

At that moment, Tang Sobu—who had been supporting Tang Wei—spoke firmly.

“That cannot be, Elder.”

“How dare you!”

For a moment a flash of anger crossed the Elder’s face. How dare a nameless girl interrupt his conversation with Tang Wei? That was unacceptable in the Tang Family.

“What do you know to dare open your mouth?”

“I know nothing. But I know one thing: there is a life worse than death.”

“What did you say?”

“If we surrender to them, what about the Family Lord? Those who have left the family—how could they fight them?”

The Elder was momentarily at a loss and stared at Tang Sobu in bewilderment.

“The Sichuan Tang Family would rather die miserably than live meekly. Isn’t that what you Elders said? How can you tell us to become hostages now?”

Tang Sobu’s cry filled the eyes of those around her with killing intent.

They had left their own families to become part of the Tang Family. Someday they might have to leave again; because of that they had never been fully accepted as true Tang, yet they remained as people of the Sichuan Tang Family.

“Then what of the children? Can you decide the fate of those who haven’t even been taught a thing?”

Tang Sobu faltered for a moment at the Elder’s question. The Elder then turned to Tang Wei and spoke.

“If it’s that difficult, then at least take the children and run…”

“Enough!”

“Great Elder! Think carefully! If the Tang Family’s women are taken like this…”

“Didn’t I tell you to stop!”

At that instant Tang Wei shouted and cut the Elder off.

“Those who wish to leave should go. The Tang Family will not call you back. Who can blame you for saving your own lives?”

“Great Elder! How can you take my words like that?”

“Ha… hah.”

A hollow laugh escaped Tang Wei’s lips.

The Elder’s eyes were certain his words were not wrong. It was the same look Tang Wei had seen in his past.

“The young… they cannot be trusted.”

“…Yes?”

“What can those who haven’t lived long know? I believed only those who’d endured life’s hardships deserved to lead the family.”

“Great Elder?”

“Yes, I thought that. I believed that women who never trained in the Tang Family’s martial arts, or those young and naive about the world, should simply endure and bear it.”

Tang Wei looked down at Tang Sobu. Though she showed clear signs of exhaustion, her eyes remained bright and clear.

“…I’ve wasted my life.”

“G-great Elder.”

“Growing old doesn’t make one wise, and being young doesn’t make one foolish. I thought I must somehow lead those childish ones. The real fool was me all along though.”

“I…”

Tang Wei shook his head.

“There is no more time for words. Those who will leave, leave now! And those Elders of the Tang Family who choose to die, hold the rear and stop the Ten Thousand People Clan! The Tang Family fights to the very end. That is the duty of those who bear the Tang name!”

A resolute light filled Tang Wei’s eyes. The Elders, infected by that look, answered firmly.

“Yes, Great Elder!”

Tang Wei turned his body, as if determined not to look back.

“Everyone, hurry! The enemies are pursuing us.”

Having urged the tired people on, Tang Wei gripped Tang Sobu’s arm.

“Let’s go!”

“Yes.”

Tang Sobu supported Tang Wei and led him out.

A rueful smile kept escaping Tang Wei’s lips. Hustle and bustle could be felt from behind. Most Elders would have firmly taken the rear to hold the ground, but perhaps a few were already running away somewhere.

But Tang Wei refused to look back. He did not want to know who stayed and who left. Whoever left, it was ultimately his responsibility, was it not?

‘The Tang Family.’

Who is the enemy of the Tang Family?

Are those who chase them with swords the Tang Family’s enemy?

Or is the enemy the one who divided the Tang Family into martial and non‑martial, men and women, the young and the old, tearing it into pieces?

‘Father…’

The previous Family Lord told Tang Wei he was never fit to be Family Lord. Despite being superior in skill and strategy to his younger brother, he was not to become the Family Lord.

He couldn’t understand it, so he spent his life angry and resentful, devoting himself to proving those words wrong.

But now at last he seemed to understand.

‘I was not fit.’

He should never have been the Tang Family’s Family Lord.

He had divided everything within the Tang Family, strictly categorizing and drawing boundaries.

For a time, that may have seemed efficient and better.

As young Tang Wei once lamented, that decision may have strengthened the Sichuan Tang Family’s name for a while.

But now Tang Wei had seen what that path led to.

‘Family Lord.’

He had long resented Tang Gunak.

He thought that if Tang Gunak hadn’t foolishly allied with Mount Hua, the Tang Family wouldn’t be in this crisis.

But perhaps the one who created the Tang Family’s present crisis was none other than Tang Wei himself.

If he had not interfered and had trusted what Tang Gunak sought to achieve, the Tang Family might have become stronger and greater than it was now.

“Ahem!”

“Now then, Elder. Are you all right?”

Tang Wei turned his head and looked at Tang Sobu.

“…I’m sorry.”

“Don’t say that. Stay strong a little longer!”

Tang Wei twisted the corner of his mouth.

‘Too… too late to realize.’

The image of Tang Jo‑Pyeong roaring loudly flashed by. Probably he wanted to say this; his crude speech honed by years of forging steel couldn’t convey it, but he’d expressed it with his gaze and gestures.

Maybe he had been shouting all along, but Tang Wei did not hear. No matter how much they clamored around him, one who plugs his ears hears nothing!

If only he had known this a little sooner…

“They, they’re coming! They’re coming! Great Elder!”

Tang Wei couldn’t help but turn around for a moment. He tried not to look, but he had no choice.

A red host racing in from beyond the distant horizon.

Their red figures spreading across the ochre earth were terrifying in themselves. But Tang Wei did not look at them—he looked at the man at their center.

The man’s red cloak fluttered in the air. A pained voice escaped Tang Wei.

“Hege…mony Lord.”

He had wanted to see him once with his own eyes, but never like this. Not at least like now.

Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso’s eyes seemed to pierce him chillingly even from this distance.

“Alliance lord! They’re here.”

Jang Ilso lightly scratched his brow with a fingernail.

“Hmm, is that all? The Tang Family is finished.”

“There are hardly any who can fight! At most a few old men!”

“So it seems.”

“Take them alive?”

Jeok Ho glanced at Jang Ilso. It was a natural question. Killing them would be trivial, but taking them hostage to pressure Tang Gunak and the Heavenly Friends Alliance would be far more useful.

But only a click of Jang Ilso’s tongue returned.

“Tsk tsk. That’s why the Evil Sect are like this.”

“S‑sorry.”

“Look.”

Jang Ilso gestured forward with his chin. Jeok Ho watched that direction. The Tang Family’s Elders were moving to the rear and radiating momentum, like enraged wildcats.

“What are you looking at? Not there. Look beyond them.”

“…Yes?”

At that, Jeok Ho looked past the Elders to those behind them.

Women and children bursting into tears.

And only craftsmen who hadn’t trained in martial arts, panting as if they might perish at any moment.

“Do you see?”

“…What do you mean?”

“If you want to know whether someone is an enemy, look at their eyes, not their strength. Look—so many eyes are speaking. They say they’ll fight.”

At those words, Jeok Ho couldn’t help but nod.

Ordinarily, those without martial training would be terrified under pursuit by the Ten Thousand People Clan. But the eyes of the women and craftsmen looking this way were filled with murderous intent.

“Strong or weak doesn’t matter. Those who step onto this land with the will to fight are all warriors.”

“…”

“And if the opponent is a warrior, we must show them respect. Understand?”

“Yes, Alliance lord!”

“Good.”

A sly smile curved Jang Ilso’s mouth.

“Kill them all. Leave not a single one.”

“At your command!”

When Jeok Ho gestured, the Ten Thousand People Clan surged forward.

“Hyaaaargh!”

Watching them charge, Jang Ilso murmured softly.

“It seems a bit much for the price of a drink, but… this is the Evil Sect’s arithmetic, so understand it.”

His oily eyes curved like a waning moon.

Chapter 1433: You Will Have To Kill Me First. (3)

“Oh, they’re coming!”

The blood drained from the faces of the Tang Family elders.

They had stepped forward with resolve in their hearts. But the moment they saw the Ten Thousand People Clan fiends charging like beasts, the three-letter word “fear” surged over them.

“Evil Sect bastards who wouldn’t be enough even if we chewed them up!”

Ordinary people would have been consumed by that fear, but these were the elders of the Sichuan Tang Family. Though they had not faced proper combat their whole lives, the effort etched into their bodies and the pride carved into their chests did not vanish.

“Pull yourselves together! If we break, our family will suffer!”

Tang Byeok shouted at the top of his lungs.

‘Hold them off!’

A fierce resolve clouded his face.

The location was bad. The open plain was the worst place for the Tang Family to show their strength. Still, when it came to holding back enemies, wasn’t the Tang Family a sect unmatched under heaven?

“Scatter it!”

As the enemies closed within range, a ripping shout tore from Tang Byeok’s mouth. At the same time, dark poisonous fumes billowed from the elders’ sleeves like clouds.

The poisonous clouds rising across the windless plain formed a massive wall between the two groups.

The toxic haze exuded an eerie chill at first sight. Tang Byeok’s gaze, who had created that cloud, shot upward.

‘Hidden weapons!’

A handful of feather needles appeared in his hand. If they couldn’t break through ahead, the attackers would surely try to leap over the poisonous cloud. What they had to do was pierce a wind-hole before those unprotected bodies leapt back to the ground!

The elders who had trained together all their lives seemed to think the same; drawing up their energy, they gripped sharp hidden weapons in both hands and stared intently at the poisonous veil.

But then.

Fwahhhh!

The center of the poison cloud, tinged with an ominous color, rippled as if wrapped. Before their minds could register the change, a group clad in red had pierced the cloud and surged forward in an instant.

‘What?’

Tang Byeok, startled as if his heart would leap from his throat, hastily released hidden weapons. But before those weapons left his hands, the blades hurled by the Ten Thousand People Clan members sliced between them.

A blade struck the torso of the lead elder and sank mercilessly in.

“Aah!”

A single scream. Before it could fully sound, the body, bisected, flew and sprayed crimson blood in all directions.

“Ugh!”

Several elders were cut down by successive swings of the blades before they could even retaliate.

“R-retreat!”

“Don’t give them distance! Fall back!”

Tang Byeok’s eyes flitted wildly amid the elders’ desperate cries.

‘W-without hesitation!’

It wasn’t surprising they burst through the poison cloud with bare bodies. Poison works that way, no matter how deadly, poisoning still takes time.

What shocked Tang Byeok wasn’t that they pierced the poison cloud with bare bodies, but that there was not a moment’s hesitation in the process.

Any person would flinch at sight of a rising poison cloud. Yet these men, knowing it was the Tang Family’s lethal poison, plunged in without the slightest pause.

‘This, this is the Ten Thousand People Clan?’

“Kuhahah!”

A black aura spread across the faces of the Ten Thousand People Clan swords charging at the front. Yet the blades wielded by those darkened-faced men swung with ferocity unbefitting the poisoned.

“You, you bastards!”

The Tang elders weren’t idle either. Their feather needles, released with full force, rained down and pierced the Ten Thousand People Clan fighters; those who took hundreds of poisonous needles became like hedgehogs in an instant.

However.

Fwahhhh!

Still, a swung blade plunged straight into the chest of the elder standing there.

“Ughh…”

A Ten Thousand People Clan blade pressed its head against the shuddering elder’s shoulder, as if tearing him vertically. Wearing an indescribable grin, the fighter twisted the blade buried in the elder’s chest with all his might.

“Aaaaaaah!”

The pain of organs being shredded within the body, unable to bear it, the elder let out a heartrending scream.

“Tang Muuuuu!”

Even though they watched their sahyung-sajaes dying right before their eyes, the elders could do nothing. Fiends were already upon them too.

“Aaaaah!”

Short blades like lightning shot from the elders’ hands. These close-range blades pierced the enemies’ chests in an instant, but the Ten Thousand People Clan fighters still drove the blades into their own chests and stubbornly swung their swords to cut them down.

Slash! Slash!

The swung swords severed the elders’ arms and cut through their necks.

There was no time even to spread poison. With madmen right before them rolling their eyes and swinging blades, where was the leisure to poison them slowly?

Nor could hidden weapons solve it. The hidden weapons they threw surely landed on enemies, but that was all. There was no way to stop men who continued swinging swords with weapons lodged in their bodies.

“Aim for the head! Aim for the head! Kill them instantly!”

Tang Byeok roared, straining.

But the enemies were no fools. They ignored weapons stuck in their bodies but thoroughly blocked those flying toward their heads and hearts.

And in that moment Tang Byeok realized his mistake.

They should have created distance. They shouldn’t have clumsily stood their ground to hold them. The Tang Family, who rely on hidden weapons rather than close combat, and who subdue enemies by scattering poison, inevitably lack stopping power.

The moment they allowed the enemy swords to reach them, the outcome was essentially decided.

“Kraaaaaah!”

At that moment, two enemies lunged toward Tang Byeok. He gritted his teeth, and poisonous intent flared in his eyes.

“You fucking bastards!”

From his sleeve Tang Byeok drew something like lightning and leveled it at the attackers.

And then.

Kwaaaaang!

A tremendous blast exploded and swept across the area.

“What?”

Even the Ten Thousand People Clan elites, who had been raging as if the Tang Family’s poison and flying weapons were invisible, turned their heads in shock. What they saw were three bodies shredded so badly they no longer resembled human forms.

“Don’t underestimate the Sichuan Tang Family!”

Tang Byeok shouted at the top of his lungs.

“Don’t panic, use the thunder cannon! Pour everything you’ve got into it!”

No sooner had he finished speaking than overwhelming detonations echoed from all around.

Wails and explosions followed, and the barren ground soon boiled with blood and death.

“Hmm?”

Jang Ilso’s eyes narrowed slightly. A small wrinkle appeared between his brows, as if he disliked the scene before him.

“Cannons?”

“Probably forbidden concealed weapons.”

“Forbidden concealed weapons…”

There’s no other reason for the term ‘forbidden’ to be attached. They are weapons that shouldn’t be used, quite literally.

The Tang Family unilaterally designates these as forbidden concealed weapons because their power is so great that using them risks drawing the world’s attention as a crime.

But in reality there was an even more sensitive reason.

“Not even the Evil Sect, but gunpowder, so the Tang Family must have been desperate, huh?”

Jang Ilso chuckled.

No matter how small the cannon, the use of gunpowder is the same. Gunpowder inevitably invites official intervention. That’s why even the Evil Sect avoids touching it unless it’s on the Yangtze, where official involvement can be minimized.

Yet the Tang Family elder brought out a weapon that used gunpowder.

“The damage will be great.”

Jeok Ho’s concern was by no means exaggerated. Whatever the reason, it was clear the Tang Family’s forbidden concealed weapons possessed staggering power.

Those forbidden concealed weapons were not simple cannons. They combined gunpowder’s force and internal qi to rapidly throw the Tang Family’s special hidden weapons.

If you charged directly into that, the result would be obvious, wouldn’t it?

“Well.”

But Jang Ilso remained completely at ease.

“That cannon.”

“Yes, Alliance lord.”

“Doesn’t it look pretty big?”

Jeok Ho narrowed his eyes at that. It certainly looked too heavy to be wielded with one hand.

“Since it uses gunpowder, they probably can’t fire many times…”

A grotesque twist shaped Jang Ilso’s lip.

“How many of those could fit inside that wide sleeve? Hidden weapons that become useless after one use.”

Jeok Ho immediately understood and nodded.

“Annihilate them!”

“Yes!”

Those around Jeok Ho sprang forward at once.

The forbidden concealed weapon they used wasn’t purely to their advantage. The blast pressure from the thunder cannon scattered the poison cloud they had created in all directions. With no chance to spread poison again, they were effectively exposed on this wide plain.

Left like this they’d collapse soon enough. But Jang Ilso had no intention of taking it so slowly.

“Red Dogs.”

The Red Dogs who guarded Jang Ilso like an iron wall all turned to look at him.

There wasn’t even a reply. A reply would only show the command was heard. Why would those who had all their attention fixed on Jang Ilso need that?

Jang Ilso slowly licked his lips with his tongue.

“How boring. Facing old men always saps my energy.”

Madness flared in the Red Dogs’ eyes.

“Clear them all out. I’m a busy man.”

At his words, the Red Dogs turned to stare at the elders of the Sichuan Tang Family.

And then.

Fwahhhh!

Without a gesture or brief reply, the Red Dogs surged. Like famished wolves finding prey on a snow-covered plain, their feral cries swept across the bleak Sichuan land.

“We’ll be back.”

Jeok Ho, as if understanding Jang Ilso’s intent, bowed deeply to him.

“Don’t get too rowdy. Let those kids have their fun too.”

“Yes, Alliance lord.”

Jeok Ho bowed once more and drew his sword.

A blackened sword, an ink blade. Gripping his favored weapon, Jeok Ho surged forward with killing intent in his eyes.

“Oh my, oh my.”

Jang Ilso shook his head.

“Everyone’s so excited, really.”

Come to think of it, it made sense. The Ten Thousand People Clan had grown through countless wars. But since the Four Sects Alliance stabilized, they hadn’t really tasted blood, had they?

What might have been a brief respite for others had been a long period of suppression for them.

“I hope it’s not too brutal…”

Gifts are good, but an excessive gift can offend the recipient.

But…

“There’s nothing to be done.”

Jang Ilso shrugged and slowly took a step.

“That’s because you arrived late. The Poison King, and… Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Jang Ilso chuckled and walked across the blood-soaked ground as if taking a stroll.

Chapter 1434: You Will Have To Kill Me First. (4)

“Y-You bastards!”

The Elders’ faces went pale when they saw the enemies charging again.

Did you not see it clearly?

How dangerous the Sichuan Tang Family’s forbidden weapon is. How formidable its power. And yet those bastards show no fear. How could they act like this if they weren’t willing to throw away their lives?

“Aaaaaah!”

They had seized momentum, if only for a moment, but sadly could not keep it. One hoarse, aged scream in their ears made them give up everything they’d gained.

“Hyunggggg!”

Those who had lived their lives as warriors of the Tang Family. Feared and dreaded across the land. The emotions that rose in their eyes now were bewilderment and fear.

Should they have fled?

Like those who ran without looking back the moment Tang Wei’s words fell, should they have abandoned this meaningless struggle and at least saved their lives?

Or even now…

“Throw the poison!”

At that moment, Tang Byeok’s screaming voice burst out.

“B-Brother! In this situation, poison is…!”

“What are you waiting for! Deploy the poison at once! You idiots! Is our goal to beat them?”

Those words snapped the Elders awake.

“We will die here. Spray poison until the moment we die, throw out the hidden weapons! We cannot stop them from trampling our corpses. But do not let them follow our household members!”

Tang Byeok shouted, his face veined with blood.

“We are Elders of the Sichuan Tang Family! Die worthy of that name!”

It usually takes a great reason to risk one’s life. But sometimes no great reason is needed. As befits Elders of the Sichuan Tang Family. What more could be needed than that?

In an instant, the Elders’ eyes, who had been looking for a way to save themselves, filled with a poisonous intensity.

“Aaaaaaaaah!”

A flying blade lodged into one of the Elders.

But even with a sharp piece of metal buried in his chest, the Tang Family Elder no longer despaired.

“Kuh…”

Just as the Ten Thousand People Clan’s elite fighters were about to contort their faces at the Tang Family Elder’s grotesque smile.

Whooaaa!

Pinkish poisonous vapors billowed up from the Elder’s sleeve.

“Gasp!”

The startled Ten Thousand People Clan’s warriors tried to pull back, but no matter how hard he tried to pull it, the blade he had stuck into the Elder’s chest did not budge, as if driven into rock.

“The Tang Family…”

The Elder’s face twisted grotesquely.

“Do not underestimate the Sichuan… Tang Family…”

Sssshhh!

Black liquid sprayed everywhere from the Tang Elder’s sleeve. The Ten Thousand People Clan swordsman hit head-on by that black poison bit his lip and grabbed the Elder’s throat.

“Useless old man!”

Crack!

The Elder’s neck snapped and he collapsed on the spot. But the Ten Thousand People Clan’s warrior who ended his life was not safe either.

“U-Urghhhhhh!”

Black blood poured from the swordsman’s mouth like a waterfall. Excruciating pain, as if struck in the head with a hammer, overwhelmed him.

“Ugh…”

His body trembled with convulsions. Had he not mustered internal qi to suppress it, his body would already have been reduced to a handful of bloody pulp.

“Spray it!”

At Tang Byeok’s shout, black poisonous vapors rose simultaneously from all around. It wasn’t the dragon-poison wall that rose before them as before; it was a dragon-poison technique that enveloped the ground where they stood.

No matter how resistant the Sichuan Tang Family Elders were to poison, they were not completely immune. To wrap themselves in poison in this situation was a resolve to risk death. No, a resolve to beckon death.

And that was not the end.

The Elders spat blood-poison into their own mouths. This poison would certainly poison them, but it would turn their blood into a deadly toxin. Those who cut their bodies would not be safe either!

Even the Ten Thousand People Clan warriors of the world faltered for a moment at such a vicious countermeasure.

But…

“Kuhahahaha!”

What those who were about to step back saw was nothing less than the Red Dogs pouring in behind them.

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors’ eyes filled with fear. If they moved forward they’d be poisoned; if they retreated they’d be certain to die. Those mad Red Dogs never considered them allies in the first place.

“Aaaaaaaah!”

The Ten Thousand People Clan warriors, trapped on all sides, screamed and charged into the rising clouds of poison.

“Come!”

The Tang Family Elders, driven to fury, shouted as if their throats would rip watching the Ten Thousand People Clan.

“We will never let them go! Never!”

“Aaaaaah!”

Tang Sobo flinched and tried to look back at the agonizing screams bursting from behind.

“Don’t look!”

Tang Wei’s shout forced her head to stay forward.

She wanted to plug her ears. She wanted to close her eyes and ignore what was happening. But she didn’t even have that luxury now.

‘I must go!’

She knew what she had to do now.

One step. Even one more step. If she didn’t, those Elders’ deaths would become meaningless.

‘I must live! Absolutely!’

Tang Sobo wiped her streaming tears and pulled Tang Wei along.

One.

“Go!”

In that moment Tang Wei pushed her from behind.

“Elder!”

Despite Tang Sobo’s urgent cry, Tang Wei remained steadfast.

“Go quickly! Now!”

“Then Elder…”

“Didn’t I tell you to go!?”

Where he found such vigor, Tang Wei let out a thunderous shout.

Tang Wei, eyes bloodshot, looked at the bewildered Tang Sobo and shoved her shoulder again.

“Go! You must survive! You must at all costs!”

“Elder!”

Tang Wei no longer urged her. He simply turned his body away.

He could clearly see the black poisonous mists rising as if to the sky’s edge. He could clearly hear the screams and shouts coming from within them.

The Elders had resolved to die.

But they would not hold out long. They were by no means weak, but their numbers were too few and the few were too formidable.

Not everyone remaining could live. Someone had to block them. Someone had to hold their feet here.

A momentary conflict flickered in Tang Wei’s eyes. The order that burst out at the end of that brief inner struggle was something the original Tang Wei would never have given.

“Send the children and women first! Now!”

“Yes! Elder!”

The men, hearing him, handed the children they were holding to the women.

“Dear!”

“Go! Quickly!”

Women crying mixed with screams were shoved by the men’s hands.

Tang Wei’s hand trembled for a moment as he watched that sight.

Is this right? Is this the right thing?

Even men untrained in martial arts, even if they had lost the workshop and the stocks of poison they’d built up—they were craftsmen who made secret armaments, researchers who manufactured poisons.

Is it right to use such people as shields for women who will not in the least help the family’s future?

He didn’t know.

Tang Wei realized he could not know everything. He could not answer everything in the world. His belief that he knew and understood more was pure arrogance.

Now he was only listening. Listening to what his heart shouted. To the voice that urged him to do something utterly foolish.

“Elder, lead the women away! The men cover behind them!”

“Yes!”

Biting his lip, Tang Wei turned his head unconsciously.

“I’m sorry.”

Tang Wei himself didn’t fully understand what those words meant, but the craftsmen who heard them instead laughed.

“A hammer wasn’t made to hit people, but can one not break a man while smashing iron?”

“…Even if we never learned martial arts, we are Tang Family people. Of course we’ll do it. If it were the other way around, I wouldn’t have followed the Elder’s orders.”

“I’ve always wanted to prove that the one who makes poison uses it best—this is as good a time as any.”

Tang Wei gave a hollow laugh at the men’s banter.

‘Have I been underestimating these people?’

They are all Tang Family. Yes, all of them.

From the child sweeping the Tang Family’s courtyard to the craftsmen waiting to die of old age—they were all Tang Family.

“Don’t talk nonsense! I’m not telling you to die here! You must survive too! Go! One more step!”

“Yes!”

Tang Wei was about to turn and run again.

Kraaaash!

With a terrifying tearing sound the poisonous mist scattered, and a man burst out wrapped in a blue aura.

A middle-aged man with oppressed-looking eyes. Jeok Ho gripped his curved blade inverse and charged at them in a single bound.

“Run! Quickly!”

“Yes!”

The Tang Family craftsmen ran desperately, but with their strength there was no way to escape the pursuit of an absolute warrior; it was a pipe dream.

The Tang Family craftsmen clenched their lips as the distance closed and the enemy surged toward them.

“We can’t go!”

The hammers in their hands were swung fiercely. The hammers they had never abandoned even while fleeing were now being used to defend the Tang Family.

But their hammers were far too weak to face that massive curved blade.

Wheee!

With a sound that seemed to crush the air, the craftsmen’s bodies were cleaved and flung up. A horrendous sight. Even then the Tang craftsmen clung to Jeok Ho’s ankles, and the pale Tang family’s poisoners desperately sprayed poison.

“Don’t get in the way!”

But Jeok Ho’s blade cut through even those like they were a group. A vicious killing intent rose in his usually composed eyes.

Taking them one by one from the rear was meaningless. The way to crush the fleeing ones at once was to break their vanguard.

Jeok Ho split apart those who blocked him and surged forward in an instant.

“Go quickly! Now!”

Seeing that, Tang Wei turned his body sharply.

“Elder!”

Tang Wei’s hand slipped out of that sleeve. Holding a dozen or so poison vials in both hands, he emptied opened vials into his mouth.

With a burning sensation in his throat, his body flared up in an instant.

‘Heaven and earth gods!’

Poison in his mouth, Tang Wei staggered and leapt at Jeok Ho. Jeok Ho’s blade calmly sliced straight through Tang Wei’s chest.

Shick!

A crimson line appeared across Tang Wei’s chest, and the focus left his eyes.

As Jeok Ho was about to pass over Tang Wei as if it were nothing, the crimson line on Tang Wei’s chest darkened black and his hands wrapped around Jeok Ho.

“Hm?”

“C-cannot go…”

Jeok Ho’s face twisted. He saw black blood flowing from Tang Wei, who was clinging to his leg.

“Vile old bastards!”

Kradash!

Jeok Ho’s curved blade plunged into Tang Wei’s back in an instant. Tang Wei convulsed, but the hands wrapped around Jeok Ho’s leg did not let go.

“Can’t… go… can’t…”

“What the—!”

Shick!

Jeok Ho’s curved blade severed Tang Wei’s arms at the shoulders.

“These damned poisoners.”

Jeok Ho ground his teeth. The clothing Tang Wei clung to was blackened and rotted away by the poison.

There was no immediate problem, but if time was dragged out they would inevitably be poisoned.

“Then don’t waste time!”

Bang!

Jeok Ho’s eyes flashed a steel-blue killing intent. In his sight was the back of a woman fleeing ahead.

‘I must go!’

Tears streamed endlessly from Tang Sobo’s eyes.

The screams behind her were so agonizing. And so heartbreaking.

Still, she had to go. She must go.

“Do not cry! I am a Tang Family person!”

Even if she had not mastered martial arts, proving oneself Tang Family was not only about martial skill. One who carries Tang blood must never show weakness before the enemy.

“Aaaaaaaah!”

Another chilling scream rang out behind her. The faces of those who turned in shock went deathly pale.

A child bursting into tears.

Someone who had simply collapsed to the ground.

Someone shouting curses like a hellish dirge.

They would all die. Every last one of them.

No matter how hard they tried, they could not survive with their strength. The deaths of all who sacrificed themselves to save them would be in vain.

Tang Sobo’s mouth fell open.

“Who…”

Somebody, please.

“Someone help! Please!”

Then—a fierce shout came from behind her.

“Die! You worthless—!”

A chill ran down her back. Sensing her fate, Tang Sobo instinctively squeezed her eyes shut.

‘Unnie!’

Kaaaaaang!

But the moment Tang Sobo faced it, it was neither pain nor peace.

It was simply a fierce gust of wind, as if something had burst and rushed across her back.

And then.

A chin.

Something touched her face.

Before she could understand what it was, a gentle voice reached her ear.

“Are you alright?”

“…”

Tang Sobo opened her eyes wide. In her sight was the figure of a man she’d never seen before.

“I’m too late, aren’t I. I’m sorry. But… it should be alright now.”

“…Yes?”

The man gave Tang Sobo’s back a firm pat, then passed by her and moved forward.

“…Who?”

Questions filled Tang Sobo’s eyes. But her doubt was no greater than Jeok Ho’s.

“…”

Jeok Ho’s face turned cold.

The wrist that held his cherished curved blade ached sharply.

‘What is this?’

No matter how casually he had struck, for it to be so easily deflected; no, that it was deflected wasn’t the point. The important thing was that the moment his blade met that sword, he felt a heavy weight as if he’d struck a thousand-pound rock.

Jeok Ho’s face contorted abruptly.

“You are…”

The man blocking Jeok Ho looked around calmly. Many were already bleeding and down. Lives that could have been saved had he arrived a little sooner.

The man’s face hardened.

“Do you find killing so easy?”

“…Who are you, you brat?”

“Lee Song-Baek of the Southern Edge Sect.”

“What?”

Confusion crossed Jeok Ho’s face. Someone of this level of martial might, he should have at least heard the name before—but he’d never heard this one.

“If you intend to kill them, you’ll have to kill me first.”

The man’s feet dug into the ground. Lee Song-Baek stood firm like a thousand-year tree, his eyes shining with crystal clarity.

“Come.”

His heavy voice echoed widely across the earth frozen in silence.

Chapter 1435: You Will Have To Kill Me First. (5)

Jeok Ho’s eyebrow twitched slightly. The first words that caught his ear were the two syllables ‘Southern Edge.’

‘The Southern Edge Sect is here?’

Of course, he had considered that Southern Edge Sect might step forward. No matter how sealed, once the Four Sects Alliance launched a full-scale incursion into Shaanxi, they couldn’t just sit back and watch.

But the Four Sects Alliance had not yet set foot in Shaanxi. Even so, someone claiming to be Southern Edge Sect had deliberately appeared in this Sichuan land. That alone made his insides twist.

What truly unsettled Jeok Ho more than the name Southern Edge Sect was the man standing before him.

He had never even heard the man’s name. That meant he wasn’t renowned in the Central Plains. And judging by his appearance, he was still just a green youth.

Then he could have cut him down in one stroke, but strangely Jeok Ho’s sword intent wouldn’t move as easily as he expected.

Could he be cowed by this child’s momentum? Impossible. He was Jeok Ho, not someone to be pushed around by a kid.

Yet there was one reason he couldn’t easily extend his curved blade.

‘There is no opening.’

It felt as if he faced a massive wall.

Many people swing a blade at a rock, more often than you’d think. But if the condition is that you must split the rock with a single strike, no one would easily lift their sword.

Just like Jeok Ho now.

“Southern Edge Sect’s Lee Song-Baek…”

Jeok Ho bit his lip and glared coldly at Lee Song-Baek. Even standing before him, his eyes were unnervingly calm, showing not a tremor.

“Do you intend to stop us all by yourself?”

“…I never said I was alone.”

Jeok Ho’s eyes widened slightly. Lee Song-Baek met his gaze with an unflinching face.

“There are others who arrived a bit later.”

As if to prove the words, a group swiftly ran up behind Lee Song-Baek.

Wham!

A man in white martial robes sprang up from the ground and landed beside Lee Song-Baek, then glared at Jeok Ho. His intent was different from Lee Song-Baek’s, blade-like.

“I didn’t know you liked being so conspicuous.”

At the man’s words, Lee Song-Baek kept his eyes fixed on Jeok Ho and bowed slightly.

“Sorry, sahyung. I was in too much of a hurry.”

“Ha.”

The man snorted as if displeased, glanced at Jeok Ho, and spoke.

“Indeed, we are a bit late.”

Jeok Ho’s face twisted for a moment. The youth who had glanced at him casually turned his gaze to the rear as if nothing had happened.

‘Th…!’

A kid barely past thirty dared to turn his gaze away from Jeok Ho, something only a fool could do.

Yet Jeok Ho had to admit the arrogant manner oddly fit the youth.

Probably because of his flamboyant appearance.

Although he wore plain martial robes like the man who called himself Lee Song-Baek, this man’s aura was completely different.

If Lee Song-Baek was a steadfast ancient tree, this man was a gilded palace.

What was certain was that, despite their differing intents, both radiated an undeniable force.

‘Is this Southern Edge Sect?’

A chill ran down Jeok Ho’s spine.

If these youths, barely second-rank disciples, were so formidable, how strong must Southern Edge Sect’s elders and primary disciples be?

They said the center of the Nine Great Sects was Shaolin, Wudang, and Southern Edge Sect, and it seemed that wasn’t an empty boast.

Thud! Clang!

Soon dozens of swordsmen lined up around the two men.

“Great sahyung!”

“No need for long words. Take them down…”

“Sahyung. Do not forget the Sect leader’s admonition.”

At the weighty remark beside him, the flamboyant man stopped speaking, clicked his tongue, and said.

“Saving people comes first. Don’t rampage recklessly.”

“Yes!”

Jeok Ho’s eyes calmed of their own accord.

‘Damn.’

Remnants of the Tang Family had passed by Southern Edge Sect and moved away. But Jeok Ho couldn’t catch them; the moment he moved Southern Edge Sect’s sword would seize his ankle.

‘Why now of all times!’

Jeok Ho bit his lip involuntarily at the rising murderous intent, then opened his eyes flashing with killing intent.

‘It doesn’t matter who the opponent is. I only carry out orders.’

A surge of internal qi shot through Jeok Ho’s blade for a moment. A calm voice rang out.

“Stand down.”

Jeok Ho snapped his head to look at the speaker. Jang Ilso had already come close, beyond the struggling elders.

Stand down? He came all this way? For only these few?

Rebellion flared in Jeok Ho’s eyes for a moment. His hand on the blade tightened.

But that momentary fury froze the instant he heard the cold voice that followed.

“Jeok Ho.”

“…”

“Do you not hear me?”

Jeok Ho glanced at Lee Song-Baek and Jin Geum-Ryong, bit his lip, and stomped the ground. Standing beside Jang Ilso, he bowed slightly.

“I’m sorry.”

“No. I understand. My own gut churns from this too. But…”

Seeing Jin Geum-Ryong and Lee Song-Baek and Southern Edge Sect’s disciples arrayed around them, Jang Ilso’s eyes curved oddly.

“It can’t be helped. Desire is fine, but greed invites disaster. Behind a young tiger there is always its mother; she’ll likely arrive soon.”

Jeok Ho’s face hardened. The mother tiger, that would be Southern Edge Sect’s main force. What he’d just imagined, Southern Edge Sect’s main contingent, was now pressing toward this place.

“And those aren’t the only ones coming here. Truly dangerous ones are on the way.”

At that, Jeok Ho closed his eyes.

It was hardly wrong. They might handle Southern Edge Sect, but Mount Hua was likely coming this way now. Perhaps the Tang Family too.

The Ten Thousand People Clan was strong. But strength is relative. Facing the three sects of Mount Hua, Southern Edge Sect, and the Sichuan Tang Family would change the situation entirely. The Four Sects Alliance might have to stage a desperate retreat across the vast Sichuan.

“A shame, but greed is forbidden. Shall we withdraw then?”

“…Alliance lord, your words are most correct. But wouldn’t it be better to slay those rascals?”

Jeok Ho’s eyes turned to Lee Song-Baek and Jin Geum-Ryong. Jang Ilso, too, scanned the two with a strange look as if reluctantly amused.

“Not a bad thought, but you should’ve said it earlier. It’s already too late.”

“Eh?”

At that moment, several figures flew in behind Southern Edge Sect’s swordsmen.

“See? This is why I dislike old men.”

As everyone’s gaze fixed on Southern Edge Sect, a few Tang Family elders who’d joined from somewhere stood guarding his rear.

They had detached at the start of the battle to watch the situation, only to reveal themselves again upon Southern Edge Sect’s appearance.

Watching the Tang Family elders gasp for breath, Jeok Ho asked with a puzzled face.

“They’re only a few, after all.”

“Tsk tsk. This is why you always get trounced.”

“…What do you mean?”

“Arrogance is a shortcut to hell. Of course, a lone Tang Family standing on open ground is nothing; you can trample them as you like. But a Tang Family that has acquired a shield to block your path becomes like a marksmanship master on high ground: a terrible foe.”

“…”

“A marksman becomes greater by cunning. In that sense, those old men will no doubt be formidable, won’t they?”

At those words the elders’ faces flushed red.

“The shield may be small, but it’s not to be trifled with. This won’t go as I please.”

As Jang Ilso exaggeratedly sighed, Jin Geum-Ryong spoke.

“The Evil Sect sure likes to yap, doesn’t it?”

“…Hmm?”

“If you’re so confident, come on. I’ll cut your throat for you.”

Jang Ilso gave a deep, amused smile at the arrogant gaze.

“Kind words. Though it’s less impressive reciting them while you’re half-retreating.”

“What did you say?”

A murderous light rose in Jin Geum-Ryong’s face. Before he could snap, Lee Song-Baek quickly spoke.

“You! Are you unmoved after slaughtering so many people?”

Jang Ilso’s eyes met Lee Song-Baek’s.

“How could I be?”

“Then…”

“I’m pleased. Very much so.”

This time Lee Song-Baek’s momentum flared with killing intent as well.

Hegemony Lord Jang Ilso, the very man who stained the world with blood while Southern Edge Sect sealed it. He wanted to cut his throat on the spot, but…

‘He is strong.’

He knew for certain: the man was brutally strong. His whole body tingled. Honestly, none of them were confident they could cut him down even at the cost of their lives.

Jang Ilso leisurely looked at the rings on his hand and slowly twirled them.

“For mere rabbits, you’re quite cheeky.”

He shrugged his shoulders.

“But to correct the manners of a few presumptuous rabbits by becoming a fox caught in a trap would be absurd. Let’s go back before the fearsome tiger arrives.”

“Yes.”

Jeok Ho silently nodded. He still didn’t fully understand, but since Jang Ilso had made the call, there must be a solid reason.

Then Jang Ilso turned with exaggerated motion and mocked,

“And Jeok Ho, as you think, it’s not such a great victory.”

Jeok Ho turned back puzzled, then understood those words.

On the land emptied of rising poisonous mist, the scene differed from his expectations. The Ten Thousand People Clan’s elite who had charged boldly lay strewn, convulsing.

“Gurgle… gurgle…”

Red and black foam kept spilling from their mouths. Those convulsing so horribly that it made one’s hair stand up tore at themselves in agony, clawing their exposed flesh, which quickly blackened.

“Ugh…”

A groan escaped Jeok Ho. When did this happen…?

The fallen numbered as many as fifty. Normally that might not have mattered, but those who’d charged to fight them were the Ten Thousand People Clan’s handpicked elites.

Losing fifty elites while dealing with only about twenty elders meant this battle was by no means profitable.

Seeing Jeok Ho’s reaction, Jang Ilso burst into laughter.

“Did the Tang Family seem laughable to you? For an Evil Sect scum to think so.”

Jeok Ho clenched his face and thought. He’d faced poison countless times in Evil Sect fights and knew how to counter it. But now he had to admit: the poison they’d fought and the Sichuan Tang Family’s poison differed fundamentally.

“D-decontaminate…”

“Hmm?”

At Jeok Ho’s words, Jang Ilso’s gaze darkened slightly, as if scolding him for making such a judgment now.

“…Alliance lord.”

“Tsk tsk. So tender-hearted.”

Jang Ilso stepped forward slowly.

Beneath him lay Tang Family elders cut down and his own subordinates writhing convulsively from poisoning, the men he’d painstakingly raised. Dozens of them suffered convulsions from toxin.

“True tenderness isn’t that. This is Sichuan, and enemies will arrive soon. In such circumstances, tenderness…”

Jang Ilso looked down at his writhing subordinate. The man’s bloodshot eyes desperately followed him, and Jang Ilso smiled with a gentle, merciful expression.

“Yes, I’m here.”

Crack.

Jang Ilso’s foot snapped the convulsing man’s neck in an instant. The subordinate trembled once and then fell still.

Jang Ilso said in a calm voice.

“Now do you understand?”

At that moment the rings on his hands emitted beams of light.

Crack! Crack!

The flying rings pierced the necks of the convulsing men at once.

“Al-Alliance lord…”

“This is what mercy looks like.”

Jang Ilso cast a cold glance over his dying subordinates, then turned to watch Southern Edge Sect’s disciples who still stood sharply on guard.

‘The Southern Edge Sect.’

He’d thought it would take longer even if they’d moved at once upon hearing the news. Apparently that ponderous posterior had proved rather nimble.

“This is what makes the world interesting. There’s always a variable.”

Lee Song-Baek and Jin Geum-Ryong.

And the massive force approaching from afar. Having taken them all in, Jang Ilso turned his gaze without hesitation.

“Well then…”

Just as Jang Ilso was about to speak leisurely, he widened his eyes and sharply turned his head.

Jeok Ho and the Red Dogs were startled by the reaction. They knew Jang Ilso rarely reacted like that.

“Oh dear…”

Jang Ilso’s eyes twisted slightly.

Soon a vicious killing intent shot forth. Not only Jang Ilso but everyone turned to look in that direction. The place the murderous aura swept from…

Something revealed itself at the edge of the desolate ground.

It was so far it appeared only as a dot, but Jang Ilso knew exactly what it was.

“Yes, I knew it. We might have died here if we hadn’t.”

Jang Ilso laughed and spun around. Then a dreadful roar thundered from the distance.

“Jang Ilsoooooooo!”

The roar was so fierce it froze everyone, but Jang Ilso alone shrugged it off with a joke.

“Alright, withdraw. Before that tiger’s fangs sink into my throat.”

The sword aiming at his neck.

Seeing the sword called Chung Myung, Jang Ilso tilted one corner of his mouth upward.
